new GOLD-SLAM: Gaussian-splatting Omnidirectional LiDAR-Depth SLAM

Authors: Weicong Zhang, Yuan Huang, Yu Zheng, Lin Chenxiao, Yingying Li, Lilong Fan, Liangdong Zhuang, Yufan Chen, Ruiping Liu, Junwei Zheng, Jiaming Zhang

Abstract: Panoramic cameras capture nearly the entire spherical field of view in a single frame. However, the mismatch between this panoramic coverage and the field of view of LiDAR poses a substantial challenge for panoramic Gaussian splatting SLAM that relies on LiDAR depth priors. We introduce GOLD-SLAM, a Gaussian-splatting Omnidirectional LiDAR-Depth SLAM system that combines high-quality rendering with accurate geometric reconstruction. It performs tracking and mapping in the spherical domain and uses tracked visual features in regions without LiDAR coverage to improve pose estimation. To address insufficient coverage of newly observed surfaces and floating artifacts during incremental mapping, we introduce Depth-Aware Map Extension and Pruning (DAEP). To enable broader evaluation, we collect a new dataset, WHOLE, using a UAV platform across Weak-geometry, High-dynamic, Omnidirectional, Low-light, and Easy scenarios. Extensive experiments on public benchmarks and WHOLE demonstrate competitive rendering quality and improved geometric accuracy relative to perspective systems. Compared with existing panoramic baselines, GOLD-SLAM improves mean PSNR by 5.56-9.10 dB and reduces mean Depth-L1 by 71.9-83.9%. To assess deployment feasibility on resource-constrained platforms such as UAVs, we evaluate an embedded configuration of GOLD-SLAM on a Jetson Orin NX (16 GB), demonstrating online operation at the cost of reconstruction quality.

new Get the Next Moment Right: Short-Term Event Planning for Physically Plausible Video Generation

Authors: Hao Liu, Xiang Tang, Ruotong Li, Xiaopeng Fan, Yujiu Yang

Abstract: Video generation models synthesize realistic imagery yet still struggle to produce physically plausible evolution. Existing methods guide generation with a motion trajectory predicted for the entire clip in advance, so prediction errors accumulate and degrade later frames. We propose STEP(Short-Term Event Planning), a training-free closed-loop framework that guides generation one short clip at a time. Each round consists of four steps: grounding measures the scene state from the latest frames, event planning predicts the next physical event and renders it into a control clip, latent-guided generation produces the next short clip under this control, and verification checks whether the event occurs and resamples the clip if it does not. Because each round is grounded in the frames actually generated, control relies only on reliable short-range predictions. STEP improves the physical plausibility of three frozen generators on Physics-IQ Verified and PhyGenBench, and can be applied to new generators without training.

new Part-Set Prototypes for Explainable Mountain Gazelles Sex Classification

Authors: Tali Boneh Shitrit, Amir Kedem, Ilan Shimshoni, Anna Zamansky

Abstract: Self-explainable visual models for fine-grained recognition are often hindered by rigid geometric constraints that cannot capture diverse semantic shapes in unconstrained "in-the-wild" imagery. Real-world camera-trap data introduces challenges, including non-optimal poses, lighting variations, occlusions, animal camouflage, and environmental shortcuts. To address this, we introduce set prototypes, a general architecture where both part cardinality and unconstrained spatial layouts are mined directly from data. Our joint-matching formulation places parts simultaneously on a frozen DINOv3 token grid via truncated Sinkhorn competition, allowing unsupported parts to abstain without spatial collapse. We evaluate our approach on Mountain Gazelle sex classification, a camera-trap benchmark, where our model achieves 0.878 macro-F1--outperforming prior architectures and at par with an unconstrained linear probe. The strongest prototypes localize to distinctive biological areas such as horns, neck and body, allowing ecologists to visually verify decisions before trusting the metric. This formulation generalizes naturally to diverse ecological monitoring challenges beyond a single species.

new DiCT: Teaching X-ray Vision-Language Models to Reason via Imagining CT

Authors: Zhenghao Lu, Xiaotian Zhang, Songtao Jiang, Yiqun Lin, Siming Fu, Hualiang Wang, Zuozhu Liu

Abstract: Diagnosing with X-rays often requires reasoning about the 3D locations of abnormalities and their spatial relationships to surrounding anatomical structures, yet such reasoning remains challenging due to the loss of spatial information in 2D projections. To address this challenge, we propose Dreaming in CT (DiCT), a three-stage progressive CT-to-X-ray reasoning transfer framework that leverages a CT vision-language model (VLM) to guide an X-ray VLM. Through this CT-guided transfer, the student acquires CT-informed 3D reasoning capabilities, enabling it to reason directly from 2D X-rays without explicit CT reconstruction at inference time. The framework begins with instruct the dreamer, which equips the X-ray student with initial CT-oriented reasoning capabilities and establishes a structured interface that distinguishes X-ray observations from CT imagination. Building on this structure, visualize the dream introduces CT-guided on-policy distillation (OPD), where the CT teacher selectively guides the X-ray student's reasoning trajectories to transfer 3D anatomical knowledge. Finally, manifest the dream adapts the acquired reasoning capabilities from digitally reconstructed radiographs (DRRs) to real clinical X-rays. Experiments across two VLM backbones on 3D-RAD and ReXVQA demonstrate DiCT's effectiveness across radiological reasoning tasks, achieving a 1.66 percentage point improvement over a CT reconstruction-based baseline on 3D-RAD Abnormality Identification with 12.77$\times$ lower inference computation. Our code is available at https://github.com/HongYep/DiCT.

new LRQ-Cache: Compressing Feature Caches in Video Diffusion via Low-Rank Approximation and Quantization

Authors: Ruizhe Yao, Zhen Huang, Songpan Gao, Danyi Liu, Zijian Cao, Weijie Zheng, Haohuan Fu

Abstract: Video diffusion transformers (DiTs) achieve high-fidelity generation, but inference is expensive because every denoising step evaluates a large transformer over all spatiotemporal tokens of the video. Temporal caching accelerates them by reusing intermediate features across denoising steps. However, retaining dense feature tensors introduces a persistent storage cost that grows with video length, resolution, and cache coverage, reaching 109-346 GiB per video for the predictive caches we evaluate. Such states exceed the memory of a single GPU, so native caches must either be offloaded to the CPU, which slows generation, or be sharded across several GPUs, e.g., with sequence parallelism, so that the cache rather than the compute sets the number of GPUs. We instead shrink the cached state itself, exploiting its redundancy: a few channel patterns shared across tokens capture much of the energy of each cached feature. Based on this observation, we propose LRQ-Cache, a training-free method that combines low-rank approximation and quantization for feature-cache compression. LRQ-Cache represents each cached feature with a small set of factors, quantizes them before storage, and reconstructs the feature at reuse time, leaving cache mechanisms unchanged. Across three caching mechanisms on HunyuanVideo and Wan2.1, LRQ-Cache reduces the persistent state of predictive caches by 42-71x, to 2.3-4.9 GiB per video. VBench stays within 0.25 pp of the native cache for TeaCache, and the larger drops of the Wan2.1 predictive caches, up to 3.5 pp, shrink at higher ranks. As a result, predictive caches that exceed the memory of a single 80 GB H100 GPU fit on it with LRQ-Cache, making generation 2.7-5.9x faster than with CPU-offloaded native caches.

new LookAnything: A Foundation Model for Holistic Gaze Following

Authors: Jiayu Tang, Yuchen Zhou, Hongsheng Wu, Hanjing Ye, Ke Huang, Cheng Wang, Yuqin Dai, Yufei Huang, Yonggen Ling, Chao Gou

Abstract: Human gaze provides a rich window into attention and intent, making its understanding essential for applications ranging from social signal analysis to human-robot interaction. Existing methods typically employ highly customized architectures relying on auxiliary inputs like head crops or depth maps, causing cascading errors and inference overhead. Trained on single domains and confined to isolated gaze following with heatmap outputs, they lack flexibility and generalization for diverse interactive scenarios. To bridge this gap, we present LookAnything, a foundation model that unifies diverse gaze-centric tasks for holistic gaze following. Benefiting from large-scale multi-task and cross-domain training, LookAnything flexibly adapts to various settings, generating diverse outputs (points, boxes, heatmaps, and language) and supporting broad gaze-centric localization, description, and reasoning. At its core, we introduce learnable gaze-prior queries to promote gaze-aware representation learning, eliminating the need for auxiliary external inputs. To facilitate this paradigm, we construct HoliGaze, a comprehensive corpus and benchmark comprising 311K question-answer pairs across 5 capability dimensions and 9 distinct tasks. Experiments demonstrate that LookAnything consistently outperforms 33 leading vision-language models on holistic gaze following, while remaining highly competitive with dedicated specialists across 5 conventional target detection benchmarks. Evaluation on unseen datasets and deployment on a real robot further validate its strong out-of-domain generalization and flexible adaptation in real-world scenarios.

new Sub-Riemannian structure for Oriented Point Clouds

Authors: Thomas Besnier, Stefan Sommer

Abstract: Many frameworks dealing with 3D (oriented) point clouds define locality in the ambient Euclidean space: $k$-nearest-neighbor graphs and radius balls in $\mathbb{R}^3$ are critical to define which points are "close to one another" and should exchange information. This can unintentionally merge sampled surfaces that are close in space but geometrically distinct such as the two faces of a thin leaf. Then, features are more prone to leak across boundaries which hinder performance. To address this, we propose to define a sub-Riemannian contact metric on position-orientation space $\mathbb{M}=\mathbb{R}^3\times \mathbb{S}^2$: points are lifted to $(x,n)$ with $n$ a surface normal, and "motion" is constrained to a non-holonomic horizontal distribution in which spatial velocity must stay in the plane orthogonal to the normal which can reorient at a cost. From this, we derive an explicit score, which locally approximate the distance, to compare oriented points for downstream tasks. We report improved segmentation and correspondence accuracy near contact when using the score as a drop-in change inside PointNet++ aggregation mechanism as well as reduced leakage when using it to simulate heat diffusion on oriented points.

new Visual-State Graph Memory for Streaming Video Understanding

Authors: Ailar Mahdizadeh, Pritam Sarkar, Xiangteng He, Leonid Sigal

Abstract: In streaming video understanding, the evidence needed to answer a question may have already left the recent-frame window. Keeping all frames does not scale with video length, while dropping older frames limits the model's ability to reason about the past. We propose Visual-State Graph (VSG), a training-free memory that stores the state of a stream rather than the raw sequence of frames. VSG represents the stream as a graph of objects and their relations, together with a log of events; similar to a dynamically evolving scene graph. An object observed repeatedly is represented by one persistent node, with a timestamped history of how its state changes. To determine whether a newly mentioned object already exists in the graph, we compare the relations it takes part in with those of stored objects. We build this memory as the video arrives, before the questions are known. When a question arrives, we retrieve relevant objects and events alongside recent frames under a fixed input budget. Our evaluation spans four streaming-video benchmarks and seven open-source vision-language models ranging from 7B to 32B parameters. Under matched input budgets, VSG improves reasoning about the past while preserving real-time understanding.

new Improving Character Identity Consistency in Diffusion-Based Visual Story Generation through Identity Memory and Automatic Reranking

Authors: Maria-Emilia Grigore, Stelian Sp\^inu

Abstract: Automatic visual story generation using diffusion-based models has become an increasingly important research direction in generative artificial intelligence. However, maintaining character identity consistency across consecutive frames remains an open challenge, as existing models often produce significant variations in facial traits, clothing, visual style, or anatomical proportions. This paper proposes a modular pipeline to improve character identity consistency in multi-frame visual story generation using Stable Diffusion XL alongside additional conditioning and automatic selection mechanisms. The proposed system integrates LoRA fine-tuning for character personalization, ControlNet for pose-based structural control, and IP-Adapter for visual identity transfer. Furthermore, a reranking mechanism based on Best-of-K candidate selection and persistent identity memory is introduced to automatically select the most visually consistent generated images. The work also includes integrating and extending the StoryDiffusion framework into a reproducible local workflow, along with improvements for generated frame selection. Performance evaluation uses automatic metrics such as CLIP image similarity, DINO similarity, and CLIP text-image alignment. Experimental evaluation was conducted on a custom benchmark of multiple characters and visual stories organized into difficulty levels (easy, medium, and difficult). Both progressive variants of the proposed pipeline and extended versions of StoryDiffusion were evaluated, including SDXL baseline, Best-of-K, Identity Memory, and the Full Pipeline. Results show that reranking, identity memory, and visual conditioning reduce identity drift across frames and improve the overall coherence and stability of generated visual stories.

new Adaptive and Detection-Aware Super-Resolution for Small Object Detection

Authors: Cezica-Elena Luncanu, Stelian Sp\^inu

Abstract: Image super-resolution aims to reconstruct a high-resolution image from a low-resolution input and is relevant for surveillance, satellite imagery, medical imaging, and object detection. This paper investigates SRCNN-based single-image super-resolution for a scale factor of x4, starting from a classical baseline and progressively introducing residual learning, Charbonnier loss, and adaptive loss functions. The proposed approach includes a three-region adaptive loss, where image areas are divided into smooth, texture, and edge regions using gradient information. The models are evaluated using PSNR, SSIM, LPIPS, and EdgeMAE in order to measure both reconstruction quality and structural detail preservation. In addition to reconstruction evaluation, the paper analyzes the impact of super-resolution on object detection. A YOLOv8 detector is applied to images processed through bicubic interpolation and SRCNN-based variants, using images from the COCO validation dataset. The results show that improved reconstruction quality does not always lead to better detection performance. However, for small objects, a detection-aware variant combined with residual scaling improves the F1 score compared to the bicubic baseline. These results highlight the importance of task-aware evaluation when super-resolution is used in computer vision pipelines.

new PRC4D: Physical Relation-Constrained Persistent 4D Reconstruction

Authors: Junho Kim, Hyunyoung Choi, Jiseok Kim, Seongwon Lee

Abstract: A faithful 4D reconstruction should capture both the shape and motion of objects, even when they disappear from view. Existing methods preserve previously observed geometry and extend motion through occlusion using local spatial and motion associations. However, treating short-term contact or shared motion as sufficient evidence of persistent rigidity can introduce incorrect motion constraints and distort overall reconstruction. To address this limitation, we present PRC4D, a physical relation-constrained framework that identifies reliable rigid relations and uses them to maintain spatial consistency through occlusion. PRC4D combines 3D contact and relative motion consistency with sequence-level verification to distinguish persistent couplings from transient interactions. The resulting graph guides observed-pose refinement and motion propagation from visible to occluded components without altering reconstructed geometry. Experiments on diverse real-world multi-object sequences and controlled occlusion simulations demonstrate improved reconstruction accuracy, reduced relative pose errors, and more consistent object configurations under changing visibility.

new Verify then Steer: Progress Hallucination-Aware Recovery for Continuous Vision-and-Language Navigation

Authors: Jiewen Hou, Guanxiang Qi, Meina Kan, Shiguang Shan, Xilin Chen

Abstract: Vision-and-Language Navigation (VLN) requires an agent to follow language instructions to reach a target in an unseen 3D environment. However, existing methods still struggle to recover from route deviations at test time. We investigate this limitation through the lens of instruction progress, and trace it to a phenomenon we term progress hallucination: agents misinterpret off-route observations as evidence of task progress, causing their internal instruction progress to advance beyond the actual task stage and hindering recovery based on earlier sub-instructions. To address this failure mode, we propose PHAR (Progress Hallucination-Aware Recovery), a test-time recovery framework following a verify-then-steer paradigm. The verifier examines residual-stream representations of the Vision-Language Model (VLM) to identify mismatch of cross-modal evidence along the trajectories, and uses split conformal calibration to control false alarms. When an alarm is raised, the steering module uses a contextual bandit to adaptively select the rewind extent of instruction progress for online recovery. Through extensive experiments on R2R-CE and RxR-CE, our framework enhances the reliability of multiple state-of-the-art vision-language navigation methods at test time.

new BEACON: Learning Observation-Conditioned Starting Points for Guided Conditional Flow Matching in Microscopy

Authors: Anirban Ray, Florian Jug

Abstract: Fluorescence microscopy drives much of modern life science, but accessible imaging setups often yield images degraded by out-of-focus light, limited resolution, and strong signal-dependent noise. Computational restoration can recover high-quality images from such data, enabling cheaper and faster imaging at lower light dose, provided biologically meaningful structures are preserved. Guided conditional flow matching has recently shown strong results for microscopy dehazing and computational super-resolution by learning distributions of plausible restorations. However, it pairs each clean target with an independent standard Gaussian sample, so the starting point of every training path does not leverage the degraded observation. This arbitrary noise--target coupling can produce conflicting velocity targets and makes the conditional velocity field harder to learn. We present BEACON, a $BE$st-of-$K$ $A$daptive learned coupling for guided $CON$ditional flow matching, which learns an observation-conditioned base distribution. During training, a lightweight conditional noise model proposes $K$ starting points for each degraded input, and the candidate with the lowest flow-matching loss supervises both networks, while a KL term keeps the proposal close to the Gaussian prior. At inference, a single draw from the learned proposal is transported along the velocity field, requiring neither candidate selection nor the target. We evaluate BEACON on ten datasets spanning microscopy dehazing and computational super-resolution against 13 baseline families. BEACON achieves the best median perception--distortion rank for every pairing of a fidelity metric (PSNR, MicroMS-SSIM) with a perceptual metric (LPIPS, FID). Code and pretrained models will be released under a permissive license.

new Every Edit Its Own Budget: Per-Instruction Token Freezing for Training-Free Acceleration of Multi-Edit Image Editing

Authors: Jyotirmoy Nath, Neeraj Kumar, Brejesh Lall

Abstract: Accelerating instruction-based image editing when one instruction requests several edits is challenging. Many training-free accelerators for flow-matching image editors skip tokens whose prediction has stopped changing, but decide this with a single threshold for the whole image. With several edits, such a threshold, tuned for speed, freezes small and slowly settling edits first, and requested edits disappear. We propose TIDE (Training-free Instruction-aware Drift-based Editing), which gives every instruction part its own compute budget. First, TIDE finds the region of each part in the editor's own attention by unbalanced optimal transport, without masks or external models. Second, it freezes a token once its drift is small relative to its own part and moves it along the straight path to its endpoint estimate. Third, a sparse engine computes only the remaining tokens. We prove that part-agnostic rules cannot guarantee per-part error budgets, characterize the optimal per-part threshold policy up to one tie class per part, and derive when our rule attains it. On FLUX.2 Klein, one global threshold breaks at least one edit's budget in 89% of MICE-Bench images, and TIDE drops up to 77% fewer edits at matched or higher speed (48% on MICE-Bench), a gap human raters confirm. On the many-step Klein Base, TIDE is the fastest compared method on six benchmarks (4.81-4.89x) and needs 5.3x fewer TFLOPs than vanilla, the largest saving we measure. With the same settings, it is also the fastest compared method on Qwen-Image-Edit-2511, a second model family (3.46-3.52x), and drops the fewest edits on all six benchmarks. Code will be made publicly available.

new TESS: Query-Guided Temporal Event-Structured Frame Selection for Long-Video Understanding

Authors: Qi Lu, Shiyan Tong, Shujie Li, Sen Li, Likun shi, Maofei Que, Xiaolong Jiang, Yao Hu

Abstract: Temporal localization in long videos is challenging because brief, question-relevant events are dispersed among extensive redundant content. Limited visual budgets increase the risk of omitting these events or their temporal context, undermining reliable reasoning. Existing frame selection methods emphasize semantic relevance or temporal coverage, often overlooking the complementary evidence needed to locate related events and establish their temporal relationships. To address this limitation, we propose TESS, a training-free framework for query-guided temporal event-structured frame selection. TESS selects frames that jointly show the reference event and the event asked about, in the required temporal order. Leveraging frozen vision-language representations, TESS integrates soft temporal gating, role-aware budget allocation, and within-role redundancy suppression to preserve complementary event evidence. Extensive experiments across four long-video understanding benchmarks and three multimodal backbones demonstrate that TESS consistently outperforms the strongest compared baseline by 4.0-5.5 percentage points under a fixed frame budget. Further analyses reveal greater improvements on longer videos and robust performance under tighter visual budgets, while independent evidence annotations confirm enhanced event and temporal-relation coverage. These findings establish explicit temporal evidence organization as an effective training-free strategy for preserving event relationships and supporting efficient temporal reasoning over long videos. Our code is available at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/TESS_code.

URLs: https://anonymous.4open.science/r/TESS_code.

new Factorized Context Attention: From Emulated to Native Grid Probing

Authors: Mohamed Eltahir, Manar Eyad, Yara Alzahrani, Fatimah Albesher, Duaa M. Tahir, Tanveer Hussain, Naeemullah Khan

Abstract: Models read a book, an hour of video, or an image in one forward pass. As the context grows, this pass slows down, loses accuracy, and stops at the context window. Sparse attention is efficient, but keeps the other two limitations. Grid probing, a recent training-free method, recovers the accuracy and extends the reach by asking a frozen model the same question about short local and strided spans of the input, but every question repeats the whole computation, since each span is encoded again. Our insight is that grid probing is emulated attention, an attention pattern reproduced from outside the model through prompts, since each probe only restricts which part of the input the question can see. We introduce Factorized Context Attention (FCA), a training-free block-sparse attention that runs the same pattern natively, as the attention of the model itself. FCA encodes the input once into a KV cache, reads all spans as masked sequences in one batched forward, and uses a stair position schedule to keep every span inside the trained position range. Across long video understanding, long-context text inference and image grounding, FCA matches the accuracy and the reach of grid probing at a fraction of its cost. It gains about 10 points over the single pass on long video and 9 to 14 points on long novels, finds needles in videos of over a thousand frames and in texts four times the length of the window, and answers a later question about the same input in as little as a tenth of the time of grid probing. Native attention thus keeps what emulated attention gains without its cost, all with existing frozen language and vision-language models.

new One Subspace Is Not Enough: LocalSubspaceAD for Training-Free Few-Shot Anomaly Detection

Authors: Daniel Pr\"oll, Sebastian Hegenbart

Abstract: Defect-free industrial samples can vary considerably in appearance, texture, and geometry. In few-shot anomaly detection, this variability has to be modeled from only a handful of images. SubspaceAD showed that a single principal subspace of foundation-model patch features is a strong training-free model of nominal appearance. However, a single subspace has to cover all of the nominal data's variability, so a deviation that is anomalous for one semantic region can be within the nominal variability of another. We propose LocalSubspaceAD, which extends SubspaceAD from a single subspace to a mixture of subspaces, assigning each group of patches with similar features its own subspace. The set of patch-features is partitioned using k-means, one subspace is fit per cluster, and each patch is scored by the minimum of re-projection residuals over all subspaces. The proposed method requires no training and no EM refinement. On MVTec AD, VisA and Real-IAD with 1, 2 and 4 shots, it improves over a single subspace (SubspaceAD) on both evaluated backbones: by 1.2 to 1.8 points image AUROC on DINOv2, and by 1.4 to 4.4 points in image AUROC, pixel AUPR and AUPRO on DINOv3. The gains are most pronounced on objects with heterogeneous nominal appearance, such as multi-instance categories and PCBs. Ablations show that the gains stem from a finer-grained model of variability through locally oriented subspaces, rather than from the subspace offsets (cluster means). We further show that the gains do not stem from added capacity alone: the local subspaces appear to capture the structure of coherent clusters in feature space, and the model does not merely act as a compressed memory bank.

new NRG-Twins: Pixel-Level Ground Truth for Rigidity Violations in Generated Videos

Authors: Chenrui Fan, Qiyang Hu, Luca Scharr, Paolo Favaro

Abstract: Video generators now produce realistic clips of scenes and objects, which makes generated video an attractive source of 3D content. Yet generated video is not 3D consistent: static content slowly deforms or slides, 3D reconstruction degrades, and the errors are too subtle to label by hand. To make generators 3D consistent, or to build rewards and guidance that push them there, we need data that say where and by how much a generated video breaks rigidity, and these data must come from generated video itself. We obtain per-pixel ground-truth labels from real-generated twins: a real clip paired with its re-creation by a keyframe-conditioned video generator. After inserting real keyframes so that the twin camera roughly follows the real one, we measure per pixel, in degrees, how far the twin departs from a rigid scene seen along its own camera path. We release Non-Rigidly Generated Twins (NRG-Twins), 871 twins, the code that builds them for new generators, and a benchmark with an official test split. A detector trained on these data, the Non-Rigidly Generated Pixel Detector (NRG-PD), predicts the per-pixel angle from RGB alone. It separates inconsistent from consistent pixels with AUROC 0.85, against 0.40-0.74 for seven existing consistency and motion-segmentation methods, and its predictions improve 3DGS of video that contains generated content.

new Video Models are Excellent Motion Models

Authors: Kian Izadpanah, Ali Mahdavi Amiri, Daniel Cohen-Or, Yiorgos Chrysanthou, Andreas Aristidou

Abstract: Video foundation models have emerged as powerful generators of dynamic visual content, exhibiting remarkable capabilities in synthesizing complex actions and interactions. We argue that these models have also become excellent motion generators, with motion priors that are richer and more general than those learned by dedicated motion models. Their advantage extends beyond visual realism and is manifested in two important aspects: motion naturalness, including coherent body coordination, physical plausibility, and realistic interactions with objects and the environment; and motion scope, encompassing a much broader range of activities and semantic coherence, particularly uncommon, complex, and compound actions. To investigate this position, we introduce a common evaluation framework that compares video and dedicated motion models through three complementary settings: text-only motion generation, reconstruction of video-generated motion from sparse keyframes, and video-based enrichment of motion-model outputs. By converting all outputs into a shared 3D skeletal representation, we evaluate motion naturalness, semantic alignment, action coverage, physical motion characteristics, and fidelity to motion constraints. Our results suggest that video foundation models can generate more natural and semantically meaningful movements, particularly for complex and compound actions. Moreover, we investigate motion-conditioned generation through loose skeletal control, finding that dedicated motion models do not consistently improve video-generated motion, while video models can enrich skeletal animations using sparse motion guidance. Our findings suggest a shift in the role of specialized motion models, from generating complete animations to guiding and controlling the richer motion capabilities already embedded in video foundation models.

new PVWander: 3D Memory-guided Panoramic Video Wandering with Path-Facing Canonicalization

Authors: Chuanxin Song, Xiaodong Wang, Shijie Chen, Junliang Xie, Zhanmi Zhong, Suiying Wu, Peixi Peng

Abstract: Long-horizon panoramic video wandering aims to continuously synthesize scene-consistent $360^\circ$ observations along user-specified camera trajectories from an initial equirectangular projection (ERP) panorama. Existing panoramic video models have achieved promising results in camera-controlled generation, while recent 3D-guided approaches further provide geometric guidance for improving scene consistency. However, stable long-horizon wandering remains challenging due to diverse motion--orientation relationships and imperfect memory guidance induced by accumulated generation artifacts and depth inaccuracies. To address these challenges, we propose \textbf{PVWander}, a 3D-memory-guided framework that accumulates generated observations into an online point-cloud memory and reprojects it to guide subsequent generation, with two complementary designs: \textit{Path-Facing canonicalization} for motion--orientation alignment and \textit{Timestep-aware gating modulation} for adaptive memory guidance. \textit{Path-Facing canonicalization} exploits the horizontal cyclic symmetry of ERP panoramas to uniformly align each clip's ERP orientation with its principal translation direction, yielding more consistent, forward-like relative motions while preserving the prescribed camera path. \textit{Timestep-aware gating modulation} exploits the coarse-to-fine nature of diffusion denoising to adaptively regulate reprojected memory guidance through timestep-dependent channel-wise gates, preserving its structural benefits while mitigating visual artifacts from imperfect projections. Extensive experiments demonstrate that PVWander achieves improved long-horizon generation quality, while ablations validate the effectiveness of both designs.

new A Mesh to Remember: Persistent Appearance for Interactive Video Worlds

Authors: Joonghyuk Shin, Alex Trevithick, Katar\'ina T\'othov\'a, Liyuan Zhu, Shengyu Huang, Jaesik Park, Shalini De Mello, Laura Leal-Taix\'e, Riccardo de Lutio, Haithem Turki

Abstract: Video world models synthesize photorealistic observations in response to user actions but struggle to remain consistent over long interactions. Coding agents and text-to-3D models instead generate explicit 3D worlds whose geometry and state persist by construction, but whose appearance falls far short of photorealism. Recent methods combine the strengths of both by rendering such worlds into coarse proxies that guide a video model. However, since these proxies only specify where things are, the renderer must invent what they look like as the world is explored, and anything it forgets is reinvented when a surface reappears. Our key insight is that a mesh identifies which surface each pixel shows, across frames and views, even as objects move, allowing it to serve as an address for what the renderer generates. We propose M2R (Mesh to Remember), a few-step causal video renderer that stores its own features on the surfaces they depict, in each object's frame, and retrieves them by rasterizing the mesh whenever those surfaces come back into view. Appearance thus travels with objects wherever they move, persists across revisits, and is shared by every agent that observes it, while memory grows with the surfaces seen instead of the number of frames. Trained only on synthetic data, M2R transfers zero-shot to real indoor scans and to worlds authored by coding agents. It outperforms state-of-the-art methods in consistency across static, dynamic, and multi-agent benchmarks, more than doubling object permanence and nearly doubling cross-agent consistency, while rendering 720p video in real time (40 FPS).

new ObsBridge: Observation-Grounded Conditioning for Ego Trajectory Prediction

Authors: Zhenhao Wang, Shaofeng Liang, Jiemin Wu, Sijia Chen, Keni Shi, Nan Li, Wenshuo Chen, Bowen Tian, Runwei Guan, Yutao Yue

Abstract: Pedestrian forecasting from ego-view observations is challenging because occlusion, limited visibility, and tracking errors corrupt observed motion histories. Such errors are particularly consequential for autonomous robot navigation. Existing paradigms typically either impute noisy history positions before forecasting or jointly train an auxiliary history reconstruction branch. However, predictors trained on clean histories may be sensitive to residual errors in recovered histories at deployment. Meanwhile, auxiliary reconstruction still computes inter-agent interactions over raw, corrupted coordinates. We investigate conditioning future prediction on both recovered historical states and raw observation evidence, with progressive training to accommodate imperfect recovery. Building on this insight, we propose ObsBridge, a flow-based trajectory forecasting framework with observation-grounded historical bridging. Specifically, ObsBridge first employs a frozen estimator to recover historical motion from agent-relative and ego-relative positions, image-plane locations, and visibility states. During training, we introduce an agent-wise curriculum that gradually replaces clean teacher histories with complete estimated histories before context encoding. This progressively exposes the predictor to estimated histories during training. Furthermore, we design a residual geometry adapter to fuse the encoded motion with original observation evidence. We aggregate the resulting representations through social attention to condition the flow-based trajectory generator. Extensive experiments across multiple benchmarks demonstrate that ObsBridge outperforms state-of-the-art methods. On EgoTraj-TBD, it reduces minADE@20 by 6.5\% and minFDE@20 by 8.7\%. The code will be provided at https://github.com/shawnliang420/ObsBridge.

URLs: https://github.com/shawnliang420/ObsBridge.

new Which Parameters to Update? An Instance-Specific Adaptation Framework for Real-World Pansharpening

Authors: Jin-Liang Xiao, Liang-Jian Deng, Yu-Xuan Zhang, Hua Meng, Zhengchun Zhou

Abstract: Pansharpening aims to fuse a single observed image pair, comprising a low-resolution multispectral (LRMS) image and a panchromatic (PAN) image, to obtain a high-resolution multispectral (HRMS) image. Recently, deep learning-based methods have achieved significant performance due to their strong capability of extracting nonlinear features. However, distribution shifts between training data and test images can degrade the performance of pretrained pansharpening networks, limiting their generalization in real-world scenarios. In this work, we propose an instance-specific test-time adaptation framework that uses the observed PAN-LRMS pair to select and update only a subset of the pretrained network's parameters. Specifically, the framework uses cross-scoring to identify parameter groups whose updates improve reconstruction across image patches. It then updates the selected groups by minimizing a loss that combines a spectral consistency term with a newly designed spatial consistency term. Experiments show that the adapted networks perform better than their pretrained counterparts and outperform representative state-of-the-art pansharpening methods on GaoFen-2, QuickBird, and WorldView series datasets.

new ViDM: Efficient and Order-Robust Parallel Visual Decision Making

Authors: Xudong Tan, Peng Ye, Chenyu Huang, Yaoxin Yang, Huafeng Qin, Tao Chen

Abstract: Visual decision-making demands accurate judgments and timely responses. Yet for multiple questions sharing the same visual input, autoregressive multimodal large language models generate answers token by token, accumulating sequential decoding costs as question count grows and delaying completion of the decision set. Their predictions can also vary with question order. We introduce ViDM, which transforms multi-question visual inference into parallel decisions in a single forward pass, improving both accuracy and inference efficiency. ViDM organizes shared visual context, multiple questions, and question-specific decision slots into one sequence, reusing the native language-model head to produce all answer distributions simultaneously. A Decision-Query Mask aligns each decision query's direct attention with shared context and its corresponding question, while case-balanced multi-order training strengthens robustness to question reordering. Across nine benchmarks spanning image, multi-image, and video tasks, ViDM improves Qwen3.5-4B's average accuracy from 73.49% to 77.55% while achieving 8.4x the baseline's average end-to-end throughput. On GQA, where each evaluated image has 11.4 questions on average, the end-to-end speedup reaches 20.3x. ViDM also improves mean and worst-order accuracy while reducing order-induced variation across multiple benchmarks, with further validation on LLaVA-OneVision-7B and InternVL3.5-4B.

new MAGPIE: Agentic Multi-Reference Image Generation via Executable Scene Construction

Authors: Yifan Dai, Daili Hua, Bohan Zeng, Yuran Wang, Jialing Liu, Bozhou Li, Chengzhuo Tong, Yang Shi, Kai Zeng, Liu Yang, Xinyu Liu, Shujie Li, Yiyan Ji, Bingyin Mei, Yushuo Guan, Yuanxing Zhang, Fangcheng Fu, Wentao Zhang

Abstract: Multi-reference image generation requires a model to preserve the appearance of selected subjects while arranging them according to an instruction. Despite recent progress in reference-based generation and compositional control, reliably organizing selected subjects into the intended scene remains challenging. Although existing methods provide various forms of compositional guidance, the planned relationships are not always explicitly instantiated for inspection and refinement before image synthesis. To address this limitation, we introduce MAGPIE, which represents the intended composition as a simplified 3D scene defined by an executable Blender program. A coding agent links each scene instance to its reference, checks the geometry and rendered view against the instruction, and revises the program to correct discrepancies. The final proxy rendering serves as a visual plan for the image generator. Using MAGPIE-Instruct-3K, we train the generator to follow this plan while drawing each subject's appearance from its reference. MAGPIE improves both Qwen and FLUX on DreamOmni2 and SconeEval. With FLUX, it improves overall success on DreamOmni2 by 28.21 percentage points over the original backbone and achieves the highest overall score among the models evaluated on SconeEval.

new Describe, Don't Ask: Caption-Driven On-Policy Distillation for Fine-Grained Visual Perception

Authors: Haozhan Shen, Liangbo He, Jun Lan, Huijia Zhu, Weiqiang Wang, Tiancheng Zhao, Jianwei Yin

Abstract: Vision-language models (VLMs) often recognize fine-grained details more accurately in a relevant crop than in the full image. Multimodal on-policy distillation (OPD) methods exploit this by transferring the advantage of a crop-view teacher to a full-image student. However, their supervision relies on region-specific questions curated through an elaborate synthesis pipeline, which brings three weaknesses: (1) every region needs a question--answer pair written exclusively for it, locking training into costly QA curation that is hard to scale to unlabeled images; (2) all rollouts of a region answer the same question, so the teacher supervises only the details it asks about, leaving other visual elements in the region unsupervised; and (3) every question presupposes that its target exists, so the student becomes prone to answering even when the target is absent. In this paper, we study how to turn unlabeled images into broad visual supervision through multi-aspect self-description and privileged-view distillation, and propose CaptionOPD, which replaces tailored questions with a fixed set of image-agnostic caption prompts, ranging from general descriptions to focused captions of parts, colors, counts, text, and layout. The rollouts of a region receive different prompts, so the teacher supervises various aspects of the region. Moreover, because caption prompts mask the model to describe what is visible instead of querying a specific target, they avoid presupposing what the region contains. Using the model itself as the crop-view teacher, CaptionOPD requires no questions, no answers, and no larger model annotations: only images and region boxes. Experiments show that, trained on the same images and regions, CaptionOPD outperforms prior OPD methods across multiple fine-grained and general perception benchmarks.

new TDD: Target-Discrepancy Distillation for Underwater Image Enhancement

Authors: Zefan Feng, Zhaocheng Yu, Laibin Chang, Kui Jiang, Junjun Jiang, Xiaopeng Fan

Abstract: Underwater degradation changes image appearance and can compromise the semantic cues used to guide enhancement. Identifying scene content alone does not explicitly specify the appearance to recover or the input-dependent changes associated with that target. We propose target-discrepancy knowledge distillation (TDD) for underwater image enhancement, which organizes guidance around these two aspects of the same restoration problem. For each degraded-reference training pair, reference features from an underwater vision-language teacher provide target-appearance supervision, while paired feature residuals describe the observation's discrepancy relative to the same target. Both representations are jointly distilled into a lightweight guidance network that predicts them from the degraded image alone; neither the teacher nor reference images are required at inference. To align their use with their supervisory roles, we introduce progressive knowledge fusion within a multi-scale enhancement network. The estimated discrepancy first conditions spatial feature updates, after which target-appearance guidance refines the updated features and channel interactions consolidate their responses. This correction-before-refinement design lets target-guided refinement build on discrepancy-conditioned features. Experiments on six underwater benchmarks demonstrate competitive performance across full-reference and no-reference metrics. Ablations and feature visualizations analyze the contributions of the knowledge source, joint distillation, and progressive fusion.

new STAR: Counterfactual Dual-World Rollouts and Spatial Target Adaptive Reconstruction for Visual On-Policy Distillation

Authors: Yi Zou, Zhuang Yan, Yudong Zhang, Xu Wang, Yang Wang

Abstract: Vision-language models often rely on global context and language priors while overlooking answer-determining local details. Privileged visual on-policy self-distillation supervises full-image student rollouts using a frozen teacher that views high-resolution evidence crops. Yet our cross-scale diagnostics show that models retain over half of their full-image accuracy when local evidence is completely masked, indicating that single-image correctness does not guarantee reliance on local evidence, while the need for visual correction also varies across tokens with their spatial attention states. We propose STAR, a visual on-policy self-distillation framework combining independent counterfactual dual-world rollouts with spatial-attention-guided target reconstruction. Local image editing constructs paired worlds with identical questions and backgrounds but distinct answer-determining facts, where a shared student samples trajectories independently to expose contextual shortcuts under image-consistent prefixes. Within each world, the teacher contrasts real and mean-color crops to determine the visual correction direction, while the divergence between student image attention and a teacher-centroid spatial reference adaptively controls token-wise target log-probability reconstruction. Without auxiliary attention losses or test-time tool overhead, STAR improves Qwen3.5 across scales by up to 8.04 percentage points in average accuracy over initialization across six fine-grained benchmarks, reaches OPSD's final accuracy $4.7\times$ faster, and generalizes to out-of-domain reasoning tasks.

new OmniPersist: Persistent Dynamic World Modeling via Coupled Panoramic Evolution

Authors: Zhilu Zhang, Xiaoyu Liu, Wenxi Liu, Shengfeng He

Abstract: Action-conditioned world models play a vital role in robotics and autonomous driving, yet existing video diffusion models struggle to maintain dynamic object persistence. When the camera turns away and returns, out-of-view moving entities fail to evolve plausibly in latent space, often mutating or vanishing entirely due to bounded perspective views. To resolve this issue, we propose OmniPersist, a unified framework that couples perspective video generation with panoramic evolution within a single-stage diffusion model, without relying on auxiliary models or initial panoramic inputs. To maintain efficiency, we model the panoramic stream at a lower frame rate, bridging both views across space and time via Geometric Anchoring (GeoAnch) for spatial correspondence and Temporal Compensation (TemComp) for temporal aggregation. OmniPersist is agnostic to camera-control architectures, supporting both latent-level and attention-level conditioning. Extensive experiments on a newly constructed dynamic benchmark across image-to-video and video continuation tasks demonstrate consistent gains in visual quality, camera controllability, revisit consistency, and dynamic persistence.

new Progressive Reliable Supervision for Black-Box Universal Domain Adaptation

Authors: Jing Li, Zhe Zhang, Huateng Sun, Yuhui Yang, Wanli Xue, Xu Cheng, Fan Shi, Jianhua Zhang, Qinghua Hu, Shengyong Chen

Abstract: Black-Box Universal Domain Adaptation (B$^2$-UniDA) aims to adapt a target model to an unlabeled target domain under arbitrary label-space relationships, while neither source data nor source-model parameters are accessible during adaptation. Under such restrictive information access, reliable target supervision is unavailable, while predictions from the black-box source model can be unreliable due to source bias and domain shifts. To address this challenge, we propose Progressive Reliable Supervision (PReS), which progressively establishes and refines reliable target supervision by coordinating complementary knowledge from the black-box source model, foundation models, and the evolving target model. PReS first identifies reliable known samples by exploiting semantic consistency under diffusion-based perturbations and cross-model consensus between the black-box source model and CLIP. It then estimates the sample-wise reliability of the two predictors and adaptively integrates their complementary predictions to construct reliable pseudo labels. The resulting supervision is exploited asymmetrically to encourage confident and discriminative predictions for reliable known samples while maintaining uncertain yet locally consistent predictions for potential unknown samples. As adaptation proceeds, the emerging target-domain knowledge is further leveraged to refine both the pseudo labels and the known/unknown sample partition, forming a closed-loop process in which supervision construction and target adaptation mutually reinforce each other. Extensive experiments on multiple benchmark datasets under diverse label-space relationships demonstrate that PReS achieves state-of-the-art or highly competitive performance under the restrictive B$^2$-UniDA setting.

new COHERE4D: Training-Free Dense Surface Alignment for Coherent Metric 4D Human-Scene Reconstruction

Authors: Zhumei Wang, Shaofei Wang, Xiang An, Mingtao Pei, Siyuan Huang

Abstract: Human and scene foundation models offer complementary strengths for monocular 4D reconstruction: human models recover metric-scale body meshes but lack scene grounding, whereas scene models estimate globally consistent 3D geometry and camera motion without reliable metric scale. Crucially, both models reconstruct the same foreground human surface. Yet existing methods do not directly exploit this shared surface: lacking reliable foreground human geometry in scene reconstructions, earlier approaches either resort to sparse contact constraints or learn data-driven alignment modules that generalize poorly to out-of-distribution scenes. We present COHERE4D, a training-free framework that directly registers the human surfaces predicted by independently pretrained human and scene foundation models to reconstruct a coherent, metric 4D world. COHERE4D establishes dense, pixel-aligned 3D correspondences over the visible human surface and employs RANSAC with cross-frame consensus to suppress outliers from occlusion, background contamination, and depth noise. This formulation solves for two complementary geometric quantities: a sequence-level shared scale that calibrates scene geometry and camera motion to metric human size, and per-frame human translations that align camera-space human meshes with the scaled scene geometry to enforce spatial and temporal consistency. Across diverse backbones and benchmarks, COHERE4D substantially outperforms prior methods in human--scene alignment, metric scene and camera pose reconstruction, and global human motion estimation, adding only 4,ms/frame, enabling overall inference at up to 14 FPS.

new X-3DComposer: Feed-Forward 3D Scene Composition with Any Assets from Any Views

Authors: Zidian Qiu, Ancong Wu

Abstract: Composing an editable 3D scene from images requires an asset for each object and a layout that places the assets. 3D generation and CAD retrieval now supply the assets, but layout still lacks a general solution: scene generation methods couple it with generation, and pose estimators handle one object at a time and need true-size CAD models, depth, or iterative refinement. We present X-3DComposer, a feed-forward model that predicts the layout separately from asset generation. Given one or more images of a scene, the object masks, and an asset for each object from any source, it jointly predicts the rotation, translation, and per-axis scale of all objects in a single forward pass of about one second. The key idea is to treat objects as views: renders of the assets are processed together with the scene images by a multi-view geometry model adapted from VGGT-$\Omega$, and each object pose follows in closed form from the cameras predicted for its renders. Camera intrinsics, camera poses, and depth are optional inputs. When the meshes produced by five scene generation methods are kept fixed and only their layout is replaced, the layouts of X-3DComposer are more accurate than the native layouts of these methods. Compared with the stand-alone layout model SceneMaker, it reduces the scene-level Chamfer distance by 52% on IndoorMix, our test set from six indoor datasets, and by 28% on 3D-FUTURE.

new CalibTrack: Exploring Learned Heteroscedastic Covariance for Robust Multi-Camera Pedestrian Tracking

Authors: Jack Roberts, Aditya Iyer, Nora Ayanian

Abstract: Analytical multi-view multi-object tracking (MV-MOT) systems rely on measurement covariance estimates to guide association, fusion, and state estimation. These covariance estimates are typically heuristic, requiring hand-tuning to the specific deployment scenario. We investigate how learned heteroscedastic measurement covariance affects calibration and robustness in analytical multi-camera tracking. To support this analysis, we introduce CalibTrack, a detector-side predictor that replaces uniform measurement covariance with per-detection covariance. We evaluate CalibTrack on the WildTrack and MultiviewX pedestrian tracking datasets with multiple analytical tracker cores, including an identity-informed GM-PHD, JIPDA, and pruned delta-GLMB filter. We additionally evaluate robustness by simulating adverse weather conditions with stable diffusion, Gaussian noise, camera browning, and camera dropout. CalibTrack improves average adverse tracking performance over confidence-scaled covariance, while producing better calibrated predictions, indicating the value of heteroscedastic covariance within analytical tracking. Comparisons with stronger heuristic covariance methods show that the benefits of learned covariance remain bounded by the quality of available observations and the downstream tracker choice. Together, these findings provide a principled exploration of learned covariance estimation and its potential for uncertainty-aware analytical tracking.

new D\'ej\`a-VAE: Training-Free Consistent Editing with Pseudo-Invertible Encoders and Latent Back-Projection

Authors: Ron Libman, Yamit Ehrlich, Ohad Fried, Assaf Shocher

Abstract: Consistency is essential to generative workflows: repeated processing should preserve information beyond the changes explicitly requested. In latent diffusion models, this requires stability both when moving between latent and pixel spaces and when applying successive edits. Autoencoding errors and unintended changes from the editing model can accumulate across these operations, progressively altering image structure, texture, and color. We introduce D\'ej\`a-VAE, a framework that combines exact autoencoding consistency with calibrated editing stabilization, without training or fine-tuning. Its autoencoding component converts a pretrained variational autoencoder into a surjective pseudo-invertible neural network, with a fixed gain balancing deviations from the pretrained encoder and decoder. The modified decoder $D'$ is an explicit nonlinear right inverse of the encoder $E'$, satisfying $E'(D'(z))=z$ by construction. Consequently, $D'$ is a reflexive generalized inverse: latent round trips preserve their input, up to numerical precision, and repeated image reconstruction is idempotent. To suppress editing drift, adaptive wavelet back-projection finds the closest latent to the editor's output in $\ell_2$, subject to matching the previous latent on wavelet coefficients whose changes fall below calibrated thresholds. This correction is applied once per turn, with no additional network evaluations after calibration. Together, these components support workflows that remain in latent space or interleave generation with pixel-space operations and constraints. Tests across models and editing benchmarks show less drift than stock pipelines and dedicated consistency methods.

new An Integrated Remote Sensing and Machine Learning Framework for Mapping Hazard Associated with Leached Metals

Authors: Salman Karim, Bahareh Nojabaei, Hosein Foroutan

Abstract: This study integrates satellite remote sensing, groundwater monitoring, and machine learning to map leached-metal-associated hazards at four coal combustion residual (CCR) sites in Virginia, USA. Constituent-specific groundwater concentrations were expressed as Hazard Quotients (HQ) and combined into a composite Hazard Index (HI). Predictors included Sentinel-2 vegetation indices, biophysical parameters, and spatiotemporal metrics (2019-2025), alongside soil, climate, hydrology, topography, and land cover. Six supervised classifiers were evaluated using spatially separated test blocks, feature-set ablation, and leave-one-site-out testing. Higher HQ coincided with lower vegetation indices in 24 of 33 metal-site combinations near monitoring wells (sign test p = 0.007). Well HI was negatively correlated with CIRE and canopy chlorophyll content at three sites (Spearman rho as low as -0.71). Random Forest achieved within-site HI classification accuracies of 0.768-0.924 and higher-HI-class recall of 0.965-0.999. For 34 constituent-site combinations, HQ classification accuracies ranged from 0.775 to 0.989. Environmental covariates generally contributed more predictive information than vegetation features. Removing vegetation predictors did not reduce performance, and distance to the CCR unit alone performed best at one site. Zero-shot cross-site accuracy declined to 0.734-0.749, no better than the majority-class baseline. These findings demonstrate the potential of integrating satellite observations and environmental data with groundwater measurements for site-calibrated spatial hazard screening. The framework identifies indirect environmental associations rather than directly detecting dissolved metals, highlighting the importance of local calibration and independent validation.

new GeoVLDrive: Geometry-Grounded Vision-Language Reasoning for Autonomous Driving

Authors: JiaHui Huang, Songyan Zhang, Hanbing Li, Hanchao Leng, Kuiyuan Yang, Guang Chen, Hangjun Ye, Chen Lv

Abstract: Vision-language-action (VLA) planners inherit semantic knowledge from pretrained vision-language models, but their reasoning is rarely grounded in 3D scene structure and its evolution over time. We introduce GeoVLDrive, a geometry-grounded VLA framework for 3D spatiotemporal reasoning and planning. Multi-layer features from a 3D foundation model augment the vision-language backbone through gated cross-attention. A structured latent chain of thought (CoT) organizes future scene geometry, key-agent motion, and meta-actions into ordered, separately supervised latent states. An action expert generates trajectory proposals from these states and multi-layer scene features, and ranks them using scene-grounded, interacting score tokens. We also introduce NAVSIM 3DQA to train and evaluate metric 3D spatial and motion understanding. GeoVLDrive achieves 91.8 PDMS on NAVSIM v1 and 90.9 EPDMS on NAVSIM v2. On NAVSIM 3DQA, the geometry-grounded backbone scores 89.2% overall, 4.6 percentage points above the fine-tuned Qwen3-VL-2B baseline. On LingoQA, the backbone achieves 68.6% Lingo-Judge accuracy.

new DiffAnchor: Anchor-Space Diffusion for Monocular 3D Hand Pose Estimation

Authors: Changlong Jiang, Youci Tang, Haohong Kuang, Hang Xu, Jinghong Zheng, Ran Wang, Zhenghong Yu, Chen Feng, Xiaofeng Yue, Yang Xiao

Abstract: Accurate and efficient monocular 3D hand pose estimation is an important perception capability for embodied human interaction and dexterous manipulation. Recent diffusion-based methods model pose uncertainty by iteratively denoising 3D joint coordinates. However, directly diffusing the final joints requires the generative process to simultaneously explore plausible poses and recover precise coordinates, often relying on additional denoising steps or multiple hypotheses and consequently increasing inference cost. In this work, we propose DiffAnchor, a few-step anchor-space diffusion framework that diffuses anchors rather than joints. Our key insight is that stochastic exploration does not need to operate directly on the final output. Instead, diffusion searches for informative intermediate anchors that serve as local 3D regressors, while anchor-to-joint offset prediction and weighted aggregation complete precise joint localization. Specifically, fixed context anchors provide stable global references, whereas noisy dynamic anchors are iteratively refined toward informative joint regions. An importance-guided anchor selection and replenishment mechanism further retains useful anchors, removes ineffective ones, and explores insufficiently covered regions. This design reduces the precision burden of the diffusion process and enables accurate prediction using only a few denoising steps and a single hypothesis. Extensive experiments on HO-3D V2, NYU, ICVL, and InterHand2.6M demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on the first three benchmarks and competitive performance on challenging interacting-hand estimation, while maintaining inference efficiency comparable to deterministic regression baselines.

new HS-OVSeg: Multi-Granularity Open-Vocabulary Semantic Segmentation in Remote Sensing via Heterogeneous Supervision

Authors: Wei Huang, Chenying Liu, Xudong Kang, Xiao Xiang Zhu

Abstract: Open-vocabulary semantic segmentation (OVSS) enables flexible, text-driven mapping of remote sensing (RS) imagery beyond predefined categories. Despite advances in segmentation foundation models, RS-OVSS remains constrained by the scarcity of large-scale, semantically diverse datasets with accurate pixel-level annotations. Meanwhile, existing RS datasets contain abundant scene- and object-level annotations that offer valuable semantic information but lack precise pixel-wise supervision. To exploit these complementary sources, we propose HS-OVSeg, a unified training framework that integrates heterogeneous scene-, object-, and pixel-level supervision for RS-OVSS. Specifically, we generate pixel-wise pseudo-labels using SAM3, conditioned on scene-level class names and object-level class names with bounding boxes. Combined with ground-truth semantic masks, these pseudo-labels enable joint training across all three annotation granularities through LoRA-based fine-tuning of SAM3. Our unified training spans 20 RS datasets, including 3 scene classification, 4 object detection, and 13 semantic segmentation datasets, with 1,000 images from the 7 classification and detection datasets manually annotated for pixel-level evaluation. HS-OVSeg substantially outperforms existing RS-OVSS models across both the 20 training-source and 5 held-out datasets, demonstrating strong multi-granularity segmentation capabilities under heterogeneous supervision. The code will be publicly available upon publication.

new Rescuing Discarded Boundary Samples in Semi-Supervised Learning

Authors: Yuzhao Jiang, Meng Zhang, Jie Ma, Xiaohong Guan

Abstract: The field of semi-supervised learning (SSL) has seen substantial progress, driven by ongoing refinements within the pseudo-label SSL paradigm. The main difficulty lies in reducing the adverse impact of confirmation bias. The predominant approach is to apply a confidence threshold during training to discard low-confidence samples. Nevertheless, while this mitigates the bias, the discarded samples are mostly situated near class boundaries, and consequently, this strategy also loses valuable boundary information. To address this problem, we propose Boundary Refinement and Correction (BRC), an add-on for confidence-thresholded SSL that rescues useful discarded samples without changing how the base SSL method selects pseudo-labels. BRC keeps the original confidence-based selection rule and operates only on the rejected set. It first identifies discarded samples that may still be useful for training, then replaces their original pseudo-labels with corrected soft pseudo-labels and returns them to the unsupervised loss. Extensive experiments on CIFAR-100, STL-10, and TissueMNIST demonstrate the flexibility and broad applicability of BRC across diverse datasets and base SSL methods, reducing error rates by up to 4.97 percentage points. On boundary-near samples, BRC further improves Top-1 accuracy by 12.80 percentage points, directly supporting its ability to refine the model's decision boundary. An exploratory extension to semantic segmentation also yields consistent gains of 1.03-2.39 mIoU, indicating that BRC generalizes beyond image classification.

new RSReasoner: Reformulating and Auditing Geospatial Reasoning Segmentation

Authors: Xu Zhang, Xinpeng Zhang, Yunrui Zou, Hongliang Chen, Xiaotian Jiang, Yang Zheng, Kaitai Guo, Jimin Liang

Abstract: Geospatial reasoning segmentation aims to localize geographic targets from language requiring semantic inference beyond direct visual perception. However, existing benchmarks often operationalize reasoning as category omission while overlooking attribute mixing, which neglects explicit-category reasoning, introduces perceptual shortcuts, and fails to precisely evaluate reasoning capability. To diagnose and address these limitations, we first reformulate geospatial reasoning segmentation along category expression (explicit vs. implicit) and attribute nature (perceptual vs. inferential), and audit 90,708 queries across existing benchmarks to reveal that pure explicit-inferential queries account for only 0.61% whereas 31.98% mix both attribute types. We then develop a three-stage pipeline that harnesses OpenStreetMap attributes while mitigating geometric and semantic noise to construct RSReasoner, a large-scale dataset of 106,610 images with 213,220 reasoning queries, and RSReasoner-Bench, a diagnostic benchmark of 75,400 queries across four canonical types and controlled variants. Extensive experiments demonstrate that RSReasoner-Bench precisely evaluates model capability across multiple dimensions while exposing shortcut behaviors, and fine-tuning on RSReasoner boosts both reasoning and cross-type generalization. Our findings offer key insights for future geospatial reasoning research and benchmark design. Datasets and code will be released at https://github.com/Ricardo-XZ/RSReasoner.

URLs: https://github.com/Ricardo-XZ/RSReasoner.

new Advanced Synthetic Image Detection Using Hybrid Feature Extraction

Authors: Ameya Uppina, S Navaneetha Krishnan, Nikhil N Iyer, Joe Dhanith P R

Abstract: In the realm of generative AI, models such as GANs, MidJourney, DALL-E, and others have been continuously evolving to generate increasingly lifelike images. This rapid advancement has created an urgent need for robust methods to distinguish between real and AI-generated images. This paper proposes a novel approach for the detection of synthetic images by employing deep learning techniques for feature analysis. The proposed architecture classifies images by identifying variations that generative models struggle to replicate, particularly fine-grained textures and complex object features. Deformations in complexes are examined with the help of the CBAM architecture along with ResNet50. We have performed a selective patch-based analysis using Discrete Cosine Transform (DCT) scoring, extracting the regions with the most and least complexity for further processing with an SRM Module and ResNet50 module. Experimental results show that our approach achieves high accuracy in classifying real-world images from synthetic ones, contributing to the development of more reliable detection frameworks.

new Smooth Essential-Manifold Residuals for View-Graph Calibration

Authors: Viktor Larsson, Johannes Sch\"onberger

Abstract: View-graph calibration jointly estimates camera intrinsics from multiple pairwise fundamental matrices. The correct intrinsics transform each fundamental matrix into an essential matrix, casting the calibration problem as minimizing the distance to the essential manifold. We study how to represent this objective for nonlinear least squares. The conventional singular-value formulation collapses two independent essential-matrix constraints into one scalar residual. Its per-edge Gauss-Newton model is therefore rank deficient, which leads to poor convergence. We instead derive an isometric reduction to 2x2 matrices, where each branch of the nonlinear essential-matrix constraint becomes a linear subspace. This reduction yields a smooth two-dimensional residual whose squared norm is exactly the original manifold distance, whose Gauss-Newton approximation is generically full rank, and whose evaluation requires no per-edge singular value decomposition. Experiments show that the new residual retains the estimation accuracy of the geometric objective while converging in fewer, computationally cheaper iterations. Combined with robust initialization and optimization, it achieves state-of-the-art view-graph calibration accuracy at a runtime comparable to inexpensive algebraic objectives.

new Align or Fail: The Hidden Gap in Cross-Domain Panoptic Segmentation

Authors: Huy Hoang Nguyen, Oliver Zendel, Tobias Glueck, Minh Nhat Vu, Andreas Kugi

Abstract: Vision foundation backbones are often treated as drop-in upgrades for improving region proposals in open-vocabulary panoptic segmentation (OVPS). Yet, improvements in backbones often fail to translate well into downstream cross-domain performance. Using ODISE, a diffusion-based OVPS model with a CLIP-based recognition branch, as a testbed, we replace its diffusion visual extractor with DINOv2, ConvNeXt, or DINOv3, and find that backbone upgrades can introduce a grouping-recognition mismatch: an alignment gap between dense-region features and CLIP text embeddings. Across source and near-domain, the newer DINOv3 variant improves source-domain performance but collapses on several cross-domain targets, whereas other baselines, interestingly, show the opposite. Stage-wise diagnostics, linear probes, and region-text alignment analyses indicate that DINOv3 embeddings retain semantic information but are geometrically incompatible with CLIP. We test adaptations, including text-side adapters, conformal mask retention (CMR), and a lean 2.7M-parameter visual-side LoRA, and find that text-side adaptation is more sensitive to vocabulary overlap. CMR provides only limited and target-specific gains, indicating that retention alone cannot resolve the full cross-domain failure. To understand why ADE20K resists improvement across all interventions, we evaluate OVSeg, CAT-Seg, and MaskCLIP. This separates two failure axes: a recoverable visual-to-CLIP interface gap that visual-side LoRA largely repairs, and a vocabulary-boundary limitation shared by the evaluated CLIP-based recognition systems that requires different remedies.

new SetCon: Setup-Conditioned IMU-Language Learning from 3D Human Motion

Authors: Lennart Schiweck, Alaa Saleh, Crist\'obal Curio

Abstract: Aligning inertial measurement unit (IMU) signals with text enables zero-shot activity recognition across wearable devices. A worn device's signal depends on its setup, the combination of body site and mounting. Leading IMU-language models make their encoders invariant to the mounting, which also discards the device's orientation relative to the body, such as the direction of gravity that separates sitting, standing and lying. We introduce SetCon, which replaces full invariance with selective invariance. It estimates each device's mounting on its known body site from a few unlabeled windows and conditions its encoder on this estimate. Because the mounting stays nearly fixed while the device is worn, the moving body serves as a calibration target. Both calibration and representation learning are supervised by 3D human motion, since one movement can be simulated under many known sensor setups. At test time, a class is represented by its name or by an anchor, which averages the text embeddings of the name and of activity descriptions and needs no labeled window. A language model built on the frozen encoder also answers how and in what order a movement is performed. Trained only on IMU simulated from the same Nymeria motion capture as AnyMo, SetCon reaches 56.6% mean zero-shot accuracy on 13 real datasets with class names and 65.3% with anchors, against 41.8% and 46.7% for AnyMo.

new MinPair: Measured Minimal Pairs for Fine-Grained Control in Text-to-Motion Generation

Authors: Lennart Schiweck, Crist\'obal Curio

Abstract: Text-to-motion generation has made great progress in capturing what action to perform, yet it remains limited in controlling how that action is executed. For example, when only the height word of a kick request changes, none of the tested state-of-the-art methods reaches all three of knee, waist and head height. The other words of a caption usually imply an attribute such as height, so neither the training loss nor the standard metrics require a model to use the word that states it. A model has to use this word only when it alone separates two otherwise identical captions. We introduce MinPair, which creates such caption pairs from real motion clips. It starts from rule-based motion descriptors that measure the 3D body motion of each clip. From these descriptors a language model writes MinPair captions, timed body-part captions that say how each body part moves. The same descriptors show in which attributes two clips of the same action differ. A measured minimal pair joins the true caption of one clip with an altered copy in which only one attribute takes the value measured in the other clip. A contrastive pair loss then requires a diffusion denoiser to reconstruct the clip more accurately from its true caption than from the altered copy. Training needs no synthetic motion and inference stays unchanged. Control is tested on 22 target tasks and on caption edits that each change one attribute. MinPair captions alone help motions reach their targets and improve the standard metrics M2M, R@3 and FID, yet a model trained on them still ignores most single-word edits. The pair loss raises the share of followed template edits from 11% to 60%, while the standard metrics do not improve further. With body-part captions, MinPair reaches more targets than state-of-the-art methods that were trained on up to 99 times more motion data.

new UnifiedAD: One-Shot Anomaly Detection across Modalities via Evidence Calibration

Authors: Shangze Li, Chuancheng Shi, Simiao Xie, Wenhua Wu, Xingyu Zhu, Fei Shen, Tat-Seng Chua

Abstract: Both single-modal and multi-modal anomaly detection aim to identify deviations from normal patterns, providing a common foundation for unified detection. However, existing methods rely on modality-specific modeling and scoring, making it difficult to obtain comparable anomaly evidence with limited normal references. To that end, we propose UnifiedAD, which unifies single-modal and multi-modal anomaly detection through anomaly evidence calibration, requiring only one normal reference per target category and no gradient updates. Specifically, we first derive neuron-level discriminative priors from normal and anomalous samples in an independent auxiliary dataset and combine them with neuron response consistency under normality-preserving transformations of the target reference to select reliable neurons, termed anomaly neurons, and identify anomaly directions. Next, we construct a low-rank normal subspace for each layer and modality from the selected neurons' spatial responses to the reference and quantify query feature deviations from normal structure using projection residuals. Finally, we separately calibrate projection residuals and anomaly-direction responses using the response distributions obtained from the normal reference and its transformations, converting them into comparable anomaly evidence for aggregation across layers and modalities. Experiments on multiple anomaly detection benchmarks demonstrate that UnifiedAD achieves strong performance in both image-level anomaly detection and pixel-level anomaly localization.

new PartAgent: Agentic 3D Part Segmentation via Expert Integration and Closed-Loop Refinement

Authors: Yang Cao, Zhe Zhu, Yiheng Zhang, Zhengqiang Zhang, Haoyuan Li, Le Wan, Dan Xu

Abstract: Segmenting a 3D object into semantically meaningful parts requires joint reasoning about geometric structure and semantic context. Pretrained 3D experts provide spatially grounded region proposals, while 2D experts provide semantic cues for identifying object parts. The challenge is to integrate these complementary capabilities into a framework that can both produce meaningful part segmentations and progressively refine them. We present PartAgent, a training-free agentic framework for 3D part segmentation built upon two complementary modules: (i) Structural-Semantic Expert Integration combines regions predicted by the 3D expert with multiview semantic evidence from the 2D expert through image-to-surface correspondences, constructing compositional part partitions at multiple granularities. (ii) Observation-Driven Closed-Loop Partition Refinement observes the current partition and its supporting evidence to select targeted inspection, evidence repair, composition, or reselection actions. It verifies proposed changes and uses the updated state and action outcomes to guide subsequent decisions. On PartObjaverse-Tiny-WT, PartAgent achieves 68.41 mIoU, outperforming the strongest competing method by 8.53 points.
