Across 3DCoMPaT200, PartNeXt, PartNetE, and PartObjaverse-Tiny, PartAgent achieves the highest mIoU on each dataset, averaging 69.36 across the four datasets, 7.28 points above the highest mean among competing methods.

new Sol$^{3}$: Accelerating Video Diffusion for Free via Hierarchical Sparse Attention Routing

Authors: Guoxin Fan, Weilun Feng, Chuanguang Yang, Mingqiang Wu, Xiaokun Liu, Jitao Lu, Zhulin An, Libo Huang, Boyu Diao, Di Gao, Yongjun Xu

Abstract: Sol-Attn (Sparsifying online attention) combines sparse exact computation with low-cost representative approximation to accelerate training-free inference in diffusion video models. However, intra-block attention can focus on only a few tokens. While preserving these key interactions exactly, the block-level decision incurs the cost of evaluating many low-response members. We propose Sol$^{3}$, a training-free hierarchical sparse attention method that further refines how exact computations are allocated. First, we introduce Gaussian-guided hierarchical routing, which determines the scope of exact computation step by step, from blocks to tokens, using statistical thresholds at each level. Only a subset of members within Mixed blocks that require refinement is selected for exact computation, while representative terms approximate the remaining members. We further design heterogeneous routing statistics that combine block-center correlations with variations in Key and Value within blocks to identify blocks that are difficult to approximate. Among these Mixed blocks, we uniformly compare raw token relevance to select members requiring exact computation. Experiments on video generation, audio-video co-generation, and world simulation tasks demonstrate that Sol$^{3}$ achieves 86% attention sparsity and delivers a $3.07\times$ attention speedup while preserving quality. Our code is available at https://github.com/FofGofx/sol3.

URLs: https://github.com/FofGofx/sol3.

new CoReQ: Condition-Aware Cross-Modal Repair and Quality-Guided Robust Collaborative Perception

Authors: Ziwei Guo, Lingzhi Li, Jin Wang, Yunjiang Xu, Yupeng Ouyang, Benyuan Yang, Yujun Shi

Abstract: Multi-vehicle collaborative perception can use observations from different perspectives to alleviate the problems of occlusion and limited sensing range. However, missing echoes and false echoes caused by bad weather will reduce the quality of single-vehicle features and affect collaborative detection. LiDAR provides richer geometric information, and 4D Radar has strong weather adaptability. The complementarity of the two provides the possibility to improve perception under degraded observations. However, how to adjust cross-modal repair according to the current observation conditions and how to reasonably integrate multi-vehicle features whose reliability still differs after repair are still problems that need to be solved. To this end, we propose CoReQ, a condition-aware repair and quality-guided collaborative perception framework. For the former, the condition-guided bidirectional cross-modal repair module (CBCR) extracts vehicle-level observation conditions from point cloud occupancy, density and feature statistics, and adjusts the bidirectional correction of LiDAR and Radar features accordingly. For the latter, we combine local reliability assessment with collaborative weighting: first, the task-aware quality estimation module (TaQE) uses the classification and positioning information of the EMA teacher to construct a supervision target and predict the spatial quality of the repaired features; then, the condition and quality guided fusion module (CoQF) matches the quality information with the content, and the observation conditions of both parties are jointly used for multi-vehicle feature weighted fusion. Experiments on V2X-R and V2X-Radar-C show that the framework is competitive under a variety of observation conditions, achieving significant gains in AP@0.7 on V2X-R under snowy conditions, and achieving the highest AP among the compared V2X-Radar-C methods.

new FewRef-5: Fixed-Reference Test-Time Adaptation for Virtual Staining

Authors: Wenxuan Wang, Liwen Wang, Mingmin Wu, Xingbo Dong, Zhe Jin

Abstract: Virtual staining synthesizes special-stain images from routine hematoxylin and eosin (H&E), extending the information available from an existing digital slide. However, a fixed inference rule can leave available real target-stain references unused, limiting query-specific correction of stain appearance at deployment. Test-time adaptation (TTA) provides a mechanism for this reference-guided, query-specific refinement without retraining the generator. We therefore propose FewRef-5, a reference-assisted TTA method for virtual staining built on StainPromptInversion. We extract tissue-masked optical-density means and standard deviations from five real Masson's trichrome (MAS) training patches and average them into a fixed stain target. A scheduled statistic-matching loss introduces this target into per-query visual-prompt optimization alongside our edge/Gram consistency penalties and the inherited reconstruction objectives. The target remains unchanged across queries, avoiding recursive dependence on earlier generated outputs while the pretrained generator stays fixed. On recorded H&E-to-MAS outputs for 1,989 queries, FewRef-5 reduces target-set FID from 183.201 to 166.904 and increases source-referenced SSIM from 0.92787 to 0.94996 relative to our Edge+Gram variant. Source PSNR and MS-SSIM decrease, showing an endpoint trade-off rather than uniformly better reconstruction.

new VenusOCR2.0: Towards Efficient and Effective Document Parsing

Authors: Lei Li, Zheng Wei, Yi Yuan, Qingbin Liu, Jiang Bian, Zang Li

Abstract: We present VenusOCR2.0, a compact 1B-parameter vision-language recognizer designed for both high-accuracy and high-throughput document parsing across text, tables, and formulas. To continuously improve recognition quality without self-training collapse, we build a bounded recursive self-improvement (RSI) data engine where the model acts strictly as a weakness probe, while external multi-expert medoid consensus, VLM visual verification, and factorized synthesis supply reliable supervision across pretraining, curated SFT, and RLVR. VenusOCR2.0 couples a dynamic-resolution SigLIP2 encoder and 2x2 spatial token merging with a Qwen3-0.6B decoder, adopting compact atomic OTSL tokens to shorten table sequences and reduce structural errors. To further eliminate the autoregressive decoding bottleneck, we introduce PTPv2, a lightweight ($\sim$67M) decoupled parallel draft module that combines frozen multi-layer verifier conditioning, a standalone learned mask, and block-causal all-position next-token prediction. On OmniDocBenchv1.6, VenusOCR2.0 achieves an overall score of 96.45 and a state-of-the-art 98.57 formula CDM, substantially outperforming massive general-purpose VLMs such as Gemini-3-Pro (92.91) and Qwen3-VL-235B (89.78). Deployed in vLLM on a single H20 GPU, PTPv2 delivers a 2.96x single-request speedup and up to 2.36x higher batched throughput while guaranteeing lossless, token-for-token equivalence with standard autoregressive decoding.

new ShapeQuest: Towards Granularity-Controlled Edit-Conditioned 3D Asset Retrieval

Authors: Faris Abu-Ali, Sankalp Sinha, Didier Stricker, Muhammad Zeshan Afzal

Abstract: Given a reference asset and an instruction such as "remove one armrest," a retriever must find variants that make the edit and preserve the other parts. This search is well established for images but remains underexplored for individual 3D assets. To study this problem, we introduce ShapeQuest and ShapeQuester, a new benchmark and model for edit-conditioned 3D asset retrieval. ShapeQuest's open-source pipeline edits textured meshes through part additions, removals, and material and colour changes. It controls edit granularity across five levels, defined by the fraction of the object's surface area each edit affects. It constructs multiple valid targets when an instruction leaves the choice of part open. A vision-language model judges each edit from views chosen to expose the edited parts, and human raters audit its judgments. ShapeQuest has 87,824 queries in 50 categories and a 50,919-shape gallery with competing edits. To distinguish these variants, ShapeQuester learns retrieval alongside reconstruction of the edited point cloud, with part masks supervising changed and preserved regions. With a frozen pretrained point encoder, ShapeQuester reaches 50.4 operation-macro mAP@10 on the test split, against 47.2 for the fusion baseline with the same inputs and 48.6 for the strongest baseline. Reconstruction adds 6.5 points on the validation split when the point encoder is trained from scratch, including 3.3 from part masks.

new On the Capabilities and Limitations of Zero-Shot Open-Vocabulary Cloud Segmentation

Authors: Jose Sosa, Thomas Gillet, Rim Sleimi, Albert Abello

Abstract: Recent advances in vision-language foundation models have enabled open-vocabulary semantic segmentation (OVSS) without task-specific training, offering new opportunities for Earth Observation (EO) applications. However, their capabilities for cloud detection remain insufficiently explored. In this work, we evaluate twelve OVSS approaches across six publicly available EO datasets in a zero-shot setting, i.e., without fine-tuning or adaptation to the target data. We investigate both binary cloud detection and multiclass segmentation, distinguishing between thick clouds, thin clouds, cloud shadows, and background. Our results show differences in performance across models and datasets, with SegEarth-OV3 achieving the highest binary cloud detection performance on four of the six datasets. While several approaches demonstrate promising performance in binary cloud detection, multiclass segmentation remains more challenging, particularly when distinguishing between fine-grained cloud categories. Furthermore, our controlled prompt sensitivity analysis reveals model-dependent responses to alternative textual descriptions, with appearance-oriented prompts benefiting certain approaches while degrading the performance of others. Overall, our findings demonstrate both the potential and current limitations of zero-shot OVSS for cloud detection, establishing a comparative benchmark for future research on generalisable cloud segmentation. Code will be available at: https://github.com/josesosajs/zero-shot-cloudovss.

new Completion-Aware Compression for Progressive 3D Gaussian Splatting

Authors: Reagan Koo, Yeong-Gyu Kim, Eun-Seok Ryu

Abstract: 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) enables high-quality novel-view rendering, but its large scene representations are costly to transmit. Pruning and compression reduce file sizes, yet progressive delivery also requires usable reconstructions before the full scene arrives. Existing progressive representations provide partial scenes, but level-based updates are limited by predefined coding boundaries. This paper presents a progressive 3DGS codec that balances attribute fidelity with the image benefit of making Gaussians available earlier. Its single compact stream supports partial 3D reconstruction and adds newly decoded Gaussians as more bytes arrive. The stream supports fine-grained updates at arbitrary received-byte budgets, without separate encodings or later attribute refinement. At matched complete-file sizes and received bytes, completion-aware allocation improves 1/8 PSNR over attribute-only allocation by 0.48-1.43 dB. The proposed codec compresses original 3DGS representations by 62-88 times while retaining near-reference Full PSNR. Under the evaluated 50-Mbps delivery model, partial reconstruction becomes available in approximately 0.2 s.

new HazeFlux: Few-Step Generative Priors for Real-World Image Dehazing

Authors: Yunong Liu, Danna Xue, Javier Vazquez-Corral

Abstract: Real-world single-image dehazing suffers from severe domain shifts, as paired real data is scarce and models trained on synthetic haze generalize poorly to real-world haze. While pre-trained generative editing models offer powerful image priors to bridge this generalization gap, applying them to image dehazing poses two major challenges: standard generative models prioritize distribution alignment over strict pixel-level fidelity, and operating in compressed latent spaces inherently discards fine-grained structural details. In this paper, we propose a novel two-stage framework that adapts the generative prior of a few-step flow-matching backbone to real-world dehazing. In the first stage, we fine-tune the latent backbone using Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA), supervised not only on a flow-matching objective, but also on pixel-level supervision, thus enabling direct hazy-to-clear translation. In the second stage, we introduce structure-preserving skip connections directly to the VAE decoder, bypassing latent compression artifacts to recover precise spatial edges. Experiments on four realworld benchmarks demonstrate that our approach significantly outperforms existing dehazing methods in real conditions.

new ICLWorld: Benchmarking In-Context Learning of Agents from Digital to Physical Worlds

Authors: Qingle Liu, Jieming Li, Pei-lin Li, Shenghan Zheng, Ruize Yang, Sunqi Fan, Deyao Hong, Huan-ang Gao, Meng-Hao Guo, Shuojin Yang

Abstract: In-context learning (ICL) lets agents acquire new rules from context and adapt to user-specific requirements at inference time. The same frontier agents now operate both computers and robots, yet their ICL remains insufficiently assessed: existing benchmarks are largely offline, rely on text or static images, and risk training-data contamination, while robot evaluations are limited to method-level ablations. We present ICLWorld, an online benchmark that evaluates the ICL of agents from digital to physical worlds, covering computer use and robot use, together with UIverse, a unified harness that turns every environment into a user interface. We build 30 environments, record 630 demonstration videos (10+ hours), and design 160 tasks with customized, often counter-intuitive rules that cannot be satisfied without learning from the context. Each task comes with three rule versions, given as text or video, and we test generalization at three levels, from replaying the demonstration to new instances and unseen environments. Across six frontier models, the strongest agents, Claude Opus 5.5 and GPT-6 Astra, succeed on 82% and 76% of computer-use runs but only 27% and 26% of robot-use runs, and a no-rule control reduces success to at most 8%. GPT-6 Astra succeeds under all three rule versions on only 38% of computer-use tasks, half its rate under a single rule, and answers 98% of robot rule questions correctly yet completes only 20% of these tasks. Frontier agents learn rules comparably well from video and from text and transfer them across environments, whereas weaker agents rely on text and lose most of their video-based success once the demonstrated situation is resampled. These findings reveal substantial gaps between the digital and the physical world and between identifying a rule and acting on it.

new LaWanle: Why Can't Leading Driving Models Make a Simple Turn?

Authors: Chengzhi Gao, Junzhe Gao, Yongzhi Liu, Weichao Zhuang, Weilong Hu, Taonian Liang, Guodong Yin

Abstract: We were surprised to find that leading end-to-end driving models across major paradigms, despite scoring close to human drivers on NAVSIM, cannot reliably make a simple 90-degree turn in photorealistic closed-loop simulators. To investigate why, we build LaWanle, a benchmark of turns drawn from three such simulators. Driving each turn from several starts, we find that the same model passes it from one start and fails it from another, and that the two drives look alike until they enter the junction. Rotating the car by a few degrees at the junction entry can fail a turn it otherwise passes. Conversely, a slight heading correction to the plans can rescue about half of the failed turns we tested. Such errors arise when a model drives by itself, so we post-train an open-source lane-keeping model with closed-loop DAgger on its lateral path. Training 5% of its parameters is enough to raise its turn pass rate from 19% to 76%. The gain transfers to the two other benchmarks, where the policy also scores among the leading models. Further fine-tuning on recovery data repairs the deviations it is trained on but, against a control trained equally long, improves turns on none of the three benchmarks, and every variant stays far below a human driver.

new Attack-Inference Aligned Universal Adversarial Attacks on Video Object Segmentation

Authors: Ruiqi Shen, Chang Liu, Henghui Ding

Abstract: Modern video object segmentation (VOS) relies on a two-stage inference paradigm: target initialization and memory-based propagation. However, existing adversarial attacks on VOS often overlook this structure. To bridge this gap, we propose AUV, an adversarial framework that Aligns with the Universal two-stage paradigm of VOS models. Specifically, AUV optimizes a universal adversarial perturbation (UAP) through decoupled training strategies tailored for both initialization and propagation stages. By disrupting the shared memory space, it achieves prompt-agnostic attacks across different modalities, including masks, points, boxes, and language. Extensive experiments show that AUV establishes state-of-the-art VOS attack performance, such as degrading mask-based SAM3 to only 8.4 J&F on DAVIS17-val (compared to 55.4 for the previous SOTA under the same training setup). It also exhibits superior temporal dynamics with the fastest onset, the most enduring persistence, and the support of any-time mid-video attacks. The AUV optimization framework is applicable to different VOS models, revealing vulnerabilities in real-world streaming VOS. Code is available at https://github.com/FudanCVL/AUV.

URLs: https://github.com/FudanCVL/AUV.

new Text Is Just Another Shot in Vision-Language Models: Simple Probes on Frozen Encoders Do It

Authors: Reda Bensaid, Ismail Ben Ayed, Fran\c{c}ois Leduc-Primeau, Vincent Gripon

Abstract: Few-shot adaptation of vision-language models has produced increasingly elaborate methods built on CLIP and on one shared principle: the zero-shot text classifier is the anchor, and labeled images only correct it. We argue that this principle reflects CLIP's visual features rather than a property of the task. We provide a simple Bayesian interpretation, in which a class name is worth exactly kappa = sigma^2 / tau^2 labeled images, the ratio between the visual spread of a class and the misalignment of its name. The Bayes classifier of this model counts text as kappa more samples. With modern frozen encoders, few-shot adaptation then reduces to a well-regularised convex probe. Specifically, we introduce TASP, a simple convex logistic regression on frozen features that incorporates class-name embeddings as weighted training examples and as a regularization prior. On the standard 11-dataset benchmark, TASP with frozen PE-Core-L features achieves 87.1% accuracy at 1 shot and 91.4% at 16 shots, outperforming state-of-the-art few-shot vision-language adaptation methods by 4.6 to 11.0 percentage points. While stronger representations explain much of this improvement, controlled comparisons on matched features show that existing frozen-encoder adaptation methods do not materially improve on this probe. We further find that the weight assigned to text matters more than how it is incorporated, and that combining self-supervised visual features with vision-language representations is most beneficial for weaker visual encoders. Together, these findings suggest that progress in few-shot vision-language adaptation depends more on representation quality and the appropriate use of text supervision than on increasingly complex adaptation mechanisms.

new TokenMatte: Unlocking T2V Diffusion Priors for Pixel-Native Video Matting

Authors: Zanyi Wang, Dengyang Jiang, Fengfei Yu, Shilong Liu

Abstract: High-resolution video matting requires accurate recovery of continuous alpha values, including fine hair, soft boundaries, and motion blur, yet dense alpha supervision remains scarce. Pretrained text-to-video diffusion transformers (DiTs) offer rich spatio-temporal priors, whereas existing generative video matting approaches typically route alpha predictions through RGB-trained VAE latents, which can attenuate fine details and introduce additional decoding overhead. We present TokenMatte, which adapts a pretrained video DiT into a single-pass, auxiliary-free RGB-to-alpha predictor with a pixel-native output interface. At its core is a single shared linear readout that maps each spatio-temporal token directly to its corresponding ($t \times p \times p$) block of alpha pixels, predicting spatial details and temporal variations within the same local video block. Tiling these blocks produces the full-resolution alpha sequence without a convolutional decoder or learned upsampling. The predicted alpha is supervised directly in pixel space, while the ground-truth alpha never enters the VAE, eliminating target-side alpha encoding and output-side VAE decoding. Leveraging the pretrained video prior, TokenMatte is trained using only existing public matting datasets, without additional data curation or auxiliary inputs at inference. Compared with a matched latent-output baseline, our pixel-native interface improves matting accuracy while achieving a 1.51$\times$ inference speedup. TokenMatte establishes a new auxiliary-free state of the art on VideoMatte, YouTubeMatte, and V-HIM60.

new Are We Rewarding the Wrong Module? Module-Specific Policy Optimization for Multimodal Reinforcement Learning

Authors: HuiSeong Cho, Jun Yeong Park, Yu Rang Park

Abstract: Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) based on Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) has been widely adopted to enhance the multimodal reasoning of multimodal large language models (MLLMs). However, existing methods share a common limitation of Module-Level Credit Entanglement, in which the vision encoder, projector, and LLM modules are jointly optimized using the same advantage signals even when perceptual correctness and final-answer correctness differ. To address this limitation, we propose Module-Specific Policy Optimization (MSPO), a framework that separates perceptual and outcome-based learning signals across model modules. Specifically, MSPO uses responses to perception checklists comprising auxiliary questions about visual information to derive an advantage for training the vision encoder. From these responses, MSPO computes the Confidence-aware Perception Reward (CaPR), using a perception sign obtained by thresholding checklist accuracy to determine reward direction and posterior confidence from a Beta distribution over latent perception accuracy to scale reward magnitude. We then derive the vision advantage through group-relative normalization of CaPR and scale it using main-task success rates. This advantage guides updates to the vision encoder, while the projector and LLM retain outcome-based GRPO updates. Extensive experiments on seven mathematical and general reasoning benchmarks demonstrate that MSPO outperforms existing RL-tuned models on average at both the 3B and 7B scales.

new RestPose: Canonicalization for Open Object Pose Estimation

Authors: Sandeep Inuganti, Weihang Li, Benjamin Busam, Didier Stricker, Jason Rambach

Abstract: In object pose estimation methods, predicted poses are expressed with respect to a canonical frame, defined by a reference model of that object. In learning-based methods, an arbitrarily chosen modeling convention defines this canonical frame, which is then used to generate the ground-truth pose-annotations for training. Object pose estimation methods are therefore bound to such arbitrary conventions, restricting generalization. In this work, we propose that such a canonical frame can instead be derived for any object, and present RestPose, an approach towards open object pose estimation that does not require reference models or canonical frame conventions during training. From a single input image, we generate an object mesh and canonicalize it through a geometric and physical analysis of the object, then use foundation model-based dense matching to estimate the pose. We choose category-level benchmarks for our experiments, which contain instances across various object categories and enable us to measure the effectiveness of our canonicalization. Across HouseCat6D, NOCS, and Omni6DPose datasets, we improve on the supervised state-of-the-art methods by 18.3 points in absolute metrics and 13.8 points in scale-agnostic metrics, without requiring task-specific training. Code will be published.

new Knocked Off Course: Tracing Uncommanded Motion from Perception to Action in Embodied VLAs

Authors: Dan Zlotnikov, Nadav Alali, Ohad Ben-Shahar

Abstract: Vision-Language-Action models (VLAs) enable simultaneous processing of egocentric video and textual target descriptions to produce action commands for embodied tracking platforms. However, these platforms rarely move exactly as commanded, in part due to unexpected environmental disturbances such as wind, unleveled terrain, and road bumps. Here we study how such models handle uncommanded egomotion. First, we explore performance across diverse disturbances by benchmarking three tracking VLAs on three datasets. We find that models differ in which environmental disturbances hurt them most, but all are sensitive to sudden target loss. Tracing egomotion from pixels to emitted actions, we identify two gaps. In representation, motion is reliably recovered through explicit relations between frames, but aggressive compression of past frames weakens small-motion readouts. In action, large rotations do reach the decision state, yet the decoded direction carries only a few percent of a mirrored rotation's effect on the turn. Targeting both gaps, we fine-tune one model, Uni-NaVid, on data amounting to just 1.7% of its original training set in a ladder of analysis-motivated variants. These gain 5-22 points after target-loss without degrading clean success; after a smooth loss, the best one improves success by 72% (relative), recovering 76% of its gap to clean. We also show that targeted fine-tuning improves recovery partly via a text-mediated action dependency, without new architecture, large-scale data collection or training from scratch.

new SplatSeg3D: Efficient Zero-Shot 3D Segmentation via Point Cloud Surfelization and Splatting

Authors: Sandeep Inuganti, Mahdi Chamseddine, Marcel Rogge, Martin R. Oswald, Didier Stricker, Jason Rambach

Abstract: Open-vocabulary 3D segmentation methods rely on 2D foundation models applied to registered images captured alongside the 3D point clouds. However, such images may not be available in many cases, while direct 3D point cloud segmentation methods require semantic class supervision and are challenging to deploy in a zero-shot manner. To address these issues, we present SplatSeg3D, which segments a bare colored point cloud in an open-vocabulary manner without requiring the accompanying images, camera poses, class supervision, or instance mask proposals. To achieve this, we first surfelize the input point cloud and obtain oriented 2D Gaussians. We then render views of the scene by splatting, with a view selection strategy that maximizes scene coverage with as few views as possible. Next, we apply text-prompted segmentation on the rendered views, giving each 3D point a set of multi-view semantic cues, fused to produce a 3D segmentation of the scene. We evaluate SplatSeg3D on semantic segmentation and novel-class instance segmentation on 4 standard benchmarks: Scannet, Scannet++, Replica, and S3DIS. Our pipeline not only performs better or on-par with the state-of-the-art, but we also achieve these results efficiently across runtime and compute requirements. Code will be published.

new Diving Deeper into Dense Keypoint Localization in Object Pose Estimation

Authors: Jiahong Chen, Jinghao Wang, Yueneng Yang, Banglei Guan, Zi Wang, Dewen Hu, Qifeng Yu

Abstract: 6D pose estimation of rigid objects is a fundamental and challenging task in computer vision. Recent dense semantic keypoint-based methods employ binary encoding for efficient global keypoint localization, delivering strong performance in object pose estimation. Nevertheless, the discontinuous nature of binary encoding hinders effective training at high resolutions, while its inherent discrete and quantization errors lead to significant localization uncertainty. We propose GHLAPose, a global-to-local framework that synergizes hybrid Gray-binary (HGB) encoding with local-aware refinement (LAR). Specifically, HGB encoding first alleviates discontinuity to achieve accurate initial global keypoint localization. To further address intrinsic encoding errors, the LAR module estimates uncertainty and refines keypoint localization. This synergy enables a precise global-to-local strategy by aggregating global and local features. Extensive experiments on LM, LM-O, YCB-V, and TUD-L datasets demonstrate that GHLAPose surpasses state-of-the-art methods, particularly on fine-grained metrics. On LM-O, it outperforms the second-best method by 1.4% and 1.9% on ADD(-S) 0.02d and 0.05d respectively, and on YCB-V by 1% on ADD(-S) 0.1d. The code will be available at GHLAPose.

new AnyPrune: Optimization-Free Pruning for Any Feedforward 3D Gaussian Splatting Pipeline

Authors: Nicola Leonetti, Federico Stella, Aoxiang Fan, Saqib Javed, Pascal Fua, Luigi Di Stefano

Abstract: Feedforward 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) pipelines reconstruct a scene from images in a single forward pass, representing it as real-time-renderable 3D Gaussians. However, the resulting representations contain far more Gaussians than are needed to render the scene faithfully, especially when there are many input views. This drastically increases memory and storage requirements. Existing pruning methods either require costly re-optimization, which defeats the purpose of a single forward pass, or rely on simple strategies that do not exploit all the available information. We present AnyPrune, a transformer-based, optimization-free pruning module designed to work on top of any feedforward 3DGS pipeline. To remove unnecessary Gaussians, AnyPrune learns and exploits pipeline-specific redundancy patterns through a short single-GPU training on the frozen target feedforward pipeline. AnyPrune achieves better rendering quality than existing optimization-free pruning methods in most settings and generalizes to unseen datasets, as shown through extensive experiments across multiple feedforward pipelines, datasets, and input view counts. It prunes a 1.6M-Gaussian field in about $5\,\mathrm{s}$ on a consumer GPU, faster than every method that merges Gaussians or re-optimizes the field. It can also be combined with the pruning built into feedforward pipelines, pruning further at moderate ratios while preserving or improving rendering quality. Finally, it can even suggest a scene-specific pruning ratio that retains most of the quality.

new Qwen-Image-HD: Autoregressive Diffusion for High Definition Image Generation

Authors: Zihao Liu, Zekai Zhang, Shengming Yin, Zikai Zhou, Donghao Zhou, Chenyang Zhu, Lihan Jiang, Ningyuan Tang, Bingxuan Zhao, Yanran Zhang, Yan Shu, Zhening Liu, Jiahao Li, Jie Zhang, Kaiyuan Gao, Kun Yan, Qihang Ai, Rui Song, Shuo Cao, Xiao Xu, Xiaoyue Chen, Yuxiang Chen, Zhendong Wang, Zhouyang Li, Bo Zheng, Jun Zhou, Xiu Li, Chenfei Wu

Abstract: Fine-tuning a generator on a complete high-resolution canvas requires long image sequences and substantial computation, yet local regions can still exhibit unnatural repetitive stripes when training resources are limited. We introduce Qwen-Image-HD, which adapts Qwen-Image 2.0 to high-resolution synthesis through local training and blockwise autoregressive generation. Each prediction covers a target tile at 1K scale, conditioned on high-resolution upper and left neighbors and a compressed historical thumbnail. These conditions provide adjacent fine-scale structures and an overview of the scene generated so far. To integrate these complementary cues, we map both forms of history and the target to a shared canvas coordinate system to express their spatial relationships and scale differences. On Qwen-Image-Bench at 4K, Qwen-Image-HD achieves stronger overall evaluation performance than direct 4K generation with FLUX-3-Image-Klein. Compared with full-image 4K supervised fine-tuning of the same backbone, it achieves better generation quality with lower training compute and higher inference efficiency, while exhibiting fewer unnatural repetitive stripes in original-resolution comparisons. We further introduce a Wavefront-AutoRegressive inference schedule that generates tiles on the same anti-diagonal concurrently across GPUs to reduce single-image latency.

new A Morphological Approach to Inverse Lithography

Authors: Gijs Bellaard, Bart M. N. Smets, Tim van der Leek, Alexandru Onose, Remco Duits

Abstract: Lithography is a critical step in the manufacturing of chips that works by projecting light through a patterned mask and subsequently focusing it onto a photosensitive layer on top of a wafer. The task of finding a mask that prints a desired pattern on the wafer is called inverse lithography technology (ILT). The design of the mask is complicated by the fact that it must satisfy a set of constraints in order to be manufacturable. In this paper we provide a mathematical characterization of these constraints. Next, we develop a scoring function that allows us to measure whether a given mask satisfies the manufacturing constraints and if not, how badly the constraints are violated. Since our scoring function is (sub-)differentiable it can be used as the regularizer in a gradient-based inverse problem solver. We show that the resulting MorphILT algorithm that we developed can generate manufacturable masks that have nearly the same print quality as the (unmanufacturable) masks obtained from unconstrained inverse lithography.

new MemAnchorWorld: A Sparse World Model with Horizon-Conditioned Memory Retrieval for Autonomous Driving

Authors: Xinyang Jia, Yuqi Zhang, Jiakai Wang, Yahu Yang, Guandong Pan, Chao Tong, Xianglong Liu

Abstract: Sparse world models for 4D semantic occupancy forecasting represent scenes with a small set of evolving queries to efficiently predict future geometry and semantics for autonomous driving. However, existing sparse world models face a bottleneck in using historical temporal evidence for long-horizon forecasting. They rely on scene states to carry historical information implicitly, allowing errors to propagate and accumulate across successive forecasting steps and causing geometric and semantic deviations. To address this limitation, we present MemAnchorWorld, a sparse occupancy world model that explicitly retrieves relevant historical geometric and semantic evidence for 4D semantic occupancy forecasting. Inspired by the von Restorff effect, we introduce a forecast-time-conditioned hierarchical dual-path memory-anchor retrieval mechanism. Guided by the effective age and semantic compatibility of historical information, it combines query-level candidate selection with point-level memory-anchor retrieval to provide historical evidence for sparse points at different forecast times. To better use memory anchors and existing features, a point-level residual adapter jointly gated by the task and forecast time separately controls residual corrections to semantics, occupancy confidence, and spatial positions. On Occ3D-nuScenes, MemAnchorWorld achieves an average mIoU of 13.55% and an average IoU of 22.84% across the 1-3 s forecasting horizons, attaining state-of-the-art performance on both metrics.Project page: https://memanchorworld.github.io/

new Beyond Static Semantics: Instance-Adaptive Vision-Language Alignment for Building Damage Assessment

Authors: Hang Yin, Jiepan Li, Hongruixuan Chen, Guangyi Yang, Wei He

Abstract: Instance-level building damage assessment (IBDA) from bi-temporal remote sensing imagery remains challenging, as the same damage grade may exhibit diverse visual manifestations across individual buildings. Natural language offers explicit descriptions of physical damage, providing complementary semantic cues beyond discrete category labels. However, existing vision--language approaches have not fully addressed how damage semantics should adapt to local bi-temporal evidence and subsequently serve as instance-specific references for severity discrimination. To bridge this gap, we propose LAMIA-IBDA, a unified vision--language framework that progressively grounds shared damage semantics in local observations and individual buildings. Specifically, Adaptive Text-Visual Alignment (ATVA) enables evidence-adaptive semantic interaction by jointly contextualizing multiple damage descriptions and bi-temporal visual features under reliability-aware attention supervision. Dynamic Instance-Prototype Grounding (DIPG) further associates these contextualized semantics with individual building regions to construct instance-adaptive prototypes for fine-grained damage classification. Instance-level description preferences additionally supervise fusion-stage attention, linking semantic adaptation with instance discrimination. Extensive experiments on xBD and BRIGHT demonstrate state-of-the-art performance across optical--optical and optical--SAR settings. The source code is publicly available https://github.com/Cutensil/LAMIA-IBDA.

new Back to Raw Motion: Latent-Free Diffusion Transformers for Text-to-Motion and Exact Conditional Generation

Authors: Yun Chen, Jeonghyeok Do, Munchurl Kim

Abstract: As generative models tackle larger and more complex datasets, learned codecs enable efficient generation through compact latent representations, but may lose details and cannot guarantee exact preservation of given conditions. Generating directly in the original data space avoids errors introduced by the codec. Recent advances in image generation demonstrate that pixel diffusion can produce high-quality samples at practical computational costs. Inspired by these advances, we conduct a preliminary study of pixel diffusion, demonstrating its potential for adaptation to motion generation. Building on these findings, we present RawMo, a framework that brings text-to-motion generation to raw motion space. Our Motion Diffusion Transformer (MiT) models local skeletal structures and whole-body dynamics, while refining motion across frames to improve temporal consistency. Working directly in raw motion space further allows our high-noise text-motion alignment loss (HiNCE) to improve text consistency without intermediate motion decoding. RawMo supports multiple motion-conditioning tasks with a single conditional model while preserving given motion conditions exactly. Experiments on HumanML3D and KIT-ML show the best text alignment among compared methods and competitive motion quality, with ablations confirming the benefits of MiT and HiNCE.

new SpatialArena: Where Do Geometric Foundation Models Stand?

Authors: Yu Chen, Haosen Yang, Su Ee Tan, Zhensong Zhang, Jifei Song, Jiankang Deng

Abstract: 3D perception is shifting from task-specific pipelines to geometric foundation models: monocular depth networks, feed-forward 3D/4D reconstruction models, and vision--language models that predict geometry. However, their progress cannot be measured reliably. Published numbers are not comparable, because every paper samples frames, sets resolutions, and selects test splits differently. They are not robust because the alignment required for every up-to-scale prediction can outweigh the gap between models: fitting depth in the wrong space nearly triples its error, and standard trajectory alignment understates the error of failing methods by up to $2.6\times$. And they rarely cover long recordings, dynamic scenes, or aerial views. We present SpatialArena, an open arena that scores geometric models with the same metrics and rules, with robust and deterministic alignment. SpatialArena spans five tasks, from monocular depth to SLAM. It covers 23 public datasets with 418,929 images across indoor, outdoor, aerial, object-centric, and dynamic scenes, together with Spatial Arena, our own captures with accurate poses and depth. Evaluating 52 models, we find that single-image depth is close to saturation on public benchmarks but not on large, uncontrolled scenes, and that progress now comes from consistency across frames and views, where feed-forward 3D/4D models match or surpass monocular specialists and overtake video depth models. The benchmark, toolbox, and leaderboard will be publicly released.

new Beyond Image-Text Pairs: Aligning Semantic Changes for Fine-Grained Visual Understanding

Authors: Yujie Sun, Zeqing Wang, Shuang Liang, Zhenlin Zhao, Kunyu Zhang, Xiaowu Sun

Abstract: Image-text alignment serves as a foundation for modern vision-language models (VLMs), yet prevailing training paradigms provide limited supervision for learning how semantic changes correspond across modalities. To address this limitation, we propose a post-training method that learns cross-modal semantic change correspondences through controlled visual and textual variations. Specifically, we introduce a Semantic Difference Alignment (SDA) loss which can align the directions of corresponding visual and textual representation changes. We combine our proposed SDA with pairwise sigmoid matching to jointly learn image-text correspondences and cross-modal semantic changes, encouraging more fine-grained and discriminative visual representations. To support this learning, we construct VarFamily-40K, a dataset of image-text pairs with controlled cross-modal semantic variations. Experiments demonstrate consistent improvements in compositional understanding and downstream classification performance, alongside further gains on multimodal tasks when the adapted visual encoder is integrated into VLMs.

new PhysMend: Verification-Guided Program Refinement for Physics-Aware Video Generation

Authors: Meilong Xu, Ziyi Zhao, Bhagyashree Puranik, Shayda Moezzi, Yixiao Zhang, Kushan Thakkar

Abstract: Video generative models synthesize realistic scenes, yet their interactions often violate physics. A ball dropped onto sand should displace grains and settle into a crater, whereas a generated video may show it passing through the surface or leaving the sand flat. Post-hoc methods typically score generated videos with a vision-language model (VLM), but such scores offer limited guidance on which object or parameter to modify, and each correction costs another synthesis. Simulation-based pipelines instead run fixed stages before synthesis, so simulation errors reach the video. To address these limitations, we present PhysMend, a training-free framework for verification-guided program refinement. PhysMend describes the target event as an editable physical program of grounded objects, events, and required relations, paired with tunable execution parameters. Our key insight is to verify and repair the event on its simulated rollout and on the depth video that conditions the generator (depth control) rather than on generated videos, enabling quantitative violation measurement, targeted editing, and fast re-verification before synthesis. A hybrid verifier evaluates semantic event consistency, physical plausibility, solver stability, and visibility in the depth control. VLM judgments are confined to event semantics and cannot override failed physical measurements, which limits the impact of VLM hallucinations. Each failed check localizes the program fields eligible for repair, and its measured violation scales a bounded update of their parameters. Only accepted depth control reaches the generator, which runs once per input. Experiments on the commonly used benchmarks Physics-IQ, PhyGenBench, and VBench-2.0 demonstrate substantial gains across different generator backbones; for example, PhysMend raises the overall Physics-IQ score of Wan-VACE from 27.33 to 39.40.

new Reliable and grounded reporting of any computed tomography scan with FORA

Authors: Sezgin Er, Utku Turkbey, Alperen Gur, Omer Faruk Durugol, Suprosanna Shit, Murong Xu, Julia Wolleb, Jiayi Wang, Xiaoliang Wang, Hadrien Reynaud, Teodora Spasojevic, Dong Yang, Andriy Myronenko, Pengfei Guo, Yufan He, Can Zhao, Marc Edgar, Andreas Maier, Michael Moor, Mehmet Kemal Ozdemir, Daguang Xu, Bernhard Kainz, Bjoern Menze, Ibrahim Ethem Hamamci

Abstract: Artificial intelligence systems for computed tomography (CT) remain largely restricted to specific anatomical regions, examination types or predefined abnormalities, limiting their ability to provide comprehensive and spatially grounded interpretations. Here we present FORA, a general-purpose CT model that recognizes and spatially localizes 1,028 hierarchically organized radiological concepts and generates reports across the 28 CT protocols represented in our cohort. Trained on 304,962 routine CT examinations supervised by their accompanying reports, FORA learns to localize abnormalities without manually annotated lesions. Its architecture combines multiple-instance learning with an interpretable concept bottleneck that connects spatial evidence, radiological findings and report generation. We first developed and benchmarked the architecture on chest radiographs, achieving state-of-the-art performance, before training a separate model on three-dimensional CT examinations. On 36,619 held-out CT examinations, FORA achieved a macro area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUROC) of 0.918 for radiological concept recognition, exceeding the strongest of 18 existing CT models by 0.21. FORA also ranked first on three public benchmarks, outperforming Merlin on its own test set without training on that institution's data (AUROC 0.907 versus 0.826). On independent localization benchmarks for pulmonary emboli, renal masses and rib fractures, FORA's strongest spatial evidence fell within annotated lesions at rates up to 348 times greater than chance. FORA achieved more than threefold higher macro-averaged finding recall than the strongest existing report generator. These results show that a single model can combine broad abnormality recognition, spatial localization and report generation across 12 CT examination types, using routine reports without lesion-level supervision.

new EEG-World: Context-Conditioned Dynamics of the Watching Brain

Authors: Junxiang Zhang, Jiayu Zuo, Wenhao Ye, Junfeng Xia, Xiang Chen, Mo Wang, Quanying Liu

Abstract: Brain encoding models predict neural responses from audiovisual input, but typically overlook how ongoing brain activity shapes an individual's response. We introduce EEG-World, a recurrent world model that forecasts 10 seconds of EEG band-power dynamics from 6 seconds of preceding EEG and the subsequent audiovisual stimulus. Pretrained on continuous EEG, EEG-World learns shared neural dynamics and adapts to unseen individuals through lightweight subject-specific readouts. Using 2,584 hours of EEG from 78 participants, we show that nonlinear recurrent forecasting surpasses strong history-aware linear baselines at scale. EEG-World achieves a forecasting correlation of 0.261 versus 0.233 for the strongest history-and-video linear model, with a 3.3-second forecasting-horizon advantage. Its learned dynamics transfer to unseen participants, outperforming the linear baseline in 23 of 24 individuals after calibration. While ongoing EEG history accounts for most predictive performance, controlled stimulus analyses reveal an additional video-dependent component. This component also enables evaluation of visual representations, with V-JEPA 2 improving clip-locked prediction by 35% over combined CLIP and DINOv2 features. Our results demonstrate the value of transferable neural dynamics for individual EEG forecasting and highlight the importance of separating state-driven from stimulus-driven predictability.

new SAVERA: Bridging Frozen Vision and Language Models via Sparse Alignment and Visual Evidence Reuse

Authors: Jiajun Shen, Chuancheng Shi, Jiayi Lyu, Yanpeng Sun, Fei Shen, Tat-Seng Chua

Abstract: Independently pretrained vision and language models may learn shared structures of the same underlying world, providing a foundation for combining them to build multimodal capabilities. However, a key challenge remains in aligning cross-modal feature representations while preserving access to visual information before and after language adaptation. To this end, we propose sparse alignment with visual evidence-guided residual adaptation (SAVERA), a framework that combines frozen vision and language models through bidirectional alignment in a shared sparse space and prompt-guided residual fusion. Specifically, we first present shared-space projection, activation reconstruction and encoding (SPARE), which represents original visual, adapted visual, and textual features through shared soft-gated sparse encoding, with branch-specific reconstruction to preserve their respective information. Second, we devise bridge--text embedding and activation matching (BEAM), a bidirectional cross-modal alignment module that aligns adapted visual and textual features at both the sample and neuron levels, establishing fine-grained cross-modal correspondence. Finally, we introduce prompt-conditioned residual integration of vision and adapted features (PRIVA), a prompt-guided residual fusion module that models vision--language interactions through gating and attention and integrates complementary information from original and adapted representations through residual fusion. Extensive experiments show that SAVERA achieves competitive performance on image captioning and visual question answering while reducing object hallucination.

new Aqua2CAD : Observation-Guided Point Cloud Correction and Completion for CAD Reconstruction from Underwater Stereo Images

Authors: Weizhao Huang, Jiayi Zhang, Pangkit Fong, Chongrong Fang, Jianping He

Abstract: Recovering editable computer-aided design (CAD) models from underwater stereo images requires accurate and complete geometry, yet stereo reconstruction produces displaced points and missing regions. The original views retain cross-view disagreement and ray evidence lost during point-cloud fusion. Aqua2CAD exploits this evidence in two stages: view-conditioned correction and quality filtering establish reliable geometric support, while multi-view observations condition a learned shape prior to infer missing surfaces in the original metric frame. The recovered points support surface fitting and solid assembly with manually revised face groups. On an OceanSim-based synthetic benchmark, Aqua2CAD reduces point-cloud Chamfer distance by 60% relative to the stereo reconstruction. new DOPER: Deep Object Pose Estimation Revisited

Authors: Hugo Hadfield, Alperen Degirmenci, Jonathan Tremblay

Abstract: We present DOPER, an RGB pose estimation framework that shares a visual backbone across object detection, segmentation, and keypoint prediction to recover the 6D pose of known objects through Perspective-n-Point. Starting from synthetic training, DOPER adapts to real observations using partial supervision while retaining synthetic keypoint labels. Calibrated multi-view observations provide geometric constraints for keypoint supervision. During inference, optional heatmap refinement fits a rigid pose directly to the predicted keypoint distributions, resolving ambiguous correspondences without depth input or a learned pose refiner. Starting from underspecified CAD models, DOPER progressively refines the object representation and pose estimator. Results on SPEED+ and SHIRT further demonstrate effective adaptation to illumination changes and accurate temporal pose estimation. new CityRecast: Suppressing Persistent Artifacts in Generative Satellite 3D Reconstruction

Authors: Shuo Cui, Yingyan Hou, Wanxuan Lu, Hongfeng Yu, Daobing Zhang, Xian Sun

Abstract: Satellite-based 3D reconstruction is limited by sparse observations and changes across acquisition dates. Existing methods use generative priors to recover missing visual detail, but ghosting, streaking, and blur persist in their Gaussian scenes. These artifacts become particularly apparent in low-elevation renderings. We introduce CityRecast to suppress these artifacts through Gaussian scene repair and supervision calibration. To correct the Gaussian distributions that produce these artifacts, we first use multi-angle warm-up to constrain their spatial extent. This reduces the initial ghosting carried into progressive refinement. Surface-guided replacement then continues to repair the Gaussian distribution while preserving existing appearance. To provide more reliable supervision for this process, observation calibration mitigates interference from multi-temporal observations. new MemHand: Memory-Enhanced World-Space Hand Motion Reconstruction from Egocentric Videos

Authors: Junyi Ma, Zhangshuo Qi, Erhang Zhang, Mingjie Li, Hesheng Wang

Abstract: Recovering the continuous world-space motion of both hands from egocentric video is essential for applications such as human-to-robot policy transfer and augmented reality. Achieving this goes beyond reconstructing the hands from each individual frame, as the context that a frame needs is often spread over time, especially around occlusions or when a hand moves out of sight. Recent works have explored how to exploit temporal context, yet they mostly draw it from fixed-length windows. Such windows cannot guarantee that every frame has the context required to reconstruct the hands in world space with accurate poses and metric scale. To address this limitation, we introduce MemHand, a memory-enhanced framework for world-space hand motion reconstruction. MemHand maintains two complementary forms of memory. The Short-Term Event Memory keeps the context of the well-observed hand around each occlusion or out-of-sight event and supplies it to frames with weak or missing visual evidence. In addition, since the metric scale holds over the whole recording, the Long-Term Scale Memory retains the most reliable observations across the video as scale anchors within a bounded state. Across benchmarks featuring severe occlusions and frequent out-of-sight intervals, MemHand achieves state-of-the-art accuracy and generalizes well to unseen domains. Benefiting from the synergy of both memories, it cuts W-MPJPE by 57\% on ARCTIC and 70\% on HOT3D relative to StableHand, delivering continuous and metrically accurate world-space hand poses and trajectories. We will release our code and models at the project page: https://irmvlab.github.io/memhand.github.io

new Simultaneously Boosting Efficiency and Capability for Linear Attention via Adaptive Channel Compression and Activation Purification

Authors: Senqi Guan, Xianchao Zhang, Moyan Tian, Linlin Zong, Wenxin Liang, Xinyue Liu

Abstract: Self-attention's quadratic complexity constitutes a core bottleneck for Vision Transformers (ViTs). Existing efficiency-oriented optimizations, including softmax-based and linear attention (LA) variants, generally compromise model representational capacity, leading to inevitable performance degradation. We propose a novel linear attention which alleviates this efficiency-capability dilemma. For the first time, we embed an Adaptive Channel Compression and Activation Purification (ACCAP) mechanism into the QKV projection of linear attention, which enables the model to retain high-dimensional feature information while performing computations with reduced channels. We further fuse a focused kernel function and a self-gating module into ACCAP to construct a lightweight linear attention module (ACCAP-LA). Our theoretical analysis shows that, compared to standard linear attention, ACCAP-LA reduces the attention module's parameter count by 79.17% and FLOPs by 77.5%. Extensive experiments demonstrate ACCAP-LA's effectiveness: when applied to ViT, it achieves 79.4% Top-1 accuracy on ImageNet-1K with only 5.8M parameters, and more notably, it boasts strong cross-architecture compatibility, boosting EfficientViT-b1 and PVT-T by 3.3% and 3.4% respectively.

new 3D Tracking Any Point with Events and Frames

Authors: Roberto Pellerito, Zijia Dai, Laurent Kneip, Davide Scaramuzza

Abstract: Tracking a fast-moving point in 3D is prohibitive when RGB frames are blurred or too far apart to reveal its motion. Event cameras record brightness changes between frames, providing measurements inside these temporal gaps. We introduce TrackAnyMotion, which combines monocular RGB frames and events to track arbitrary points in 2D and 3D, including at times when no RGB frame is available. new Biomechanically Grounded Prediction of Selective Motor Control and Clinical Outcomes from Infant Video

Authors: Divya Joshi, Ruize Yang, Colleen Peyton, R. James Cotton

Abstract: Early identification of cerebral palsy (CP) relies on observational assessments of spontaneous infant movement, such as the General Movement Assessment (GMA) and the Baby Observational Selective Control AppRaisal (BabyOSCAR), which require trained raters and substantial scoring time. Existing automated approaches derive hand-engineered features from two-dimensional keypoints, which describe image-plane motion rather than the three-dimensional behavior of individual joints. Without being trained on diagnosis, the predicted total separated infants with CP from those without CP with an AUC of 0.86, exceeding a replicated two-dimensional feature classifier (0.67) but below the expert clinician rater's total (0.97). Predicted scores also carried information about functional ability and body distribution, though less than expert clinician scores.
