Extensive experiments on ScanRefer and Nr3D demonstrate that our framework, relying entirely on open-source models, significantly outperforms previous zero-shot methods and even surpasses fully supervised baselines.

replace Leveraging Gaze and Set-of-Mark in VLLMs for Human-Object Interaction Anticipation from Egocentric Videos

Authors: Daniele Materia, Francesco Ragusa, Giovanni Maria Farinella

Abstract: The ability to anticipate human-object interactions is highly desirable in an intelligent assistive system in order to guide users during daily life activities and understand their short and long-term goals. Creating systems with such capabilities requires to approach several complex challenges. This work addresses the problem of human-object interaction anticipation in Egocentric Vision using Vision Large Language Models (VLLMs). We tackle key limitations in existing approaches by improving visual grounding capabilities through Set-of-Mark prompting and understanding user intent via the trajectory formed by the user's most recent gaze fixations. To effectively capture the temporal dynamics immediately preceding the interaction, we further introduce a novel inverse exponential sampling strategy for input video frames. Experiments conducted on the egocentric dataset HD-EPIC demonstrate that our method surpasses state-of-the-art approaches for the considered task, showing its model-agnostic nature.

replace Pothen: Can Vision-Language Models Tell Where a Cultural Object Comes From?

Authors: Yuechen Jiang, Enze Zhang, Mohsinul Kabir, Chenying Lin, Ying Peng, Fengyuan Liu, Qianqian Xie, Stavroula Golfomitsou, Konstantinos Arvanitis, Sophia Ananiadou

Abstract: A cultural object is a trace of the people who made it, and knowing who made it, where, and when shapes how it is catalogued, valued, and returned. Vision-language models now describe such objects for museum collections, yet whether they can recover this knowledge from an image has not been tested. We introduce Pothen, a benchmark of 750 objects from the Metropolitan Museum of Art and the Getty in which a model predicts an object's title, culture, period, origin, and creator from its image alone, scored against curator-written catalogue records by an LLM judge calibrated on human annotation. Across nine models, mean field scores are 36.16% to 49.82%, but all five fields match for only 2.13% to 7.87% of objects. Images raise culture, period, and origin scores by 12.6 to 32.2 points for two models tested without them, and 83.52% of creator matches occur alongside an incorrect field. Models read visual evidence and hold facts about objects but do not bind them into one attribution, and in our error analysis their attributions follow the style an object resembles rather than the record of its making.

replace Free Geometry: Refining 3D Reconstruction from Stronger Versions of Itself

Authors: Yuhang Dai, Xingyi Yang

Abstract: Feed-forward 3D reconstruction models are efficient but rigid: once trained, they run zero-shot and cannot adapt to the test scene. As a result, plausible-looking reconstructions often contain errors under domain shifts, occlusions, specularities and ambiguous cues. To address this, we introduce Free Geometry, a framework that enables feed-forward 3D reconstruction models to self-evolve at test time without any 3D ground truth or offline retraining. Our key insight is that a model can become its own teacher at test time. When given more views, the model produces more geometrically consistent reconstructions. Aggregating multiple such predictions across different reference frames and view subsets further improves their accuracy and reliability, forming a stronger consensus teacher. Leveraging this property, given a test sequence, we mask different subsets of it to construct multiple partial observations. Free Geometry then adapts the model by enforcing camera pose and depth consistency between these partial-view predictions and the teacher's consensus. By updating only the normalization layers, this self-supervision enables fast scene-specific adaptation in about two minutes on a single GPU. Our approach improves five state-of-the-art foundation models on four datasets, yielding average relative gains of 8.8% in camera pose accuracy (AUC@3) and 3.9% in point map accuracy (inlier ratio). Project page: https://free-geometry.github.io/

URLs: https://free-geometry.github.io/

replace From Clinical Intent to Clinical Model: Autonomous Coding-Agents for Clinician-driven AI Development

Authors: Zihao Zhao, Eneko Cornejo Merodio, Wenya Zhao, Frederik Hauke, Wensa Peng, Xixi Song, Maike Bode, Juliana de Castilhos, Mathis Bode, Jakob Nikolas Kather, Sven Nebelung, Daniel Truhn

Abstract: BACKGROUND: Developing clinically useful AI requires collaboration between clinicians and AI developers, yet this process often involves time-consuming communication and potential misalignment. Coding agents may help bridge this gap. METHODS: We present a prototype that lets clinicians drive AI development directly via natural-language interactions. The system turns a clinician request into an executable workflow through semantic parsing, task initialization, and iterative code tuning, using Claude Opus 4.6 as the underlying coding agent. We evaluated this prototype across 12 tasks, including straightforward requests focused solely on model performance metrics and more complex requests. The test set was held out from the beginning and was never exposed to the coding agent. An exploratory human evaluation was conducted with six clinicians who completed a questionnaire rated on a five-point Likert scale after interacting with the system. RESULTS: Across 12 tasks, the prototype autonomously developed AI models with competitive performance. Notably, clinicians' practical knowledge helped reduce shortcut reliance (partial correlation from 0.53 to 0.07) in pneumothorax classification. Drain-free AUROC improved in every cohort and the clinician-driven model achieved stronger lesion localization than medical foundation models across all five external cohorts. All six participants commented communication as efficient and agreed that the agent understood their clinical goals and can provide useful suggestions, but also saying extensive external testing is needed before clinical use of autonomously developed AI models. CONCLUSIONS: These findings suggest that coding agents could let clinicians take a more independent and active role in clinical AI development, reducing their reliance on AI developers, although further improvements are needed.

replace SIEVES: Selective Prediction Generalizes through Visual Evidence Scoring

Authors: Hector G. Rodriguez, Marcus Rohrbach

Abstract: As the accuracy of Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) on traditional visual question answering benchmarks approaches saturation, reliable deployment requires satisfying low error tolerances in real-world out-of-distribution (OOD) scenarios. Selective prediction aims to improve coverage, i.e. the share of inputs the system answers, while adhering to a user-defined risk level. This is typically achieved by assigning a confidence score to each answer and abstaining on those that fall below a certain threshold. Existing selective prediction methods rely on text-only reasoning and learn to predict confidences simply based on whether the answer was correct or not. In contrast, we introduce SIEVES (Selective Prediction through Visual Evidence Scoring), which first requires the answering model to provide visual evidence supporting its answer, by returning crops of the image alongside their textual reasoning and answer. Second, a lightweight selector is trained to predict the quality of this evidence, assessing how well the provided crops localize the relevant visual elements and if the final answer is coherent with this evidence. We show that SIEVES improves coverage by up to six times on challenging OOD benchmarks (V* Bench, HR-Bench-8k, MME-RealWorld-Lite, VizWiz, and AdVQA), compared to non-grounding baselines. Beyond better generalization to OOD tasks, the design of the SIEVES selector enables transfer to proprietary reasoners without access to their weights or logits, such as o3 and Gemini-3-Pro, providing coverage boosts beyond those attributable to accuracy alone. We highlight that SIEVES generalizes across all tested OOD datasets and reasoner models, without benchmark- or reasoner-specific training or adaptation.

replace Text-to-CAD Evaluation with CADTests

Authors: Dimitrios Mallis, Marco Wang, Ahmet Serdar Karadeniz, Elisa Ricci, Anis Kacem, Djamila Aouada

Abstract: Text-to-CAD has recently emerged as an important task with the potential to substantially accelerate design workflows. Despite its significance, there has been surprisingly little work on Text-to-CAD evaluation, and assessing CAD model generation performance remains a considerable challenge. In this work, we introduce a new evaluation perspective for Text-to-CAD based on automated testing. We propose CADTestBench, the first test-based benchmark for Text-to-CAD, based on CADTests, executable software tests that verify whether a generated CAD model satisfies the geometric and topological requirements of the input prompt. Using CADTestBench, we conduct comprehensive benchmarking of recent Text-to-CAD methods and further demonstrate that CADTests can also guide CAD model generation, yielding simple baselines that surpass performance of current methods. CADTestBench code and data are available.

replace ProSG: Learning Progressive Structural Guidance for 3D Human Pose Estimation

Authors: Zehua Wang, Qiongjie Cui, Changwang Mei, Huaijiang Sun, Pengqi Hu

Abstract: For 3D human pose estimation from detected 2D joint locations, depth ambiguity remains a fundamental challenge. Even when two connected joints are accurately localized in the image, their relative depth along the camera axis can remain uncertain. Existing methods progressively refine pose representations through successive stages, with early stages emphasizing local 2D skeletal geometry and joint relationships and deeper stages developing increasingly depth-related representations. However, early-stage structural cues remain overlooked despite their importance for subsequent 3D inference. Our findings reveal that reusing such information provides complementary geometric guidance, particularly improving joint position estimation along the camera-space depth axis. We propose ProSG, a progressive structural guidance framework that preserves and aggregates intermediate pose representations from preceding stages to guide subsequent 3D pose refinement. Specifically, Structure Guided Accumulation (SGA) maintains a pool of these representations and adaptively retrieves relevant information for each joint and frame, enabling deeper stages to revisit early structural cues. Structural Context Reorganization (SCR) further reorganizes intermediate representations before they enter the representation pool, using compact structural contextual tokens to facilitate the effective integration of structural information across stages. Extensive experiments on Human3.6M and MPI-INF-3DHP demonstrate the effectiveness of ProSG, achieving state-of-the-art performance.

replace Can MLLMs Reason About Visual Persuasion? Evaluating the Efficacy and Faithfulness of Reasoning

Authors: Naeun Lee, Hyunjong Kim, Sunghwan Choi, Injin Kong, Yohan Jo

Abstract: Persuasive visuals play a central role in advertising, public communication, and online media, making it increasingly important to understand whether an image is persuasive and why. However, current Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have limited ability to reason about visual persuasion. Our analysis reveals that models often rely on a shortcut---selectively citing easily recognizable visual elements and treating their mere presence as evidence of persuasiveness---rather than reasoning over the diverse visual cues in an image to determine how and why they contribute to persuasiveness. To address this limitation, we propose (1) a fine-tuning approach that trains models on rationales reflecting diverse perspectives on visual persuasiveness, and (2) an evaluation framework that measures the faithfulness of models' rationales through three complementary metrics. Fine-tuning on multi-perspective rationales improves persuasiveness prediction and reasoning effectiveness. However, our evaluation framework reveals a discrepancy between prediction performance and rationale faithfulness, showing that higher prediction performance does not necessarily correspond to more faithful reasoning. These findings highlight the need to evaluate effectiveness and faithfulness separately and inform future directions for improving both the training and evaluation of visual persuasion reasoning.

replace Attention Sinks in Diffusion Transformers: A Causal Analysis

Authors: Fangzheng Wu, Brian Summa

Abstract: Attention sinks -- tokens that receive disproportionate attention mass -- are assumed to be functionally important in autoregressive language models, but their role in diffusion transformers remains unclear. We present a causal analysis in text-to-image diffusion, dynamically identifying dominant attention recipients per timestep and suppressing them via paired, training-free interventions on the score and value paths. Across 553 GenEval prompts on Stable Diffusion~3 (with SDXL corroboration), removing these sinks does not degrade text-image alignment (CLIP-T) or preference proxies (ImageReward, HPS-v2) at $k{=}1$; only under stronger interventions ($k\!\geq\!10$) does HPS-v2 exhibit a metric-dependent boundary, while CLIP-T remains robust throughout. The perceptual shifts induced by suppression are nonetheless \emph{sink-specific} -- $\sim\!6\times$ larger than equal-budget random masking -- revealing an empirical dissociation between trajectory-level perturbation and \emph{semantic alignment} in diffusion transformers. \footnote{Code available at https://github.com/wfz666/ICML26-attention-sink.}

URLs: https://github.com/wfz666/ICML26-attention-sink.

replace BARISTA: A Multi-Task Egocentric Benchmark for Compositional Visual Understanding

Authors: Patrick Knab, Orgest Xhelili, Inis Buzi, Drago Andres Guggiana Nilo, Mohd Saquib Khan, Lorenz Kolb, Manuel Scherzer, Kerem Yildirir, Christian Bartelt, Philipp Johannes Schubert

Abstract: Scene understanding is central to general physical intelligence, and video is a primary modality for capturing both state and temporal dynamics of a scene. Yet understanding physical processes remains difficult, as models must combine object localization, hand-object interactions, relational parsing, temporal reasoning, and step-level procedural inference. Existing benchmarks usually evaluate these capabilities separately, limiting diagnosis of why models fail on procedural tasks. We introduce BARISTA, a densely annotated egocentric dataset and benchmark of 185 real-world coffee-preparation videos covering fully automatic, portafilter-based, and capsule-based workflows. BARISTA provides verified per-frame scene graphs linking persistent object identities to masks, tracks, boxes, attributes, typed relations, hand-object interactions, activities, and process steps. From these graphs, we derive zero-shot language-based tasks spanning phrase grounding, hand-object interaction recognition, referring, activity recognition, relation extraction, and temporal visual question answering. Experiments reveal strong variation across task families and no consistently dominant model family, positioning BARISTA as a challenging diagnostic benchmark for procedural video understanding. Code and dataset available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/ramblr/BARISTA.

URLs: https://huggingface.co/datasets/ramblr/BARISTA.

replace TANGO: Training-free Controllable Human Motion Generation with Progressive Token Guidance

Authors: Nhat Le, Daochang Liu, Baoru Huang, Anh Nguyen, Ajmal Mian

Abstract: We present TANGO, a progressive token guidance framework for training-free controllable human motion generation. Unlike control approaches that primarily operate at a fixed temporal resolution, TANGO leverages a multi-scale hierarchy of discrete motion tokens to establish a structured control process, progressively guiding global motion structure before refining local details. At each scale, the differentiable control objective guides all current-scale token distributions, and the sampled tokens condition subsequent finer-scale predictions. To address the challenge of discrete categorical sampling, we adopt a first-order approach following the Bayesian principle, adjusting token sampling distributions with a single first-order update per scale. To further improve guidance consistency, we introduce Within-Scale Interdependency (WSI) guidance to account for how token choices jointly affect the control objective, and Cross-Scale Consistency (CSC) guidance to estimate how remaining finer scales may affect the final control outcome. Together, these complementary guidance mechanisms enable consistent and effective progressive control without additional control-specific training. Experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of TANGO across different control objectives in terms of motion quality, control accuracy, and generalization to unseen constraints.

replace CAB: Accelerating Flow and Diffusion Sampling via Rectification and Corrected Adams-Bashforth

Authors: Anuska Roy, Pravin Nair

Abstract: Flow and diffusion models achieve high-fidelity, high-resolution image synthesis, but often require many function evaluations (NFEs) at sampling time. Existing acceleration methods either require additional training through distillation or rely on training-free high-order solvers, and both can degrade sample quality at low NFE budgets. We propose CAB (Corrected Adams-Bashforth), a training-free sampler that accelerates both flow and diffusion models. CAB first transforms the sampling dynamics to a common rectified coordinate system, and then applies a multistep Adams-Bashforth predictor augmented with a simple correction term based on past velocity evaluations and therefore incurs no additional NFEs. The resulting method is simple, has the same algorithmic form across model classes, and has at least third-order local truncation error and second-order global error. Experiments on pretrained flow and diffusion models, including class-conditional and large-scale text-to-image benchmarks, show that CAB improves quality-NFE trade-offs in the low-step regime of 6-20 NFEs. It also remains competitive with strong training-free samplers at higher step counts across most tested models. The official implementation is available at https://github.com/Anuska-Roy/CAB.

URLs: https://github.com/Anuska-Roy/CAB.

replace OP2GS: Feature-Free Gaussian Splatting with Dual Opacity for 3D Instance Segmentation

Authors: Guiyu Liu, Niklas Vaara, Janne Mustaniemi, Juho Kannala, Janne Heikkil\"a

Abstract: 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) provides an explicit and efficient scene representation, but its primitives do not naturally expose object-level identity. Existing methods either learn contrastive per-Gaussian features that need post-hoc clustering, or supervise identity encodings with view-consistent 2D masks. We propose OP2GS, a lightweight object-aware Gaussian representation that augments each primitive with an explicit instance label and a dedicated instance opacity $\sigma^{*}$, which serves as an instance occupancy field for object-mask rendering. This dual-opacity formulation decouples visual appearance from instance occupancy, so that $\sigma^{*}$ can be learned and the instance labels can be corrected without touching the appearance parameters $\sigma$ and $c$. We learn $\sigma^{*}$ with an instance occupancy loss that follows the standard transmittance-based visibility of 3DGS. A discrete regrouping step then corrects the instance labels using multi-view mask consensus. Since no high-dimensional features are required, OP2GS can be plugged into existing 3DGS training pipelines and applied unchanged to frozen 3DGS assets. On the ScanNet++ validation set with the official class-agnostic protocol, OP2GS improves over OpenSplat3D, the strongest comparable baseline, with both SAM and EntitySeg masks; it is also best on Messy Rooms and, under the Gaga protocol, on Replica, while storing only one scalar and one integer per Gaussian. Code will be released.

replace Distributed Image Compression with Multimodal Side Information at Extremely Low Bitrates

Authors: Guojun Xu, Mingyang Zhang, Jianwen Xiang, Cheng Tan, Yanchao Yang, Junwei Zhou

Abstract: Distributed Image Compression (DIC) is crucial for multi-view transmission, especially when operating at extremely low bitrates (< 0.1 bpp). Its core challenge is effectively utilizing side information to achieve high-quality reconstruction under strict bitrate budgets. However, existing DIC approaches struggle to exploit global context and object-level details from side information, leading to local blurring and the loss of fine details in the reconstruction. To address these limitations, we propose a Multimodal DIC framework (MDIC), which, for the first time, leverages side information in a multimodal manner into the DIC paradigm, effectively preserving fine-grained local details and enhancing global perceptual quality in reconstructed images. Specifically, we introduce a text-to-image diffusion-based decoder conditioned on textual side information extracted from correlated images to capture shared global semantics. Moreover, we design a feature-mask generator, supervised by a multimodal fine-grained alignment task, to strengthen the exploitation of visual side information. The generated mask serves two purposes: first, it guides the extraction of fine-grained details from losslessly transmitted side information to preserve the semantic consistency of reconstructed details; second, it regulates the extraction of clustered feature representations from the quantized VQ-VAE embeddings, compensating for category information lost under the extreme compression of the primary image. Extensive experiments on the widely used KITTI Stereo and Cityscapes datasets demonstrate that MDIC achieves state-of-the-art perceptual quality at extremely low bitrates.

replace How Far Has AI Come in Liver Fibrosis Staging? A Real-World Multi-Center Dataset and Benchmark

Authors: Yuanye Liu, Nannan Shi, Zhejia Zhang, Hanxiao Zhang, Boya Wang, Derong Yu, Nao Wang, Yuxin Jin, Yang Zhou, Kunhao Yuan, Siqi Wang, Lida Yang, Xu Qiao, Wentao Liu, Xuelei He, Xin Hong, Guoyan Zheng, Xin Chen, Guang-Zhong Yang, Le Zhang, Lei Li, Yuxin Shi, Xiahai Zhuang

Abstract: Despite years of methodological progress, how far AI has come in liver fibrosis staging has never been systematically evaluated under the heterogeneous, multi-center conditions that define clinical practice. To address this gap, we introduce LiFS, a multi-center dataset and benchmark derived from the MICCAI 2025 CARE-Liver challenge, comprising 610 patients across multiple centers and scanners with multi-sequence. To the best of our knowledge, LiFS is the first benchmark providing complete gadoxetic acid-enhanced sequences with histopathology-confirmed annotations from diverse real-world scanners. Through systematic evaluation of nine independently developed LiFS methods under a common CARE 2025 setting, and comparison with in-cohort radiologist, we assess how far current AI has progressed toward clinically meaningful liver fibrosis staging from three complementary perspectives. First, against radiologists, the best AI methods approached the performance of the senior radiologist in selected settings and exceeded the junior radiologist on selected endpoints while median AI performance generally approached junior-radiologist levels. Second, from a data perspective, cross-center heterogeneity, label imbalance, and contrast-enhanced sequence variability emerge as the dominant challenges for AI methods. In particular, the hepatobiliary phase offers valuable functional information but also introduces cross-center variability, creating a practical dilemma in its use. Third, from a technical perspective, controlled experiments quantify the effects of several factors, providing practical evidence for model design in liver fibrosis staging. Overall, LiFS provides a rigorous real-world benchmark for positioning the current state of AI in liver fibrosis staging and for enabling future research on the key challenges that limit clinically reliable deployment.

replace Pinpoint: Grounded Worldwide Image Geolocation via Cross-Source Retrieval and Reranking

Authors: Nika Chuzhoy, Brian Hu, Amit A. Arora, Jae Ro, Sarthak S. Sahu

Abstract: Image geolocation aims to estimate where a photograph was taken from its visual content. At worldwide scale, this remains challenging because visual evidence is often ambiguous, diverse, and unevenly distributed. Prior work has typically treated geolocation of ordinary internet photos and street-view imagery as separate tasks, despite their complementary strengths: internet photos better match the appearance distribution of user-captured queries, while street-view imagery provides denser, geographically grounded coverage. We present Pinpoint, a retrieve-and-rerank architecture that combines both sources in a coarse-to-fine pipeline. A contrastive image-GPS embedder is trained on both user-uploaded Flickr photos and street-view imagery, learning a shared image-GPS embedding space that is used to retrieve candidate locations. An attention-based reranker then rescores retrieved candidates by combining candidate-level visual and GPS features with cross-source evidence from nearby locations to ground the prediction. Unlike recent prior work, Pinpoint does not rely on multimodal large-language models, making inference faster and more reproducible. Pinpoint achieves state-of-the-art results across all metrics on standard benchmarks for internet photos (IM2GPS3k and YFCC4k) and street-view imagery (OSV-5M).

replace Attention Alignment Between Humans and Vision-Language Models

Authors: Isaac R. Christian, Udith Haputhanthri, Declan Campbell, Samuel Nastase, Taylor Webb, Michael Graziano

Abstract: Visual perception depends on top-down goals and bottom-up sensory mechanisms. Vision-language models (VLMs) implement both, allowing us to treat each component as a separable hypothesis about what drives where we look. Following that logic, we trained four VLMs, crossing CNN and ViT encoders with LSTM and Transformer decoders in a 2$\times$2 factorial design. Our four models shared a training distribution, a vocabulary, a supervision signal, and every hyperparameter; only the encoder and decoder families differed. We extracted each model's attention weights on 200 images, for two separate tasks (social captioning and general description), and compared those weights to fixations from 49 participants performing the same tasks. We found that both decoder and encoder architecture shaped alignment, but decoder choice dominated. LSTM decoders reached 81-89% of the human upperbound and Transformer decoders 47-74%, with decoder architecture explaining nearly five times more variance in alignment than encoder architecture ($\eta^2 = 0.43$ vs. $0.09$). Despite their alignment advantage, LSTM-decoder attention maps were spatially diffuse and minimally task-differentiated; Transformer decoders, in contrast, were weaker in alignment yet showed sharper spatial concentration and stronger task differentiation. Attention weights also predicted fMRI signals from the Natural Scenes Dataset. Somewhat surprisingly, attention weights predicted early visual, ventral, and lateral cortex better than parietal cortex, a region often associated with the control of attention. And, unlike behavioral results in which decoder choice was most influential, encoder type contributed most to neural alignment, with the CNN driving the effect across decoder types. This dissociation suggests bottom-up and top-down VLM components make separable contributions to behavioral and neural attention alignment.

replace Supervising Recurrent Vision Transformers from Bottlenecked Context

Authors: Philippe Weinzaepfel, Christian Wolf, Mert B\"ulent Sariyildiz, Guillaume Bono, Gianluca Monaci

Abstract: Transformers are AI's workhorse but their computational cost becomes prohibitive when processing long sequences. We target long-horizon streaming vision and robotics applications, where it is particularly impractical to store and maintain a history of observations. Recurrent Transformers address this limitation by maintaining fixed-size memory but their performance lags behind that of transformers operating over the full observation history. We argue that this gap does not stem from architectural limitations, but from differences in how these models learn to compress past information. Without access to an observation history, recurrent models must explicitly decide what to retain in memory at each step, a significantly harder learning problem. In this work, we propose a distillation approach that transfers the compression strategy of a classical full-history transformer to a recurrent variant. We enable this by designing a teacher model that explicitly compresses its observation history into a fixed-size bottleneck representation and directly supervise the student's memory with this bottleneck representation, effectively aligning the two compression mechanisms. We show that this approach enables training of a recurrent latent robotic memory with linear-time complexity on the Mem-RPE task while substantially narrowing the performance gap to full-history transformers. We additionally validate the same principle on streaming visual question answering (VQA) and observe improved recurrent predictions thanks to memory distillation.

replace TempAct: Advancing Temporal Plausibility in Autoregressive Video Generation via Planner-Executor RL

Authors: Jing Wang, Xiangxin Zhou, Jiajun Liang, Kaiqi Liu, Wanyuan Pang, Zhenyu Xie, Tianyu Pang, Xiaodan Liang

Abstract: Autoregressive (AR) video diffusion models enable low-latency streaming generation by synthesizing videos chunk by chunk with cached visual context, but this chunk-wise formulation makes temporal instruction following ambiguous. A single global prompt does not specify which sub-event should be realized in each chunk, while naively switching to step-wise prompts often leads to delayed reactions, blended step semantics, and error propagation across prompt transitions. These failures are difficult to address with supervised fine-tuning or distillation alone: SFT suffers from exposure bias, while rollout-based distillation still optimizes low-level denoising or teacher-distribution matching rather than directly enforcing action ordering and prompt-transition correctness. We address these challenges with TempAct, a planner--executor reinforcement learning framework that jointly optimizes temporal decomposition and step-conditioned execution for temporally plausible AR video generation. TempAct uses an LLM planner to explore span-aware step prompts that are executable by the video model, and trains an AR diffusion executor to follow these prompts under its own generated histories. Its key mechanism is hierarchical group exploration: candidate plans form planning groups, and each plan induces an execution group of multiple continuations from a shared visual context, enabling plan-level credit assignment for long-horizon temporal outcomes and executor-level credit assignment for prompt-switch behavior. We further design hierarchical rewards that combine plan-quality and full-video temporal feedback for the planner with local transition-level step-following rewards, aesthetic regularization, and KL constraints for the executor. Experiments on Self-Forcing and LongLive show that TempAct improves temporal consistency while preserving overall visual quality.

replace PruneGround: Plug-and-play Spatial Pruning for 3D Visual Grounding

Authors: Duc Cao Dinh, Khai Le-Duc, Florent Draye, Chris Ngo, Terry Jingchen Zhang, Bernhard Sch\"olkopf, Zhijing Jin

Abstract: 3D Visual Grounding (3DVG) aims to localize target objects in 3D scenes given natural language descriptions. Existing approaches typically perform reasoning over the entire scene, leading to ambiguous predictions and high computational cost, especially in cluttered environments. We observe that many referential expressions rely on local spatial context and often correspond to restricted spatial regions rather than the full scene. Motivated by this insight, we propose PruneGround, an effective plug-and-play framework for 3DVG built upon three key components. First, we introduce Language-Guided Spatial Pruning (LGSP), which leverages a frozen Vision Language Model (VLM) to identify language-relevant regions, thereby reducing spatial computation and grounding candidates in the narrower search space. Second, we propose MultiView-Conditioned Description Reformulation (MCDR), which decomposes complex expressions into simplified target--anchor relations and augments missing spatial cues through multi-view reasoning. Finally, we propose LLM-Grounder, which repurposes a detection-pretrained spatial LLM into a language-conditioned grounding model by aligning point cloud and linguistic representations within the pruned region. Extensive experiments on the three most popular point cloud benchmarks demonstrate that our method achieves state-of-the-art results on all three ScanRefer settings and on 9 out of 10 Nr3D/Sr3D settings. Code and models are publicly available: https://github.com/leduckhai/PruneGround

URLs: https://github.com/leduckhai/PruneGround

replace MIBE: Multi-subject Interaction Benchmark and Evaluator for Personalized Image Generation

Authors: Zhihan Chen, Yuhuan Zhao, Yijie Zhu, Xinyu Yao, Mengcong Ren, Suwen Wang, Qiuyang Yin, Yuchen Sun, Qin Wang, Lu Xin

Abstract: Multi-subject personalized image generation aims to compose multiple distinct reference identities into a coherent scene while preserving their prompt-specified interactions. Existing generators frequently exhibit subject-binding failures as scene complexity scales: omitting requested entities (Existence), blending visual attributes across subjects (Appearance), or misassigning actions and spatial relations (Interaction). Compounding this modeling bottleneck, existing evaluation metrics, which are designed primarily for single-subject similarity or holistic image preference, align only weakly with human judgment on these coupled errors, and several degrade toward random agreement as the number of interacting subjects increases. To address this evaluation gap, we introduce the Multi-subject Interaction Benchmark and Evaluator (MIBE), a unified framework pairing a factorized benchmark (MIB) with a reference-conditioned evaluator (MIE). MIB systematically varies subject count, human-/object-heavy composition, and relation type (non-contact, occlusion, and physical interaction) within a decoupled two-tier data regime: MIB-Silver, a 60K-pair training corpus annotated via a rubric-guided dual-VLM protocol, and MIB-Gold, a 4,020-pair evaluation suite annotated under a double-blind human protocol across six state-of-the-art generators spanning both seen and held-out unseen architectures. Trained exclusively on MIB-Silver with a dual-head objective that jointly predicts pairwise preference rankings and facet-level diagnostics, MIE achieves 0.922 overall pairwise agreement with human consensus on MIB-Gold, including 0.982 on seen generators and 0.884 on held-out unseen generators. Using MIBE for cross-generator diagnosis, we quantify systematic binding bottlenecks across modern generators, providing reproducible benchmark infrastructure and interpretable supervision for future model alignment.

replace When Variance Is Not an Error Map: Calibrated Uncertainty for Radiative Gaussian Splatting in Sparse-View CT

Authors: Chulin Zhao, Yiran Xu, Gao Yang, Shu Liu

Abstract: Radiative Gaussian Splatting enables fast and high-quality reconstruction in sparse-view CT, but reliable localization of reconstruction errors within the imaged anatomy remains challenging. It is unclear whether current uncertainty maps can reliably indicate where the reconstruction is wrong. We investigate when uncertainty can serve as a spatial error map and present an error-localization system that predicts voxel-wise reconstruction error by combining predictive uncertainty with complementary reconstruction-derived information. Under fixed Gaussian geometry and independent rectified-Gaussian density variables, an exact closed-form predictive moment is derived in this work to enable direct computation of voxel-wise variance without repeated reconstruction sampling. The resulting uncertainty maps were evaluated over both the whole reconstruction volume and the object foreground. Experiments show that strong whole-volume uncertainty-error correspondence can conceal poor error ranking within the object foreground area. This discrepancy recurs across three tested uncertainty constructions on a 15-scene benchmark. Guided by this analysis, our system combines closed-form predictive dispersion with image, geometry, cross-method discrepancy, and projection-residual cues through a learned error predictor. The resulting system substantially improves voxel-wise error localization within the anatomy, providing a spatially resolved approach to reconstruction reliability assessment in sparse-view CT.

replace HGeo-TopoMap: Boosting Topological Mapping with Hierarchical Geometric Priors

Authors: Siyu Li, Kunyu Peng, Di Wen, Beiping Hou, Zhiyong Li, Kailun Yang

Abstract: Topological maps provide essential road structure and connectivity information for autonomous driving. However, accurate centerline modeling remains challenging due to the lack of explicit visual markings and the insufficient exploitation of geometric regularities in existing methods. To address these challenges, we propose HGeo-TopoMap, a hierarchical geometric prior learning framework that integrates road-structure guidance and geometric consistency into centerline instance decoding. Specifically, a Geometric Adaptive Learning (GAL) module introduces spatially adaptive cross-attention to incorporate road structure priors derived from inverse perspective mapping, where distance-aware attention biases guide instance queries toward spatially relevant structural information. Furthermore, we develop a hierarchical centerline decoding strategy that progressively predicts instance centers and their constituent points, facilitating the learning of geometric orientations and spatial configurations. Building upon this structured representation, a Geometric Consistency Learning (GCL) module imposes layer-wise contrastive supervision on matched instance queries using geometric groupings, exploiting geometric relationships among centerline instances to regularize instance representations and improve centerline detection. By jointly exploiting spatial structural guidance and instance-level geometric regularities, HGeo-TopoMap enhances centerline detection and topology reasoning. Extensive experiments on the OpenLane-V2 centerline and lane segment benchmarks demonstrate improved topological mapping accuracy. Meanwhile, the proposed method demonstrates stable performance under the robustness benchmark. The source code and model weights will be made publicly available at https://github.com/lynn-yu/HGeo-TopoMap.

URLs: https://github.com/lynn-yu/HGeo-TopoMap.

replace OSEF: One-Step Evidence Fusion for Cross-Video Scene Procedure Planning

Authors: Zhentong Ye, Lei Zhang, Sijia Zhou, Yingda Yu, Yuehan Shi, Jiaqi Xuan, Shuaiwu Dong, Guanchao Tong, Meimei Zhang, Bin Li

Abstract: Video Scene Procedure Planning (VSPP) predicts actions between supplied start and goal observations. We introduce Cross-Video Scene Procedure Planning (CVSPP), which instead takes a start-goal query and candidate videos, and predicts an action sequence together with its supporting video and interval. Two challenges arise: demonstrations of the same task can support different transitions, and an early retrieval error can deprive the planner of relevant evidence. We construct a dedicated benchmark from eleven video sources, with task-specific queries, candidate sets, and linked video-interval-plan references. Its fourteen settings support a broad study of nine adapted planner families. We propose One-Step Evidence Fusion (OSEF), which retains candidate features while producing interval-conditioned planning representations. Separate boundary scores model the supporting interval, and compatible-mass decoding combines the probability of the same complete plan across overlapping intervals before selecting the output. Joint evaluation requires video, interval, and plan to match one reference example. Across four matched development-data comparisons on NIV, CrossTask, and COIN, compatible-mass decoding increases mean joint success by 2.40-6.79 percentage points without retraining. On NIV, query-only controls retain plan accuracy yet sharply reduce joint success; donor-interface studies examine how VSPP models use retrieved visual states. We further identify temporal-reference sensitivity: changing only the reference projection can reverse the decoding gain, exposing an evaluation challenge for the new task.

replace CrossProjection: Benchmarking Spatial Grounding across Projection and Section Views

Authors: Kaho Li, Zekai Lin, Pengyu Zeng, Yuqin Dai, Jun Yin, Tianjing Feng, Shuai Lu

Abstract: Plans, elevations, and sections describe a three-dimensional structure through incomplete projections that change how an entity is drawn, not only where it is seen from. Reading them means deciding which entities correspond across views and recovering where they lie. We introduce CrossProjection, a benchmark that uses architectural drawings to test whether vision-language models (VLMs) can turn correspondence decisions into geometric answers. Its tasks cover cross-view matching, projection registration, and novel-section prediction, with outputs ranging from judgments and candidate selection to free region boxes and directed section cuts. For inverse section localization, we introduce, to our knowledge, the first VLM benchmark protocol that accepts any cut within an expert-adjudicated band of compatible positions and checks the viewing direction separately. Manually annotated drawings and BIM-derived views provide 33,788 questions from 46 buildings, and we evaluate 22 VLMs on a common set of 2,763. Strong selection or reproduction does not imply cross-view localization: on 100 matched region targets, the best model redraws 98 marked boxes but localizes only 59 across views. Section cuts remain far from expert level, with the leading model trailing two architects by 51.9 and 67.4 percentage points on shared Natural and BIM line questions. Incorrect choices favor mirrored positions, and many cuts face the correct way but violate local geometry. Curriculum fine-tuning on BIM supervision teaches a 9B model to localize regions it could not localize before, raising box accuracy from 0% to 25.3% on held-out BIM buildings and from 0.3% to 30.8% on real drawing sets not used for fine-tuning, whereas section cuts remain largely out of reach.

replace AeroLLE: Nighttime Aerial Image Enhancement with Generated Supervision

Authors: Wei Lu, Hongyuan Liu, Liuqian Wang, Qi Ming, Xue Yang, Si-Bao Chen

Abstract: Nighttime aerial image enhancement is constrained by the scarcity of aligned normal-light targets. Acquiring such targets from moving UAVs is difficult, and simple darkening of daytime images underrepresents the sensor noise, localized lighting, and uneven exposure of real nighttime captures. Generating normal-light references from real nighttime observations offers a practical way to fill this supervision gap. AeroNight pairs 1,200 real nighttime aerial RGB images with generated references selected through manual screening, preserving real imaging degradation in the inputs. This setting motivates AeroLLE, a lightweight two-stage framework whose context-aware Base Enhancer combines local processing with scene-level frequency information to recover visibility. Spatially Adaptive Exposure-Color Calibration (SAECC) then refines the base prediction through bounded low-resolution RGB gain and bias fields. AeroLLE achieves the highest PSNR and SSIM among the evaluated methods on both held-out test sets, reaching 17.62/17.89 dB PSNR and 0.5190/0.4964 SSIM on Test 1/Test 2, respectively. The model uses 255.24K parameters and 2.47G FLOPs, reducing FLOPs by 85.4\% relative to Multinex and improving PSNR by 1.56 dB on Test 2. AeroNight provides the first benchmark for pseudo-supervised nighttime aerial enhancement, and AeroLLE shows that generated references can support compact enhancement models.

replace ISRS-DETR: Detection-Guided Click Propagation for Remote Sensing Interactive Segmentation

Authors: Thanh Duc Pham, Anh Nguyen, Duong Duc Hieu, Minh-Tan Pham

Abstract: Interactive segmentation reduces the prohibitive cost of pixel-level annotation by allowing users to delineate objects with a few clicks. However, applying this paradigm directly to remote sensing imagery is non-trivial: ultra-high resolutions, small object sizes, and sparse spatial distributions all degrade segmentation quality. Recent work has addressed the resolution barrier and achieved competitive results in interactive segmentation for remote sensing (ISRS). However, they treat all instances of a class within an image as a single objective target. Consequently, interactions spent on one object contribute nothing to its same-class neighbours, and satisfactory masks may hinder the practicality of these frameworks. We observe that remote sensing scenes exhibit markedly strong inter-object correlation, meaning a single clicked object is highly informative about the rest of its category. Building on this, we propose ISRS-DETR, a detection-guided interactive segmentation framework that injects object-level evidence into both training and inference. Our ISRS-DETR employs a looped DETR-based decoder, a single weight-shared decoder layer iterated several times, with the interactive segmentation backbone to localise co-occurring same-class objects, and introduces a Dynamic Top-K Click Selection strategy that retains only reliable proposals and converts each into a simulated click, so one user interaction propagates across an entire class. Experiments on three standard remote sensing benchmarks show that ISRS-DETR achieves state-of-the-art accuracy while substantially reducing Number of Clicks (NoC). Code will be released for reproducibility upon acceptance.

replace UniView: Large-Baseline View Synthesis via Video Diffusion Models

Authors: Wangbo Yu, Haiyang Zhou, Chaoran Feng, Xunyu Zhou, Li Yuan

Abstract: Novel view synthesis from casually captured monocular images and videos offers a practical route to immersive content creation without dedicated multi-view capture. However, the limited viewpoint coverage of such inputs leaves scene geometry insufficiently constrained, restricting the ability of reconstruction-based methods, such as neural radiance fields (NeRF) and 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS), to render views far from the observed viewpoints. Generative approaches leverage learned visual priors to synthesize unobserved content, but maintaining geometric consistency and precise camera control remains challenging under large viewpoint changes. We present UniView, a unified framework for large-baseline novel view synthesis and scene generation from monocular images and videos. Central to our approach are an occlusion-aware point cloud renderer and a dual-branch conditioning mechanism. The renderer uses triple reprojection to identify and filter unreliable projections under large viewpoint changes. The conditioning mechanism integrates the resulting geometric guidance with source-view appearance within the video diffusion model, enabling precise camera control while preserving visual details and synthesizing unobserved content. To extend view synthesis to scene generation, we introduce a two-stage generation strategy that decouples viewpoint changes from temporal evolution. This strategy produces dense, synchronized multi-view videos that provide additional supervision for dynamic Gaussian Splatting, enabling more complete 4D generation from monocular observations. Extensive evaluations across static and dynamic scenes demonstrate the effectiveness of UniView in novel view synthesis and scene generation, combining precise camera control with geometric consistency and visual fidelity.

replace Adaptation Circuits for Compute-Efficient Fine-Tuning

Authors: Uri Z. Kialy, Gil Ben-Artzi

Abstract: Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning (PEFT) has become the de facto standard for adapting Vision Transformers (ViTs) to downstream tasks. While parameter count has been the dominant efficiency metric in PEFT, it does not imply compute efficiency: parameter-sparse methods can still incur full-model training cost per step, and typically need long schedules to reach peak accuracy. Across seven fine-tuning methods on ViT-B/16, trainable parameters span $0.1\%$ to $100\%$ of the backbone, while the measured time of a training epoch stays within $0.74\times$ to $1.69\times$ that of full fine-tuning and does not necessarily grow with the trainable fraction. We introduce Circuit Fine-Tuning (CFT), which uses attribution to select an adaptation circuit, a parameter-budgeted set of attention heads and MLP blocks, before any task head is trained, and tunes it in place, reducing the number of training steps rather than the cost of a step. CFT adds no parameters, uses no learning-rate warmup, and leaves the inference graph unchanged. On VTAB-1k, CFT is comparable with the most accurate baseline while reaching its best checkpoint after $24$ epochs on average, against $35$ to $72$ for the baselines; at equal accuracy the most efficient baseline needs $1.1$ to $3.6\times$ the training FLOPs of CFT and $1.6$ to $3.8\times$ its training time. In a $5$-epoch comparison under domain shift, on mammography lesions (CBIS-DDSM), CFT has the highest F1 on ViT-B/16 and Swin-v2-B and ties on DINOv2. A feasibility study applies CFT to a vision-language model (Gemma-3) as well.

replace Beyond Statistical Priors: Architecture-Agnostic Data-Free Knowledge Distillation via Unified Explicit Semantics

Authors: Xuewan He, Tong Chu, Zihan Cheng, Yuchen Su, Qianxin Xia, Guoming Lu, Jielei Wang, Wen Li

Abstract: Data-Free Knowledge Distillation (DFKD) transfers knowledge from a pretrained teacher model to a compact student model by synthesizing semantically informative data, eliminating the need for access to the original training dataset. Existing DFKD methods rely heavily on architecture-specific statistical priors (e.g., Batch Normalization statistics) to guide data synthesis; however, such architecture-dependent priors are often absent in modern architectures such as Vision Transformers (ViTs), resulting in degraded semantic quality of the synthesized data and consequently catastrophic performance degradation. In this paper, we propose UniDFKD, a unified data-free knowledge distillation framework that replaces architecture-specific statistics with explicit, architecture-agnostic semantic priors. UniDFKD governs the entire synthesis-distillation pipeline along three dimensions: (1) Categorical Semantic Conditioning (CSC) defines what to synthesize by persistently modulating the generator with language-derived embeddings to capture semantic diversity; (2) Spatial Semantic Anchoring (SSA) regularizes where evidence belongs by anchoring the teacher's spatial attributions to a Gaussian prior; and (3) Spatial Semantic Distillation (SSD) controls how knowledge is transferred by explicitly aligning teacher-student spatial evidence alongside predictions. Extensive experiments across CNNs and ViTs demonstrate that UniDFKD sets a new state of the art. It outperforms the strongest baseline NAYER by average absolute margins of 17.23 and 24.49 in top-1 accuracy under homogeneous and heterogeneous settings, respectively.

replace DistMoE: Private-data Rehearsal-free Routing in Mixture-of-Experts for Distributed Instruction Tuning

Authors: Mainak Singha, Niccol\`o Biondi, Elisa Ricci, Subhankar Roy

Abstract: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have shown strong multimodal instruction-following ability, but adapting them to diverse visual-language domains typically assumes centralized data access and costly joint training. This is restrictive when data is distributed across private, domain-specific, or permission-limited clients. To this end, we propose DistMoE, a mixture-of-experts (MoE) approach for distributed visual instruction tuning. In each layer of the language decoder it augments the public feedforward network (FFN) with a client-specific private FFN expert, with the goal to acquire domain-specific knowledge. However, independent expert training causes the private FFNs to learn representation of different scales and magnitudes, making merging the experts difficult. To reduce client-specific drift, we introduce a public-anchored expert composition stage that updates only routers and lightweight private projection adapters on a mix of local client data and public data, via an isotropic regularization loss, therefore making it cross-client rehearsal-free composition. During inference, DistMoE performs modular routing over public and private experts, enabling token-wise domain composition without explicit domain labels. Experiments across diverse visual-language benchmarks show that DistMoE enables flexible expert reuse, effective domain adaptation, and competitive performance while preserving modular control over client-specific knowledge.

replace Semantic Prism: Generative Segmentation with an Explicit Semantic-Image Interface

Authors: Weize Cai, Yongqi Dong, Zhida Shao, Zixin Fu, Xiaoxi Hu

Abstract: Conditional image generators can render segmentation maps as semantic RGB images, but color drift and boundary mixing can undermine direct decoding. We present Semantic Prism, a framework that combines an explicit semantic-image interface with hierarchical refinement for road-scene segmentation. A generator adapted from a diffusion-distilled backbone produces a semantic RGB image with a single denoising update per crop. Distances to a fixed class-color codebook define interface logits and pixelwise class probabilities. Hierarchical Generator Evidence Alignment (HGEA) combines spatially aligned multilevel features from the VAE encoder of the generator with the input image, generated semantic image, and interface probabilities to predict additive corrections to these logits. Contextual Interface Hierarchy Disagreement (C-IHD) combines pointwise uncertainty, local context, and interface-refinement disagreement in a fixed score for ranking pixel errors, without an auxiliary predictor or an additional network pass. On the 500-image Cityscapes validation set, Semantic Prism achieves 72.07% mean mIoU, improving on direct-interface decoding by 11.39 percentage points. Extensive capacity-matched ablations show higher mean mIoU for multilevel refinement than for flat and single-level alternatives. The Cityscapes-trained model transfers to ACDC Dataset, achieving 46.89% mIoU without target-domain adaptation. Beyond Cityscapes, on BDD100K dataset, a separately trained model achieves 62.22% mIoU on its full 1,000-image validation set. Across the 3 evaluated datasets, C-IHD improves pixel-error area under the AUPR over uncertainty derived from maximum softmax probability, with segmentation predictions held fixed for each comparison. In particular, on ACDC, AUPR increases from 0.6580 to 0.7557, demonstrating that Semantic Prism presents more effective error ranking under adverse-condition domain shift.

replace AdaGEM: Granularity Adaptation and Geospatial Evidence Modulation for Ultra-High-Resolution Remote Sensing Understanding

Authors: Kexin Ma, Jing Xiao, Bowen Xing, Liang Liao, Chia-Wen Lin

Abstract: Ultra-high-resolution (UHR) remote sensing imagery provides rich spatial details and extensive geographic coverage for Earth observation understanding. However, such imagery generates quadratically growing visual token sequences, where sparse task-critical evidence is easily overwhelmed by redundant and task-irrelevant tokens, leading to excessive computation and ineffective evidence preservation. Existing methods seek to balance computational efficiency and task effectiveness through selective multi-scale inspection or direct token pruning, yet often overlook task-dependent granularity requirements and struggle to distinguish sparse critical evidence from overwhelming visual redundancy. To address these challenges, we propose AdaGEM, a unified framework that jointly adapts visual granularity to task demands and modulates token selection to preserve essential geospatial evidence. Specifically, a Granularity Router predicts detail requirements and evidence scope from the query, then selects input resolution and token budget via a rule-based mapping. At the adapted granularity, a Geospatial Evidence Modulator selects complementary visual tokens to preserve critical evidence and spatial context while suppressing redundancy in homogeneous regions. Extensive experiments across diverse remote sensing benchmarks and multiple MLLM backbones demonstrate that AdaGEM consistently improves task performance over strong baselines and existing UHR methods while substantially reducing inference costs.

replace ArmorOCR: Grounded Adversarial Visual Perception via Observation-Transferred Self-Distillation

Authors: Linhan Cao, Siyuan Li, Jun Lan, Liangbo He, Guannan Li, Xiaolei Huang, Jun Jia, Shuheng Zhou, Huijia Zhu, Weiqiang Wang, Wei Sun

Abstract: Large multimodal models (LMMs) have demonstrated strong OCR recognition capabilities, yet remain vulnerable to adversarial visual text that is readable to humans but challenging for models to localize and recognize. Existing OCR benchmarks mainly focus on natural or document-style text, while adversarial OCR evaluations remain limited in scale, task coverage, or region-aware evaluation. In this paper, we formulate adversarial OCR as a \textbf{grounded OCR perception} task and introduce \textbf{AdvSpot}, the first benchmark for grounded adversarial OCR evaluation. AdvSpot comprises 383 images with region-level annotations, spanning 5 primary categories and 13 fine-grained adversarial OCR types. To address this challenge, we propose \textbf{ArmorOCR}, a two-stage training framework for robust adversarial OCR perception. ArmorOCR first acquires missing adversarial OCR perception from privileged transformed observations through On-Policy Self-Distillation (OPSD), and then refines grounded OCR perception through Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) with task-conditioned rewards for localization, recognition, full spotting, and visual question answering (VQA). Experiments on our AdvSpot, other adversarial OCR benchmarks, and general OCR benchmarks demonstrate that ArmorOCR consistently improves adversarial OCR perception while preserving competitive general OCR capability. The benchmark and code are available at https://github.com/ant-research/ArmorOCR

URLs: https://github.com/ant-research/ArmorOCR

replace GraspHOI: Full-Body 3D Human-Object Reconstruction with Articulated Hands from a Single In-the-Wild Image

Authors: Semin Kim, Haechan Shin, Jongyoo Kim

Abstract: Existing monocular full-body 3D human-object interaction (HOI) methods do not combine category-agnostic object reconstruction with finger-level contact. Their hands may float off or sink into objects, breaking realism in AR/VR environments. We present GraspHOI, the first category-agnostic framework that reconstructs a full-body 3D HOI from a single image and jointly refines the arms and fingers so that the hands meet the reconstructed object surface. GraspHOI reconstructs the body, hands, and object separately with foundation models and places them in a shared metric camera frame through depth-based registration and silhouette alignment. Occlusion-aware contact correspondences seat the object against the hand, and contact-aware optimization refines shoulder, elbow, wrist, and finger rotations while anchoring the joints to the image. We find that with fingers capped near the image estimate, refining them alone is insufficient; freeing the arm removes most of the remaining hand-object gap, since in a full body the arm chain places the hand. In experiments on three datasets, GraspHOI yields more plausible hand-object contact than four baselines and better relative human-object placement than HDM and TeHOR.

replace SignSeek: Learning Transferable Representations for Sign Dictionary Retrieval

Authors: Sobhan Asasi, Ozge Mercanoglu Sincan, Richard Bowden

Abstract: Sign language dictionaries are essential resources for sign language learners, yet automatically retrieving a sign from a dictionary, given only a query video, remains a challenging problem due to the natural variability between signers. Existing sign representation learning methods are built for closed-set recognition, producing embeddings that do not generalise to the open-set, signer-independent setting that retrieval demands. \textbf{SignSeek} closes this gap by contrastively learning sign representations with saliency-guided articulator masking. A contrastive objective aligns same-gloss signs across signers, while our Articulator Saliency-Guided Masking (ASGM) pinpoints the single most critical articulator per sign. This drives two complementary objectives, a masked contrastive alignment (MAC) loss that sees the sign through a single articulator and a masked prediction (MAP) loss that reconstructs it in latent space from the surrounding spatio-temporal context. Pretrained on 266K samples ($\sim$5,700 glosses) across multiple sign languages, \textbf{SignSeek} sets a new state-of-the-art performance in cross-corpus retrieval on ASL-Citizen, WLASL, and NMFs-CSL without any downstream fine-tuning. Strikingly, it achieves zero-shot generalisation to an entirely unseen British Sign Language (BSL), surpassing methods explicitly trained on BSL, and transfers seamlessly to isolated sign recognition and subtitle alignment, outperforming prior skeleton-based methods.

replace EgoPathBench: Evaluating Zero-Shot Egocentric Waypoint Decision-Making in Vision-Language Models

Authors: Yang Zhao, Zhuo Chen, Xubo Yang

Abstract: Widely used spatial-QA benchmarks increasingly motivate embodied navigation, yet evaluate foundation vision-language models (VLMs) through isolated queries about space rather than feasible action sequences in space. However, zero-shot waypoint navigation requires turning spatial understanding into a feasible action sequence: from a single first-person view, a model must jointly ground the target, respect agent-specific clearance, maintain edge-by-edge legality, and stop at the goal. To evaluate this capability, we introduce EgoPathBench, a dataset and five-task benchmark for first-person waypoint decision-making. Each example pairs an egocentric RGB image, a natural-language goal, and numbered visible waypoints, and scores predicted candidate sets or ordered routes against registered 3D scene geometry under task-specific Point or Embodied feasibility graphs. EgoPathBench comprises 31,852 training, 1,345 validation, and 1,111 benchmark questions, with at least one verified reference route for every route question. Across ten foundation VLMs, the highest EgoPath Score is only 35.6. While models often make locally plausible choices and the top model reaches 41.7% success on Point Path, it drops to 10.0% on both Embodied Path and Intent Path, with the remaining nine models below 3% on Embodied Path (and at most 4.0% on Intent Path) -- exposing a sharp gap between local plausibility and complete embodied routes. Beyond evaluation, we construct the EgoPath-SFT training set. Fine-tuning Qwen3.5-4B on EgoPath-SFT raises its EgoPath Score from 3.9 to 38.9, improves closed-loop Isaac Sim execution by 20 percentage points, and transfers consistently to three external spatial benchmarks.

replace PointEvent: Polarity-Conditioned Motion Evidence Accumulation for Event-Based Tiny Object Detection

Authors: Zongze Wu, Baofeng Jia, Weiqi Yan, Jingyuan Zhang, Min Liu, Yu Zang, Xiaoyu Chen, Jing Han

Abstract: Event cameras offer high temporal resolution and motion sensitivity for tiny UAV detection, but distant targets produce sparse, fragmented events easily overwhelmed by clutter and noise. Dense aggregation mixes weak target responses with background activity, while local spatiotemporal modeling may fail to connect distant observations. Polarity encodes the sign of brightness change; a moving target tends to produce opposite polarities at its leading and trailing boundaries, whose spatial arrangement indicates motion direction. We propose PointEvent, which serializes events into complementary spatiotemporal and timestamp orders. Spatiotemporal orders preserve x-y-t locality, while timestamp orders preserve temporal succession across spatial neighborhoods, establishing long-range propagation paths. Along each path, a recurrent state accumulates evidence, while polarity routes each event to its corresponding state subspace. This separates how evidence propagates from where it is written. Experiments on EV-UAV and ES-UAV demonstrate state-of-the-art accuracy with the fewest parameters (0.12M) and fastest measured inference (29.9 ms) among compared methods. Code: https://github.com/wzz-z/PointEvent

URLs: https://github.com/wzz-z/PointEvent

replace ConsistWorld: Evidence Routing for Consistent Multi-Agent World Models

Authors: Qianxun Xu, Xianfang Zeng, Xinyao Liao, Wei Cheng, Gang Yu, Chi Zhang

Abstract: Autoregressive video world models enable temporally coherent generation for a single observer. Extending them to multiple agents requires consistency across independently controlled views and temporal gaps under causal streaming. We present ConsistWorld, a multi-agent world model that generates camera-controlled video streams of a static scene from one shared image. We formulate consistency as routing evidence from committed multi-agent history and concurrently generated peer views to the tokens being generated. Pose Conditioned Memory Retrieval selects relevant historical observations from all agents, recovering evidence beyond the recent context window. Visibility-Gated Peer Sharing regulates current peer information according to estimated historical coverage and current-view overlap. Together, they determine which historical observations enter the context and where concurrent peer information contributes, supporting long-term recall and coordinated exploration. Both mechanisms use camera geometry and maintain a bounded active context for a fixed agent count and retrieval budget. Experiments on evidence sharing cases and video length and agent number generalizations show that ConsistWorld achieves a strong cross-time and cross-agent consistency while preserving competitive generation quality.

replace Forensic Twins: Self-Supervised Residual Learning for AI-Generated Image Forensics

Authors: Javier Mu\~noz-Haro, Ruben Tolosana, Ruben Vera-Rodriguez, Aythami Morales, Julian Fierrez

Abstract: Detectors of AI-generated images are typically trained using samples from specific Generative AI architectures and degrade as soon as a new architecture emerges. Recent approaches have explored self-supervised pre-training as an alternative solution, yet standard frameworks work against the forensic task, since their augmentations overwrite the micro-statistics of image formation. This paper introduces Forensic Twins, a Self-Supervised Residual Learning (SSRL) framework whose pretext task removes shared image content between views. Each image is mapped through a forensic residual extractor, from which two spatially disjoint crops are drawn. Sharing no pixel, the two views leave the redundancy-reduction objective with a predominantly content-independent common signal, the stationary fingerprint of the image acquisition pipeline. The encoder is exclusively trained on real images; no AI-generated image is used to learn the representation. Experiments show competitive results in zero-shot, few-shot, and linear probing scenarios, reaching 97.21% AUC across 26 unseen AI generators, including GANs, diffusion models, and commercial systems. We also provide insightful analysis on the learned representation regarding unsupervised clustering on different AI generators and its forensic properties. Code, weights and exact splits are publicly available.

replace Resolving State-Representation Mismatch: State-Space Visual Reasoning for Open-Loop VLA Planning

Authors: Junhao Xiao, Haoxiang Zhao, Menghao Fang, Jinkui Zhang, Jinghan Yu, Xinyu Huang, Zhiyu Wu, Kaiming Xu, Yi Chen, Youjun Bao, Zhiyuan Ma

Abstract: Despite rapid progress in vision-language-action (VLA) models, existing reasoning paradigms still face a fundamental \emph{state-representation mismatch} in open-loop planning. Given only an initial observation, models must internally simulate action-conditioned state transitions, whereas text-, pixel-, and latent-space reasoning can suffer from lossy spatial compression, error-accumulating visual generation, and bypass of intermediate latent tokens, respectively, undermining reliable long-horizon planning. We propose \textbf{State-Space Visual Reasoning} (SSVR), which decouples static visual context, language constraints, and a recurrent latent state. SSVR encodes the initial image and instruction once, then conditions each action prediction on the latent state and updates it with an action-conditioned GRU. Using Qwen2.5-VL as the backbone, SSVR achieves 99.5/99.6, 96.3/98.0, and 83.9/90.6 EM/PR on FrozenLake, Maze, and MiniBehavior, substantially outperforming prior methods. Extensive experiments support the effectiveness of recurrent state modeling for VLA open-loop planning across input transformations and transfer settings. By reusing static visual-textual context and updating a compact recurrent state, SSVR supports efficient multi-step inference, achieving up to $98.58\times$ faster Maze decoding rollouts than the evaluated baselines with the prefix cache prebuilt.

replace DirectUV: Image-Conditioned UV Texture Generation with Surface-Aware Positional Encoding

Authors: Jiantao Lin, Yingjie Xu, Mingzhi Sheng, Yangkai Wei, Hao Chen, Ying-Cong Chen

Abstract: Generating high-quality UV textures for 3D meshes remains challenging. Multi-view projection pipelines suffer from occlusion and view inconsistency, and recent methods that generate textures directly in UV space still rely on auxiliary modules to supply 3D information, leaving the attention mechanism tied to UV-grid positions rather than to the underlying surface geometry. This mismatch limits coherence across seams and disconnected UV islands. We propose DirectUV, an image-conditioned UV texture diffusion framework that operates in the latent UV space of a pretrained image VAE, in which a Diffusion Transformer denoises the UV latent given a single input image and a coarse UV map. At its core, Surface-Aware Positional Encoding (SAPE) replaces the standard 2D-grid positional encoding with encodings derived from per-token 3D surface coordinates obtained via UV-to-surface correspondence. As positional encodings define the distance metric used by attention, SAPE enables tokens to interact according to 3D positional proximity derived from surface correspondence rather than UV-grid distance, restoring coherence across seams and disconnected islands. A multi-level extension further assigns different attention heads to progressively finer subdivisions of the same latent UV patch, allowing the model to reason about surface structure at multiple granularities. Experiments show that DirectUV produces sharper and more globally consistent textures than other baselines, with the largest improvements in occluded and view-unseen regions where projection-based methods leave gaps or stretched textures.

replace Sparse-View Interpretable 3D Animal Behavior Representations for Neural Encoding and Decoding

Authors: Xinming Dai, Qihang Jin, Jiaying Zhang, Tianshu Tan, Baiyuan Chen, Hanrui Lyu, Lenny Aharon, Kyle Daruwalla, Xun Helen Hou, Matthew R. Whiteway, Liam Paninski, Yizi Zhang

Abstract: A deeper understanding of brain function requires a precise, structured characterization of behavior. Yet, extracting behavioral representations from video in a form suitable for scientific analysis remains a fundamental challenge. Many prior studies represent behavior via pose estimation or nonlinear video embeddings. However, pose tracking discards rich information beyond predefined keypoints, while nonlinear video embeddings lack interpretability. We address this limitation with SABLE (Sparse-view Animal Behavior Latent Embeddings), a self-supervised framework that leverages a geometric inductive bias to learn behavior representations. By augmenting a multi-view transformer with priors from monocular depth and pose estimation, SABLE reconstructs 3D animal behavior from extremely sparse views while learning explicit 3D latent structure. Without ground-truth 3D labels, it reliably recovers 3D behavior from two-view videos, whereas state-of-the-art (SOTA) methods fail or yield degenerate solutions. Across the International Brain Lab and Cheese3D datasets, we demonstrate that SABLE learns 3D representations that match or exceed prior SOTA performance in neural encoding and decoding. Single-neuron analyses further identify neurons with putative 3D tuning, generating testable hypotheses about how the brain represents 3D behavior. Once pretrained across animals, SABLE serves as an off-the-shelf model that generalizes zero-shot to unseen animals without animal-specific calibration or retraining. Our method establishes 3D-aware video embeddings that capture complex behavior, opening new avenues for studying brain-behavior relationships.

replace Learning via Self-Consistency for Diffusion-based Video Reasoning

Authors: Zhenghao Ni, Weimin Qiu, Meng Tang

Abstract: While video diffusion models have demonstrated emerging zero-shot capability for visual reasoning and perception tasks, their stochastic generations often produce inconsistent predictions for tasks requiring reliable, structured outputs. Prior work has shown that self-consistency across stochastic video generations can improve diffusion-based reasoning at test time. We systematically extend the test-time method to structured prediction problems such as referring segmentation. However, test time scaling via self-consistency requires multiple rollouts and incurs much more latency. We propose self-consistency as an intrinsic learning signal for post-training video diffusion models without ground truth annotations or external verifiers. Specifically, our proposed Self-Consistency Fine-Tuning (SC-FT) distills consensus predictions into the video generation model, while subsequent Self-Consistency Reinforcement Learning (SC-RL) rewards generations according to their agreement with the consensus. The resulting model effectively internalizes the benefit of multi-sample consensus and requires only a single generation at inference time, while substantially outperforming the original model. To further reduce inference overhead, we read out predictions early in the denoising trajectory, preserving consensus quality while cutting inference time per rollout by 52%. For visual search, our method raises task accuracy from 48.4% for a single generation to 99.1%. For maze solving across three layout sizes, SC-FT and SC-RL improve single generation strict validity from 59.1% to 66.1% and 95.6% with the same inference latency. Also, for referring segmentation, SC-RL raises single-generation gIoU from 0.402 to 0.582, beyond SC-FT 0.473.

replace Where to Look Is Not How to Fix: Pre-Denoising Diagnostics and Modality-Dependent Control in Diffusion Composition

Authors: Fangzheng Wu, Brian Summa

Abstract: Understanding compositional failures in text-to-image diffusion requires identifying both where stress is detectable and how intervention changes the output. We study these questions through a controlled anchor--stress protocol that jointly evaluates text-encoder diagnostics and denoiser interventions. We introduce a text-only Compositional Stress Index (CSI), which separates common from rare compositions across SD1.5, SDXL, and the SD3 text path and provides an upstream diagnostic coordinate. A matched six-prompt localization study links intervention location to distinct outcomes: residual-minimizing embedding adapters improve representation fit, while downstream cross-attention intervention increases color hit rate (CHR) by 0.0272. Across SD1.5 and SDXL denoiser blocks, the largest positive signed diagnostic-accessibility mean occurs at the deep encoder, whereas selective boost has its largest positive mean CHR response at decoder blocks. Selective subtraction and broad ablation reveal further modality- and architecture-dependent responses, including a substantial CHR decrease when SDXL decoder cross-attention is broadly ablated. We find a diagnosis-control dissociation under our controlled attribute-object composition setting: compositional defects are diagnosable before denoising, but the representation coordinate that exposes risk is not necessarily the coordinate or modality that improves generation.

replace Motion Without State: Probing Dynamical Consistency and Conditioning Bias in Video Models

Authors: Weihan Li, Junhao Wu, Yuhan Song, Xiaofeng Lin, Xinlei Chen

Abstract: Image-to-video models serve as visual world priors whose plausible motion departs from physical simulation. We ask what dynamical structure replaces forward state propagation in generated motion, and how it biases quantitative physics evaluation. Holding law and initial state fixed, we vary visible geometry, prompt wording, and clip length across nine models and compare tracked trajectories against simulator rollouts. Generated motion splits into local visual cues and global boundary control, omitting the propagating physical state between them. Locally, tempo-normalized speed follows track undulations with a 15{\deg}-41{\deg} phase lead toward steepest descent as mean slope barely accelerates the ball, showing short memory of past slope. Local responses fail to compose: no single contact law describes a model's bounces (prescribed, self-calibrated on single-deflector contacts, or fitted in-sample), and balls clear 43 of 49 barriers above release height under an outcome-free prompt. Globally, naming the catch tray lifts arrival 14-76 percentage points above post-contact simulator rollouts, and event duration scales near-linearly with clip length, making apparent gravity 5.6-7.3 times higher in 3 s than in 8 s clips. These patterns persist on photographed first frames, motivating neutral-prompt controls, exit-state rollouts, and tempo-normalized metrics.

replace Bayesian Optimization in Sequence-to-Architecture Latent Space for Zero-Shot NAS

Authors: Ondrej Tybl, Lukas Neumann
