Abstract: Zero-shot Neural Architecture Search removes the prohibitive cost of traditional NAS, but its search process is typically based on the evolutionary algorithm (EA); lacking an explicit model of the objective, it often resorts to a near-random search through mutation. Bayesian Optimization offers a principled alternative by modeling the objective and aggregating information across iterations, but scales poorly to the high-dimensional, discrete, graph-structured spaces of modern NAS, restricting its use to only small networks. In this paper, we bring Bayesian Optimization to zero-shot NAS for large-scale architectures by learning a latent space via a Variational Autoencoder trained to reconstruct a novel prefix encoding of architectures and propose a proxy scalarization that combines several zero-shot proxies into a single Bayesian Optimization objective. After only 10,000 iterations of the proposed search algorithm (8 hours on a single GPU), our method found a network architecture which under the given model parameter count constraints achieves state-of-the-art results on three separate tasks -- image classification, object detection and semantic segmentation.

replace GS-Pool: Object-Level Change Detection in 3D Gaussian Splatting

Authors: Boaz Keren-Gil, James Gain, Patrick Marais

Abstract: Many physical spaces require photographic monitoring of changes to their structure over time. When each visit is reconstructed with 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS), a direct comparison of the two reconstructions cannot answer this because training is stochastic and two reconstructions of an unchanged space never coincide. To address this, we propose GS-Pool, which takes two independently reconstructed Gaussian scans of the same space and returns the changed objects in each, together with their change masks. Our method begins by extracting SAM2 masks from the photographs of each visit. These are then lifted onto the Gaussians that render them and merged into an object pool, a collection of 3D objects, so every decision is taken once per object in 3D. We introduce a novel photographic carrier approach, which co-opts the existing 3DGS mechanism. It does so by applying the photographs of the other visit and back-propagating them to the Gaussians that rendered each pixel. We combine this with GS-Diff's geometry and colour terms and our distilled DINOv3 features. This evidence is compared with objects identified as unchanged in both visits to set a change threshold for each scene. On the PASLCD benchmark, GS-Pool achieves mIoU/F1 scores of 0.75/0.85 against 0.64/0.76 for GS-Diff, the strongest offline method for detecting change in 3DGS. Finally, we return each changed object as a set of Gaussians, which can be inspected in 3D.

replace Seek-and-View Reasoning for Multi-View Spatial Understanding

Authors: Qixiang Chen, Cheng Zhang, Fucai Ke, Chi-Wing Fu, Jianfei Cai, Jingwen Ye

Abstract: Existing multi-view spatial reasoning remains constrained by sparse and fixed viewpoints. Consequently, vision-language models (VLMs) must infer spatial relations solely from these fixed views, leading to fragile cross-view alignment and a geometry-to-language bottleneck. To overcome these issues, we formulate a novel Seek-and-View reasoning paradigm that seeks a viewpoint to make the queried spatial relation directly observable and interprets the sought view to support the spatial reasoning. Building on this paradigm, we propose Vantage, a training-free and model-agnostic framework that structures VLM reasoning for viewpoint planning and evidence-augmented spatial understanding, with a 3D foundation model supporting view synthesis. Specifically, we develop a viewpoint-grounded reasoning stage that translates spatial queries into targeted viewpoint plans. A subsequent geometry-grounded evidence augmentation stage synthesizes new views and incorporates visual evidence alongside preceding reasoning contexts for final answering. Together, these stages reduce reliance on language-based cross-view alignment. Extensive experiments on six VLMs across six benchmarks show consistent improvements in overall accuracy, with an average absolute gain of 8.2% without fine-tuning.

replace Image Diffusion Models Are Good 3D Human Motion Generators

Authors: Siyuan Yang, Chong Wang, Hao Cheng, Jun Liu, Bihan Wen, Alex Kot, Hedvig Kjellstrom

Abstract: Text-to-motion generation typically relies on dataset-specific skeletal representations, with models trained separately for benchmarks with different joint layouts. We investigate whether image diffusion architectures can model 3D human motion through a common image-compatible representation that accommodates heterogeneous skeletal layouts while preserving motion-specific kinematic structure. To this end, we organize root-centered joint trajectories into topology-informed joint--time images and model global root dynamics through a complementary latent stream. A motion autoencoder conditions local-motion decoding on the root latent, while a text-conditioned Scalable Interpolant Transformer (SiT) jointly generates both latent streams, with supervision on decoded 3D joint positions further constraining generation in motion space. In addition to dataset-specific models, we train a single motion autoencoder on HumanML3D and KIT-ML, followed by a single text-conditioned SiT shared across both datasets, preserving their respective skeletal definitions without skeleton retargeting to a common joint layout. Experiments on HumanML3D and KIT-ML show that our dataset-specific models achieve state-of-the-art performance, while shared training yields further gains on both benchmarks, demonstrating the benefits of shared modeling across heterogeneous skeletal layouts.

replace Urbex: Agentic Spatial Grounding for City-Scale 3D Scenes

Authors: Yu Wang, Rui Dai, Wei Chen, Yi Wang, Bo Dang, Kaikui Liu, Xiangxiang Chu, Yansheng Li

Abstract: City-scale 3D grounding is commonly formulated as candidate ranking, which requires precomputed instance proposals and becomes inefficient in large outdoor scenes. We propose Urbex, an agentic vision-language framework that instead casts grounding as active spatial search. Urbex represents a city-scale 3D scene as an interactive multi-view environment, allowing a VLM agent to query landmarks, zoom into local regions, render oblique views, and commit a grounded bounding box. We optimize this tool-use policy with reinforcement learning on CityRefer, using smooth localization rewards and lightweight shaping rewards for efficient exploration. Experiments show that Urbex achieves the best Hit@1 and IoU on CityRefer, supports candidate-free grounding without ground-truth boxes, and is substantially faster than the strongest city-scale ranking baseline. Zero-shot results on STPLS3D-Refer further indicate improved cross-scene robustness.

replace Find the Fungible: Variable-Resolution Tokenization that Compacts Semantics with a Granularity Dial as a VLM "Catalyst"

Authors: Tianhao Fu, Austin Wang, Charles Chen, Roby Aldave-Garza, Victor Xie

Abstract: Efficient vision-language models should preserve a useful representation of the whole image, even under tight budgets. However, aggressive pruning or indiscriminate merging can erase critical details and disrupt the spatial structure. We thus propose Varest, which maximizes semantic fungibility: tokens describing coherent content can be summarized together with minimal information loss. Object boundaries distilled from a visual foundation model guide grouping, while text localization protects details needed for reading. At lower budgets, Varest merges the image into coarser semantic regions, while maintaining full spatial coverage. After early language decoder layers process this representation, a learned selector prunes visual tokens that later layers no longer need, reducing repeated access to already integrated information. Across three LLaVA backbones and 13 benchmarks, Varest outperforms 10 training-free and 5 trained baselines in mean performance retention at every evaluated budget, preserving 86.10-92.33% of uncompressed performance using only 5.56% of tokens. replace CryoTracer: Synthetic-to-Real Filament Instance Tracing in Cryo-EM

Authors: Rados{\l}aw Kuczba\'nski, Wojciech Koz{\l}owski, Mateusz Grzesiuk, Konrad Karanowski, Lukas Frey, Maciej Zi\k{e}ba

Abstract: Filament picking in cryo-electron microscopy requires tracing continuous centerlines while preserving filament identity through crossings and low-contrast regions. Established pickers such as Topaz and crYOLO construct traces by linking local predictions, which can fragment filaments or connect them incorrectly. Preserving filament identity helps keep segments associated for the analysis of polymorphic specimens. However, expert centerline annotations for training are scarce and costly to obtain. We introduce CryoTracer, a synthetic-to-real framework that predicts individual filaments as ordered centerline polylines. Its transformer decoder jointly refines filament predictions using image features sampled around each curve. A discrete Fr\'echet objective guides instance matching and curve regression while treating both tracing directions as equivalent. Procedural micrographs vary filament geometry, crossings, and image appearance, providing exact centerline labels for pretraining. We then fine-tune the model on annotated real micrographs. We compare CryoTracer with five reference methods on EMPIAR-10022 and a private, in-house $\alpha$-synuclein collection. The standard regime uses the full training partition, while the practical regime limits training to 50 annotated micrographs. A separate $\alpha$-synuclein split tests transfer to held-out sessions. CryoTracer achieves the highest complete-filament mF1, continuous coverage, and crossing accuracy among the evaluated methods in both regimes. We release new filament centerline annotations for EMPIAR-10022. Code and data are available at https://github.com/cryotracer/cryotracer

replace UniField++: A Unified Agentic Framework with Loop Reasoning for MRI Field-Strength Enhancement

Authors: Yiyang Lin, Chenhui Wang, Tianliang Yao, Pengli Zhu, Shuaibo Li, Zijun Sun, Yun Miao, Ang Li, Yiming Deng, Yixuan Yuan

Abstract: Magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) field-strength enhancement seeks to combine the accessibility of low-field scanners with the image quality of high-field scanners. Yet, existing methods remain prone to anatomical hallucinations: scarce paired data limit knowledge of field-strength-dependent tissue contrast and anatomical visibility, while uniform processing leaves difficult brain regions with insufficient reasoning and refinement. To address these challenges by better coupling field-aware learning with region-adaptive reasoning and refinement, we propose UniField++, a two-agent framework for unified multi-field enhancement, including 64mT-to-3T and 3T-to-7T. Unified learning exposes both the vision-language model (VLM)-based planner and the flow-matching-based enhancer to field-dependent image characteristics across tasks. Trained with radiologists' annotations of field-dependent hallucination-prone brain regions and corresponding tissue- and anatomy-specific correction plans, the planner predicts regional risk and formulates targeted restoration strategies. Guided by these plans, the enhancer performs loop reasoning along the flow-matching trajectory, repeatedly processing high-risk brain regions for additional reasoning and refinement. Together, multi-field unification and risk-guided reasoning support anatomically faithful enhancement across field strengths. Across the evaluated tasks, UniField++ improves PSNR by an average of 2.74 dB and SSIM by 14.05 percentage points over comparison methods, with corresponding gains of 3.97 dB and 13.92 percentage points over the ablation baseline. It also maintains enhancement quality on an external cohort with an unseen field strength and improves downstream whole-brain segmentation Macro Dice by 0.1457 on average over original low-field inputs. Code: https://github.com/CUHK-AIM-Group/UniField_plusplus.

replace FlashReFocus: Interactive Image Refocusing in Seconds

Authors: Ching-Heng Cheng, Chia-Ming Lee, Ming-Ching Chang, Xin Li, Chih-Chung Hsu

Abstract: Single-image refocusing changes the focal plane and depth of field of a photograph after capture. Users compare focal choices on one photograph, so content stability across edits matters alongside quality and speed. Recent generative methods rerun large diffusion models for every edit, even when they first restore an all-in-focus image, so content can change inconsistently between edits. Yet only this restoration is highly ill-posed and benefits from a generative prior, whereas rendering blur is largely determined by depth and the focal plane. We therefore propose FlashReFocus, which runs diffusion once per photograph and renders each edit with a lightweight network. A one-step diffusion deblurrer with a decoder refiner recovers fine detail efficiently while balancing fidelity and perceptual quality. The renderer uses an explicit defocus map for control and defocus-bias attention to promote information exchange between tokens with similar blur magnitudes. Because every edit reuses the same reconstruction, in-focus content stays consistent across edits, and editing the map enables tilt-shift and two-plane effects. We further collect 3CReal, a benchmark of 89 pairs from three camera-lens systems, to evaluate generalization to unseen optics. For zero-shot bokeh rendering on EBB! Val176 and 3CReal, FlashReFocus leads the evaluated methods in fidelity and perceptual quality. At 1536x1536, a complete refocus takes 2.45 s and each subsequent edit 0.46 s on one RTX PRO 6000.

replace ShadowCast: On-the-Fly Context-Aware Shadow Augmentation for Remote Sensing Image Segmentation

Authors: Dongho Yoon, Yechan Kim, Jongmin Joo, JongHyun Park, Moongu Jeon

Abstract: Shadows cast by elevated objects alter the spectral appearance and obscure the boundaries of objects in remote sensing images, thereby degrading semantic segmentation performance. Most existing approaches handle shadows as degradations to be detected and removed, requiring additional costs from dedicated networks and shadow-specific annotations. We propose ShadowCast, which takes the opposite approach: it synthesizes shadows and injects them during training for remote sensing segmentation. It extracts masks of elevated objects, such as buildings and trees, directly from the segmentation labels and estimates an image-specific cast-shadow direction cue by probing the brightness around them. It then projects synthetic shadows along the inferred cast-shadow direction, requiring no learned network and no acquisition metadata. Experiments on three widely used remote sensing benchmarks, Vaihingen, Potsdam, and LoveDA, show that ShadowCast consistently outperforms the baseline across diverse state-of-the-art segmentation architectures. Moreover, it brings additional performance gains when combined with standard augmentation and regularization techniques, indicating that stochastic shadow injection serves as a simple and complementary augmentation strategy for remote sensing segmentation.

replace Learning to Generate in the Right Order

Authors: Julius Erbach, Dominik Narnhofer, Bernt Schiele, Jan Eric Lenssen, Konrad Schindler

Abstract: We introduce PONDER, a method that learns in which order an image generator should fill in content and which of its earlier predictions to revise. Masked generative models construct a sample by repeatedly filling in subsets of missing variables. The order of these partial predictions determines the context available in subsequent steps and therefore affects the quality of the final sample. Common strategies are to choose the order randomly or based on local confidence estimates. Conditioned on the partially generated sample and the generator's features, our policy chooses which positions to fill next and/or which previously filled positions to mask for later regeneration, a mechanism we call rethinking. In this way, the generation process is able to adapt to the evolving context and also to revise earlier predictions. We optimize the policy using group-relative policy gradients, with rewards that measure constraint satisfaction, human preference, or distribution quality. For the latter, we introduce FIDgrad, an image-level reward derived from the (first-order) change in Fr\'echet Inception Distance caused by adding a generated sample to the current feature distribution. With the generators frozen, learned ordering reduces MAR-B ImageNet FID from 4.09 to 2.78, improves MaskGen-KL-XL HPSv2 from 24.50 to 26.50, and raises the accuracy on hard visual Sudokus from 4.5% to 56.6%, surpassing uncertainty-based greedy ordering (41.0%). Rethinking further improves these results to 2.70 FID, 27.80 HPSv2 and 96.7% accuracy.

Authors: Mourad Derbedrosian, Michael Hobley, Pietro Perona

Abstract: Imaging sonar enables underwater observation in conditions where optical imaging is ineffective due to darkness and turbid water. However, imaging-sonar systems previously used for ecological monitoring have typically been expensive, limiting their accessibility. Consumer forward-looking sonar has the potential to make fish monitoring more accessible, but its noisy imagery makes annotation and the development of automated methods challenging. We introduce the Huntington and Caltech Koi RGB and Sonar dataset (HaCKRS), which pairs Garmin LiveScope imaging-sonar recordings with top-down RGB video of koi carp. The dataset contains more than 105,000 temporally matched frame pairs, spanning approximately 3.3 hours of recordings across four range and orientation configurations. We provide frame-wise metadata, sonar geometry and coordinate information, synchronization data for all four configurations, and planar registration for the three horizontal configurations. The release focuses on paired observations and calibration rather than task-specific fish annotations. We intend HaCKRS to support annotation and the development of fish detection, tracking, and counting methods, and investigations into the potential of consumer sonar for ecological monitoring. The dataset, code, and OpenScope, the open-source tool we used to record the sonar, are publicly available.

replace PhysSkin++: Generalizable Self-Supervised Neural Skinning for Real-Time Interactive Physics-Based Animation

Authors: Yuanhang Lei, Baofei Liu, Xingxuan Li, Tao Cheng, Zixuan Wang, Boming Zhao, Yujun Shen, Siyuan Huang, Ruizhen Hu, Peter Yichen Chen, Hujun Bao, Zhaopeng Cui

Abstract: Generalizing real-time physics-based animation across diverse 3D shapes and spatial discretizations remains challenging. replace How Do DiTs Denoise? An Aggregator-Predictor View of Attention and MLPs

Authors: Gyeonghun Kang, William Xingxu Chen, Xiang Cheng

Abstract: Local denoising helps explain diffusion generalization. How do diffusion transformers (DiTs) implement it? Our aggregator--predictor framework decomposes population local denoising into context aggregation and patch prediction. Single-layer algebra motivates treating attention and MLP as estimators of these quantities. We test this view on pixel DiTs and pretrained U-ViT, JiT, and latent flow models across datasets and resolutions from $64\times64$ to $256\times256$. We find that (1) attention-selected context transfers across models, and adapting the selected context to each noisy image improves denoising at low noise; (2) DiTs can share a single learned key--value bank across layers for patch prediction while retaining generation quality comparable to DiTs with a separate MLP in each layer; and (3) across related face domains, transplanting jointly adapted QK preserves more source-associated appearance, while transplanting adapted VO+MLP produces more target-associated appearance. Motivated by these findings, we test matched-budget fine-tuning on MetFaces. Across three backbones, adapting VO, MLP, or both with QK frozen yields competitive generation quality, while adapting QK alone performs worse. These results illustrate how DiT components can be reused, replaced, and adapted.

replace GeoMem-VLA: Internalizing 3D Scene Understanding into Temporal Memory for Vision-Language-Action Models

Authors: Qinfan Zhang, Yifan Luo, Haoyuan Shi, Guanlin Liu, Han Dong, Haozhe Shan, Yong Dai, Xiangcong Ren, Xiaozhu Ju

Abstract: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models typically predict actions from current 2D observations, whereas existing 3D policies infer geometry per step without retaining it across an episode. Action generation under occlusion and across multiple stages requires a compact spatial abstraction that persists over time without reconstructing a full 3D scene at each step. Based on this insight, we introduce GeoMem-VLA, a framework that internalizes explicit 3D scene and motion supervision into learnable latent GeoPlantokens, leaving the vision-language backbone's native visual embeddings unaligned. During training, a frozen geometry teacher and demonstration trajectories supervise these tokens via an auxiliary flow-matching head over current scene point maps and future end-effector tracks. Across steps, the policy stores observation and GeoPlan representations in separate memory banks, merges similar adjacent states into event memories, and fuses retrieved history with current features to predict actions. At inference, the 3D teacher and auxiliary head are removed, and action generation relies solely on the latent GeoPlan tokens and episode memory without explicit scene reconstruction. GeoMem-VLA achieves average success rates of 98.9% on LIBERO, 91.10% and 91.24% on RoboTwin 2.0 Clean and Randomized settings, and 78.0% across five real-world manipulation tasks.

replace One Evaluation Is Enough: Inversion-Free Image Editing with Trajectory Correction

Authors: Lifan Jiang, Taiyang Chen, Senlin cheng, Bolei Xu, Xiaobo Li, Can Ye, Dehai Zhao, Deng Cai, Boxi Wu

Abstract: Real-image editing commonly relies on inversion and iterative sampling, while efficient one-step editors often stabilize a large update by estimating and averaging editing fields at multiple noise levels. We ask a stricter question: is a single temporal field evaluation sufficient for reliable inversion-free editing? Our answer rests on a previously underused duality in a variance-preserving (VP) one-step model: one source-target query at a shared noisy state simultaneously determines a local angular semantic tangent and a clean endpoint displacement. We integrate the tangent over the analytically known remaining VP angle to obtain a nominal one-step transport, and use the endpoint displacement to correct its long-horizon extrapolation through a constrained projection. This transport-first, correction-second construction yields Endpoint-Calibrated Transport (ECT), a closed-form, training-free editor requiring only one noise level, two text conditions packed into one denoiser batch, and one latent update. ECT requires no image inversion, trajectory integration, second temporal observation, or iterative optimization, and can be directly applied to pretrained epsilon-prediction VP one-step generators. Across the reported benchmark settings, ECT delivers stable gains in editing quality within an extremely short editing time.

Abstract: Monocular occupancy prediction in indoor scenes aims to recover a scene's 3D geometry and semantics from a single RGB image. Existing methods are restricted to a closed set of predefined categories, limiting their ability to generalize to unseen categories. This limitation is particularly critical in indoor scenes, where objects exhibit highly diverse and fine-grained semantic categories. We study open-vocabulary monocular occupancy prediction for indoor scenes and present OVM-Occ, a Gaussian-based occupancy framework with an instance-aware vision-language teacher. We further introduce geometry-aware decoupled semantic distillation to mitigate the interference between geometry learning and semantic learning. To evaluate open-vocabulary occupancy prediction performance, we introduce OpenOcc-ScanNet and OpenOcc-Replica, two extended benchmarks with more diverse and fine-grained semantic categories. replace LabWorld: Learning Interactive 4D Worlds for Scientific Experimentation

Authors: Suozhi Huang, Kelsey Fu, Binxu Li, Luna Lyu, Lezhi Tan, Kaize Wu, Wenbo Lu, David Smerkous, Jiahan Li, Siqi Zhu, Zhizhou Sha, Jiacheng Guo, Zhixuan Liang, Shilong Liu, Le Cong, Mengdi Wang

Abstract: Autonomous scientific agents must not only reason about experiments but also interact with the physical environments in which they are executed. LabWorld recovers reusable 3D assets, a metric scene, time-varying object states, and procedure events. Multi-frame observations provide complementary evidence for asset and scene reconstruction, while motion reconstruction combines visual and geometric observations with event constraints encoding the spatial and temporal requirements of laboratory interactions. For vortexing, it achieves a mean Tube-Length-Normalized PCK-AUC of 0.638, exceeding the strongest baseline by 0.035. replace RFW-A: Agentic Procedural Generation of 400K Articulated Assets over 500 Categories

Authors: Yuanbo Lyu, Changjian Pan, Saining Zhang, Yi Yang, Peishuo Li, Zhiheng Li, Jialu Liu, Junhao Chen, Jiawei Weng, Yupeng Gao, Weiqing Xiao, Wenyi Li, Jiarui Guo, Rui Zhang, Jingrun Du, Weiyi Lin, Zenan Meng, Hu Wei, Lin Qu, Zongzheng Zhang, Hao Zhao, Chaojian Li

Abstract: High-quality articulated assets are essential for embodied perception, simulation, and manipulation, yet constructing them at scale remains difficult. Manual annotation and procedural modeling require substantial human effort. Recent agentic systems automate asset construction through program synthesis, but repeat costly reasoning and refinement for every new asset. Our key observation is that assets within a category can share construction logic despite variations in structure and appearance. Agentic synthesis and procedural generation are therefore complementary: agents can construct reusable programs, while procedural execution reuses the resulting logic across many samples. We therefore present RFW-A, which combines the two in a new articulated-asset generation paradigm. Starting from existing articulated programs, RFW-A organizes reusable components and their dependencies into category-level generators. Agents test and refine these generators across sampled configurations and motion states. Once constructed and validated, the generators produce diverse assets through procedural execution with no further model calls or token charges. The estimated API cost of generator authoring averages $10.62, amortizing to $0.05 per asset over 200 assets, versus $1.24 for the sampled per-asset agentic synthesis baseline. Using RFW-A, we built RFW-A-400K, containing over 400K sim-ready articulated assets across more than 500 categories. Experiments show high structural and kinematic validity and stable execution across physics simulators. Beyond asset validity, training with RFW-A-400K improves part-level articulation estimation on seen and unseen categories, while policies trained in simulation with retrieved and program-edited assets transfer zero-shot to real articulated objects.

replace Parse Before You Execute: Efficient Tool-Integrated Spatial Reasoning Agents with Executable Primitives

Authors: Xiwei Liu, Yulong Li, Xinling Zhuang, Xuhui Li, Yichen Li, Haolin Yang, Zhixiang Lu, Yutong Xie

Abstract: Spatial reasoning in Vision-Language Models (VLMs) has increasingly benefited from tool-integrated pipelines that ground computation in explicit geometry. However, existing systems often compile queries into relatively open-ended artifacts, such as code, task constraints, tool plans, or gathered evidence, leaving what must be resolved before execution under-specified. This can lead to unnecessary tool acquisition and iterative refinement, while still producing plausible reasoning traces from incomplete spatial commitments. We identify this failure mode as under-committed query compilation. To address it, we propose PEPS, a training-free agent Parsing Executable Primitives for Spatial reasoning under a typed FESM schema over Frame, Entity, State, and Metric. PEPS follows a parse-before-execute principle: the Parser commits each query to a compact set of executable requirements; the Executor selectively grounds the required primitives through fixed geometric APIs and deterministic computation; and the Verifier checks whether these requirements are fulfilled, enabling early termination or targeted repair. Experiments on three spatial reasoning benchmarks and four VLM backbones show that PEPS improves reasoning accuracy while substantially reducing refinement rounds, API cost, and end-to-end latency over strong tool-integrated baselines. These results demonstrate that knowing what a query requires before execution makes tool-integrated spatial reasoning not only more reliable, but also more efficient.

replace Intervenable Concept Dynamics for Video Activity Forecasting

Authors: Patrick Knab, David Steinmann, Giovanni De Felice, Felix Divo, Udo Schlegel, Wolfgang Stammer, Sascha Marton, Christian Bartelt

Abstract: Concept bottleneck models route predictions through human-understandable concepts, making their intermediate states inspectable and editable. Extending this model family to forecasting requires modeling how concept activations evolve over time as objects, actions, and their interactions change, yet existing CBMs ground concepts only in observed inputs and do not forecast their future evolution. We study this problem using videos of ongoing activities and forecast future activities through a named-concept state. We propose TRACE-CBM (Temporal Relational Activity Concept Evolution), which first refines observed concept activations with sparse spatio-temporal graphs. It then recursively predicts future concept states, conditioning each transition on the prior activity distribution, and decodes both observed and predicted states with a shared activity classifier. At inference time, the model supports editing concept values, activity activations, and learned edges without parameter updates, exposing the relational spatio-temporal dependencies used for forecasting. Across multiple datasets, TRACE improves observed-activity accuracy over concept-based methods and stays close to matched black-box forecasters in forecast accuracy. Intervention experiments show that concept edits correct part of the initially incorrect forecasts without flipping initially correct ones, and that activity-belief edits can steer forecasts toward a requested activity. Code: https://github.com/patrick-knab/TRACE-CBM

URLs: https://github.com/patrick-knab/TRACE-CBM

replace Demystifying Prompt Stealing Attacks against Synthetic Video

Authors: Junyan Huang, Sanskar Agmain, Chen Gong, Yan Pang, FNU Suya

Abstract: Creators publicly showcase generated content to advertise and sell their work, but these examples may also enable unauthorized reproduction and customization. We study whether an adversary can derive reusable inputs from a single public video for faithful reconstruction and controlled editing, without access to the original prompt or generator. We adapt captioning and prompt optimization approaches to video prompt stealing attacks (PSAs) and find that prompt recovery alone provides limited reconstruction fidelity. Even the original prompt produces different spatial and temporal realizations when the initial noise changes. Using an independent generator enlarges the reconstruction gap, as reusing the original prompt and seed can still produce substantially different videos. We propose V-PSA, which uses a recovered caption and DDIM inversion to construct a reference trajectory under an independent generator. Null-Text Inversion then optimizes per-step unconditional embeddings to reduce deviations from this trajectory during reconstruction. The resulting inputs also guide generation when the caption is modified, supporting edits that change selected content while preserving the target's layout and motion. We construct a benchmark containing 12,000 video-prompt pairs from three victim generators and four prompt sources. V-PSA achieves video-to-video similarity around 0.9 between target and reconstructed videos, substantially improving over prompt-only baselines with similarity score of 0.6, and supports multiple controlled variations that preserve the target's structure. Additional experiments show that augmenting prior image PSAs with inversion-derived inputs also improves reconstruction. Our results show that evaluations restricted to prompt recovery can underestimate an attacker's ability to reconstruct public generative outputs.

replace Context-Shielded Backdoor Attack against Large Vision-Language Models

Authors: Zhifang Zhang, Qiang Huang, Chenpeng Wang, Weitong Chen, Olaf Maennel, Wei Emma Zhang, Lei Feng, Lei Zhang, Miao Xu

Abstract: Large vision-language models (LVLMs) can be efficiently customized by fine-tuning only a lightweight connector, but this fine-tuning stage also exposes them to backdoor attacks. To counteract this threat, existing defenses inspect the behavior or the attention of the released model. However, they mostly inspect the model with all visual tokens, implicitly assuming that the deployed model sees the same tokens, which rarely holds since visual-token compression is widely adopted to reduce inference cost. In this paper, we find that compression can hide a dormant backdoor attack from inspection and awaken it during deployment, as the redundant tokens regulate the strength of the trigger. Specifically, without compression, the trigger is diluted by the redundant tokens around it, whereas compression keeps the trigger for its high importance and discards its context, a phenomenon we term context stripping. Inspired by this finding, we propose StripWake, a compressor-agnostic backdoor attack whose trigger takes effect only under compression. StripWake (i) poisons each training image in four views (clean or triggered, with all or compressed tokens) and asks for the target only in the compressed triggered view, and (ii) aligns the attention on clean compressed views with a frozen benign teacher to preserve clean utility. Extensive experiments show that StripWake stays dormant with all visual tokens and reliably wakes up under compression while preserving clean utility, is far more robust to existing defenses than published backdoor attacks, and generalizes to different architectures, model sizes and compressors unseen in training.

replace dKV Forcing: Separating Generation and Memory Representations through a Differentiable KV Cache

Authors: Zhiqi Li, Bo Zhu

Abstract: A causal video generator writes every segment it produces into a key--value (KV) cache, and later segments know the past only through it. Self-rollout training stops the gradient at this cache, and Self Gradient Forcing (SGF) restores it, but applies it to the same weights that generate the current segment. We find that writing and generating are different roles: in a pretrained generator the written KV already differs from the KV used for denoising, and on shared weights the gradient from future reads is negatively aligned with the gradient of generation. We propose dKV Forcing (dKV denotes differentiable KV), which gives the write its own parameters, a low-rank residual used only when a segment enters the cache, and trains them with the gradient of the later segments that read what they wrote. Opening this gradient through the cache in common use exhausts GPU memory. We trace this to checkpoint replay re-writing the cache, not to the gradient path, which removes the need for the second reconstruction pass of SGF. dKV Forcing keeps the cache as persistent differentiable pages and computes the exact gradient in one temporal traversal, with an $11\%$ lower memory peak and $28\%$ less resident memory. With pages that store hidden states, it trains 15\,s clips on which the two-pass reconstruction runs out of memory. Trained on 5\,s clips, dKV Forcing reaches the highest VBench-Long quality score at 60\,s and 240\,s and outperforms SGF by $3.6$ and $6.1$ points of aesthetic quality, with a higher dynamic degree.

replace From Reflection to Co-Evolution: Self-Evolving Agents with Locally Verified Memory

Authors: Xinxiang Yin, Xinglang Zhang, Haodong Chen, Zhiqing Cui, Weidong Tang, Yuxuan Wang, Junrui Wang, Jingyi Zhang, Jianfeng Zhang, Jian Qian

Abstract: LLM agents have made impressive progress in coding and mathematical reasoning, motivating growing interest in learning from long-horizon interaction. Memory supports this process, but plausible lessons can be redundant or reinforce inappropriate actions. Can a memory's source decision provide a practical test for whether to retain it? We localize consequential trajectory steps, extract candidate memories, and replay each source decision with and without its candidate under the same context. This reveals two properties: most candidates barely shift the probed distribution, and both successful and failed source actions can be reinforced or suppressed. These observations motivate Anchor, which tests shift magnitude and source-role alignment before retention. Admitted memories guide later trajectories, enabling experience-driven improvement under a frozen policy. On held-out ALFWorld and WebShop tasks, Anchor improves success rate and mean score by up to 19.9% and 17.2%, respectively, relative to the strongest baseline for each backbone. With Qwen3.5-27B, it retains 41% fewer ALFWorld cards than unfiltered retention and outperforms all four WebShop cost-comparison baselines while using fewer tokens.

replace RoadState: A Domain-Prior-Enhanced Vision-Language System for Industrial-Grade Lane-Level Map Updating

Authors: Deguo Xia, Zihan Li, Jiaqi Wang, Dong Xie, Xiyan Liu, Chen Zhu, Liwei Qian, Xin Pei, Shuanglong Li, Hanmeng Liu, Jizhou Huang, Mengmeng Yang, Diange Yang

Abstract: Lane-level maps provide foundational structured road information for navigation, intelligent driving, and intelligent transportation systems. Understanding road-scene semantics from structural and contextual cues is essential for reliable lane-level map updating, yet remains challenging in production due to occlusions, ambiguous lane markings, temporary obstacles, and viewpoint changes, while downstream workflows require temporally consistent, physically valid, and auditable outputs rather than free-form descriptions. We present RoadState, a production-deployed vision-language system that transforms sequential road-scene observations into structured lane-level semantics for Baidu Maps' map-updating pipeline. Its core model, RoadState-VLM, formulates road-scene understanding as autoregressive state estimation, predicting the current structured road state from the previous observation, previous symbolic state, and current observation. To improve semantic consistency and reasoning fidelity across interdependent road-state dimensions, we introduce Geometric Chain-of-Thought (Geo-CoT), which encodes a directed acyclic graph of semantic dependencies as a domain prior. On a real-world dataset, RoadState-VLM achieves 94.33% mean accuracy across six road-state dimensions, outperforming the strongest fine-tuned VLM baseline, with ablations confirming the complementary gains of Geo-CoT and temporal transition context. In production, a cascaded routing pipeline directs routine cases to lightweight models and reserves complex cases for RoadState-VLM. Deployed since March 2025, RoadState supports lane-level map updating across 360+ cities, reduces overall production costs by 30%, and maintains robust performance under stress testing with human-in-the-loop auditing.

replace Rethinking Geometric Depth in Monocular 3D Object Detection: A Projection-Consistent Reformulation

Authors: Zhihao Zhang, Abhinav Kumar, Huaizhi Qu, Tianlong Chen, Xiaoming Liu

Abstract: Monocular 3D detection (Mono3D) hinges on recovering depth from a single image, for which the geometric formulation $z \approx f \cdot H / h_{2D}$ is widely adopted due to its simplicity and direct grounding in projective geometry. We show that this formulation harbors a systematic, projection-induced bias: substituting the 2D bounding-box height $h_{2D}$ recovers the depth of an object's visible front surface rather than its center, causing size-dependent underestimation that persists even under ground-truth inputs. We address this front-surface bias with MonoPCA, a unified framework built on a projection-consistent reformulation. At its core, PCDepth retains the measurable $h_{2D}$ and closes the gap to center depth through an orientation- and size-aware correction derived from perspective geometry, eliminating the bias without introducing ill-posed regression targets. Because PCDepth couples depth to predicted object size and orientation, attribute perception becomes essential. We therefore introduce GeoAlign, a teacher-student framework that transfers geometric representations from Vision Foundation Models into a lightweight student, strengthening attribute perception at no inference cost. Across four challenging benchmarks, MonoPCA achieves SoTA performance, including a +1.44% AP$_{3D}$ gain on the KITTI leaderboard and +2.80% on Omni3D$_{OUT}$. Code is available at https://github.com/alanzhangcs/MonoPCA.

URLs: https://github.com/alanzhangcs/MonoPCA.

replace RS-JEPA: Remote Sensing Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture with Scan-Path Views for Cross-Sensor Image Retrieval

Authors: Tanishq Rachamalla, Srishti Kaushik, Muhammad Attique Khan, Swalpa Kumar Roy

Abstract: Cross-sensor remote sensing image retrieval aims to find scenes with the same content across heterogeneous sensors, such as SAR and optical or panchromatic (PAN) and multispectral (MS) imagery, without manual labels. Existing self-supervised methods either reconstruct sensor-specific pixel detail, align each image only with its co-registered twin, or rely on large transformers with 118-173M parameters (and up to 398M). We introduce RS-JEPA, a compact joint-embedding predictive framework built on a Vision Mamba encoder shared by both sensors. Since a state-space encoder reads an image as an ordered sequence of patches, we propose scan-path views, which treat two reading orders of the same image as two views with identical pixels and predict the hidden patches in one order from the visible patches in the other.To keep this prediction challenging, we propose Adversarial View Coupling (AVC), which learns the hardest split of the visible patches, and a neighbourhood consensus loss that aligns the scene similarities of both sensors. Extensive experiments on SAR-optical (BEN-14K, CBRSIR-VS) and PAN-MS (DSRSID) benchmarks demonstrate that RS-JEPA achieves the highest F1@5 among twelve re-implemented methods (72.92, 80.35 and 94.43) with a retrieval network of only 3.88M parameters (14.57M in training, 8-16times fewer than prior JEPA retrievers). Like prior methods, RS-JEPA trains on co-registered pairs. Our results highlight reading order as a source of self-supervised views. code: https://github.com/Tanishq251/RSJEPA.

URLs: https://github.com/Tanishq251/RSJEPA.

replace eDMD: Endpoint Distribution Matching for Few-Step Diffusion Distillation

Authors: Tong Zhao, Xianfang Zeng, Yucheng Han, Ruoyu Wang, Mingkun Lei, Gang Yu, Chi Zhang

Abstract: Distribution matching distillation (DMD) has emerged as a leading paradigm for distilling diffusion models into few-step students, where an auxiliary fake score model tracks the student's distribution and the discrepancy between the teacher and fake predictions drives the student toward the teacher. In multi-step distillation, although only the student's final output is delivered, existing methods train the fake model on intermediate states or across all denoising steps. Consequently, the student's update signal becomes entangled with intermediate distributions, which can distort the matching direction and remain nonzero even after the student's final outputs already match the teacher distribution. To resolve this mismatch, we propose Endpoint Distribution Matching Distillation (eDMD), which trains a single shared fake model exclusively on the student's final outputs and uses it to correct every denoising step. Our theoretical analysis proves that this endpoint reference guarantees that the distribution matching term vanishes if and only if the student's final output distribution matches the teacher's, regardless of intermediate distributions. Beyond the fake reference, we further strengthen teacher supervision through a precise teacher (PT) that replaces single-step guided predictions with short multi-step trajectories on the final noise interval, paired with a calibrated query density for intermediate steps. Experiments on SD3-Medium, FLUX.2-klein (4B and 9B), and Qwen-Image-2.1 demonstrate that eDMD consistently outperforms existing distribution matching methods for 4-step generation.

replace Editable Video Context: Mid-Context KV-Cache Reuse for Context-Managing Video LLMs

Authors: Dayu Wang, Shasha Xu

Abstract: Context Language Models let a model edit its own context, while Suffix Cache Reuse makes edits to text efficient by adjusting the positions of cached keys for surviving tokens. We extend these ideas to video language models with multimodal rotary position embeddings (M-RoPE). We represent a long video as timestamped segments. After viewing a sparsely sampled version, the model selects segments for decoding at 4x the frame rate, and the remaining segments are removed from its context. Our rebuild method reuses the KV cache of surviving segments by rotating their keys with a single offset shared by the temporal, height, and width components of Qwen2.5-VL's M-RoPE. A content-hashed cache also avoids repeating vision-encoder computation for those segments. In preliminary experiments with Qwen2.5-VL-7B on synthetic needle-in-a-video-haystack questions over public-domain films, the method reduces prefill tokens and end-to-end rebuild latency relative to full recomputation while usually producing the same answer. Densifying the segment containing the answer improves accuracy when that segment is known. When the model selects segments itself, accuracy does not improve: it rarely identifies a needle that was absent from the sparse sample. We release the code, raw logs, and scripts used to reproduce the paper's results.

replace SplatFlow3D: Learning Scene Flow via 3D Motion Feature Splatting

Authors: Bo Wang, Zhenping Sun, Yang Yu, Jian Li, Li Liu, Dewen Hu

Abstract: Considering the motion continuity and stability of 3D objects over short inter-frame intervals, introducing multi-frame settings in scene flow estimation can potentially address occlusion. In this paper, we propose a two-frame backbone, SimpleFlow3D, which integrates dense information from the image plane and sparse information from the depth dimension in a decoupling-followed-by-coupling manner by hierarchically constructing the 3D motion feature. SimpleFlow3D achieves SOTA results with minimal parameters. We also propose a weight-sharing multi-frame framework to splat the previous 3D motion feature to the current estimation process. The framework unifies the first and non-first frame inference by constructing pseudo inputs. After combining the proposed framework and backbone, the newly born multi-frame method SplatFlow3D further advances the state of the art. It achieves lower errors than the two-frame backbone, with fewer parameters than current methods. On both KITTI and Spring, SplatFlow3D ranked 1st over all published and submitted methods. Experiments show that SplatFlow3D improves occlusion handling, non-rigid motion estimation, and generalization to real-world scenes. Code is available at https://github.com/wwsource/SplatFlow3D.

replace As Student Rollouts Continue, Visual Backdoors Keep Growing

Authors: Zane Fu, Yanzhao Zheng, Jinze Liu, Ziting Qin, Qianshan Wei, Jihuai Zhu, Yipeng Yu, Baohua Dong, Hangcheng Zhu, Ruohui Huang

Abstract: Conventional knowledge distillation can facilitate the propagation of backdoors from teacher models to student models. However, how this risk manifests in On-Policy Distillation (OPD) remains insufficiently studied. OPD has recently emerged as an important post-training paradigm for Vision-Language Models (VLMs). Unlike traditional offline distillation, OPD does not require teacher-generated responses. Instead, it uses the teacher's probability distributions to supervise response trajectories generated online by the student. Since the initial student model typically generates benign responses and receives no explicit supervision from malicious target responses, OPD appears to offer some inherent resistance to backdoor propagation. In this paper, we challenge this intuition and reveal a more subtle risk of visual backdoor propagation in OPD. Through experiments across multiple model families and datasets, together with theoretical analysis, we identify three main findings. First, unlike the relatively gradual propagation observed in traditional offline distillation, backdoors rarely emerge in OPD at low poisoning ratios. However, the attack success rate rapidly approaches 100% once a critical threshold is exceeded. Second, backdoor propagation does not depend on explicit malicious responses. Instead, it is driven by the continuous accumulation of anomalous probability mass assigned by the teacher. Finally, based on these findings, we propose a novel trigger design that enables visual backdoor propagation with zero explicit trigger injection. These findings further reveal an overlooked mechanism of backdoor propagation in OPD and highlight its potential security risks.

replace Unified Residual Translation for Paired Image Restoration

Authors: Joyanta Jyoti Mondal, Mridul Banik, Ibne Farabi Shihab, Md. Shifatul Ahsan Apurba

Abstract: Paired image restoration spans changes in imaging contrast, sensor modality, and spatial resolution. These settings differ in their input channels and target grids, raising the question of how much of the prediction pipeline must be specialized to each task. We present the Unified Residual Translator (URT), which separates fixed channel and scale mapping from learned residual prediction. A deterministic reference uses channel averaging, replication, or bicubic interpolation to match the target dimensions, while a NAFNet encoder-decoder predicts an additive correction from the complete input. Separately trained instances share the prediction rule, $\ell_1$ objective, and optimizer across MRI contrast synthesis, visible-to-infrared translation, SAR-to-optical translation, and real-camera super-resolution. Across six task and scale settings, URT achieves the highest PSNR and SSIM among the evaluated baselines on IXI, SEN1-2, and RealSR $\times3$ and $\times4$. It reaches 30.774dB on IXI and 13.478dB on SEN1-2, with luminance PSNR of 33.596, 28.742, and 27.342dB at the three RealSR scales. On LLVIP, it ranks second in PSNR and SSIM. A study of four spectral alternatives identifies complementary benefits of fitted references and bandwise residual gains. Together, these results support explicit dimensional alignment and learned residual correction as a common formulation for heterogeneous paired imaging tasks.

replace LoopZip: Visual Token Compression for Efficient Looped Vision-Language Models

Authors: Weizhong Huang, Zhicheng Liu, Yan Zhang, Fei Chao, Xiawu Zheng, Liujuan Cao

Abstract: Looped vision-language models (VLMs) deepen computation by reusing shared layers, approaching larger models' capabilities with fewer parameters and easing the storage and deployment burden of stacking independent layers. However, repeatedly processing long visual sequences still incurs substantial compute and GPU memory costs. Visual token compression reduces these costs by shortening sequences. Existing methods mainly select original tokens directly or combine selection with aggregation. In looped models, the former may overlook changing token importance across calls and discard information needed later; the latter lack joint consideration of output changes across calls, limiting information preservation. We propose LoopZip, a training-free visual token compression algorithm addressing both issues. Visual aggregation builds a compact candidate pool before the looped backbone, and cross-invocation selection averages instruction attention from the first two existing calls of the same shared attention layer to retain tokens needed by both states. Weight-tied value correction then jointly fits shared visual-value adjustments to both pre-selection layer outputs, reducing output deviations across states. To validate LoopZip's effectiveness, we conduct extensive experiments covering two open-source looped VLMs, eleven benchmarks, and three pruning ratios. On LoopVL-1B, LoopZip retains 97.16% of the original model's performance at 50% pruning ratio and exceeds the strongest baseline by 5.36% at 87.5% pruning ratio. Across Hugging Face Transformers, vLLM, and SGLang, LoopVL-1B with LoopZip reduces peak allocated GPU memory by 58.36%-58.98% and achieves 2.01-2.47x end-to-end speedups over the original model.

replace Multimodal Masked Diffusion Autoencoder for Modeling Impaired Driving

Authors: John Gideon, Anshul Gupta, Megan Applegate-Kenton, Kayli Battel, Todd J Rowell, Patricio Reyes Gomez, Laporsha Dees, Emily Sumner, Avinash Balachandran, Guy Rosman

Abstract: Human cognitive state (e.g., distraction, stress), gaze, and behavior interact with the driving environment in subtle ways that AI could use to assist drivers, but ground-truth labels for human cognitive state levels are costly to obtain at scale. We propose a multimodal masked-diffusion autoencoder, ID-MAE, that adds a self-supervised objective to impairment prediction: alongside predicting the impairment label as a masked token, it reconstructs masked gaze and trajectory. To probe the effects of different modalities and types of impairments on model inference, we introduce DIVERT, a driving-simulator dataset containing 103 hours of driving data (n = 109) with ten impairment inductions spanning cognitive, visual, and emotional states, recorded with gaze, vehicle state, and scene video. We demonstrate how ID-MAE affords significant improvements in impairment inference results on both the DIVERT dataset and existing datasets, and enables strong results in additional tasks, such as trajectory and gaze prediction. For location-matched pairs, our model achieves a paired AUC of 0.706 for impairment detection on DIVERT, compared to 0.590 without reconstruction loss, improving over existing baselines. We probe the design space in terms of input modalities, masking scheme, positional encoding, attention mechanism, and decoding. We also investigate the interaction between various impairments in training and inference, demonstrating that visually demanding distractions are separable from cognitive distractions. We plan to publicly release the code and data.

replace NIRJALA: Draining the Sea out of Gaussian Splatting with Three-Path Optics and Reversible Physics

Authors: Latchan Chhetri, Aman Kumar

Abstract: Water does not just tint an underwater photograph; it rewrites it. Each pixel mixes attenuated scene radiance, backscattered light, and scene light scattered forward on its way to the camera, and many combinations of scene and water explain the same pixels. Most Gaussian-splatting methods for underwater scenes model only the first two effects and remove the water by dividing by a transmission that decays exponentially with range, so their inversion gain grows without bound. We present NIRJALA (Sanskrit for "without water"), a 3D Gaussian Splatting framework that recovers water-free appearance and geometry together. TRIVENI, a three-path image formation model, adds a range-saturating forward-scattering term to direct transmission and backscatter; we prove that this keeps the inversion gain bounded at every range and bound how errors in the estimated backscatter and attenuation propagate into the recovered colours. PRATIBIMBA, a reversible physics anchor, ties a flexible neural water model to a ten-parameter physical model in both directions, so scene content cannot hide inside the water. SAMARASA collects the physics-informed objectives, including a range-binned colour prior. To score water removal against ground truth, we built SAJALA ("with water"), a 30-scene suite path-traced in Blender Cycles with water-free images, depth, and normals under controlled water colour and turbidity. On SAJALA, NIRJALA raises PSNR from 24.34 to 24.76 dB and lowers depth AbsRel from 0.109 to 0.091 against the strongest baseline; on real SeaThru-NeRF scenes it lowers LPIPS from 0.194 to 0.175 while rendering at 44 FPS. We also discuss where appearance metrics and geometry disagree underwater, and the limits of our evaluation.

replace DOCK: Dual-Stream-Inspired Self-Supervised Object-Centric Representations Grounded in Robot Kinematics

Authors: Pengju Liu, Caiwen Ding

Abstract: Latent-space planners select actions by comparing the embedding of a predicted future image with that of a goal image. This assumes that distance in the embedding reflects how far the scene is from the goal. We profile this assumption with simulator-executed futures, which removes prediction error. For six frozen DINO and V-JEPA encoders, feature distance barely tracks the object's distance to its goal: the rank correlation is at most 0.11, against 0.87 for object-mask centroids. It is dominated by background and robot patches, and frozen-feature readouts capture the arm far better than the object. Inspired by the ventral (what) and dorsal (where/how) visual streams, we propose DOCK, which learns an object representation whose changes agree with measured physical motion. A grasped object moves with the gripper, so forward kinematics on the joint angles gives a proxy for the object's displacement without annotation. From 9,951 held-object transport trajectories in 21 simulated kitchen scenes, we mine 404K displacement pairs and train three components: an object token pooled from patch features (what), a spatial ruler that makes differences of readout coordinates equal to measured displacements (where), and a dynamics predictor over object tokens (how). Used as a planning cost, the learned representation places the object in 15 of 21 tasks, as many as an oracle that reads the true object position, while mask geometry reaches 10 and frozen feature distance 9. It still solves the most tasks when the unseen robot arm is visible, and its ranking transfers to held-out layouts with unseen object instances and categories without any adaptation.

replace ReCoCo: From Patches to Compositions for Visual Representation Learning

Authors: Juan Guti\'errez, Saman Zadtootaghaj, Jos\'e Luis Blanco-Murillo, Sara Atito

Abstract: Self-supervised learning has produced powerful visual representations, yet learning strong dense features remains challenging. While consistency between augmented views provides strong image-level supervision, extending this principle to local visual content is less straightforward. Existing approaches often match individual patches across views or identify coherent regions, introducing uncertainties in correspondence estimation and assumptions about how visual content should be grouped. We introduce ReCoCo (Regional Composition Consistency), a principled approach that extends cross-view consistency to the regional level without requiring such correspondences or semantic grouping. ReCoCo represents the content shared between augmented views through compositions of their patch-level predictions over learned visual prototypes. By aligning these compositions, the model learns from regions containing multiple visual concepts without requiring them to be separated beforehand. ReCoCo integrates naturally with teacher-student self-distillation and introduces no additional learned parameters. Extensive experiments demonstrate substantial improvements in frozen-feature segmentation, clustering, and regional retrieval while maintaining competitive image-level recognition, alleviating the trade-off between dense and global representation quality.

replace LoopedWAM: Looped World Action Models for Elastic Inference

Authors: Anh Dao, Xinxin Liu, Hang Yu, Jeffrey A Chan Santiago, Yuzhang Shang

Abstract: World action models (WAMs) couple generative video modeling with robotic action prediction but typically require large video and action transformers, limiting deployment efficiency. We present LoopedWAM, a compact WAM that recurrently applies a shared six-block core in each expert, executing 30 transformer layers while storing only 12 blocks per expert. To account for their asymmetric inference costs, the video and action experts can operate at different depths: visual features are computed once per query, whereas action computation repeats at every sampling step. We further propose elastic self-distillation, which trains a single elastic checkpoint, LoopedWAM-E, across action-depth and sampling-step budgets. With only 0.58B policy parameters and no robot pretraining, LoopedWAM achieves 96.1% success on LIBERO, 91.9% on RoboTwin 2.0, and 52.2% on RoboCasaGR1, while using $7.4\times$ less peak GPU memory than Fast-WAM under matched inference measurements. On LIBERO, LoopedWAM-E achieves 96.0% success in 27.6 ms or 95.2% in 24.7 ms on an H100, providing multiple accuracy-latency operating points from a single checkpoint; with two sampling steps and four action loops, it stays within 0.5 points of LoopedWAM on all three benchmarks. Ablations demonstrate that weight-shared depth preserves competitive performance, while aggressive action-depth reduction requires explicit budget-aware training. On a real AgileX PiPER arm, LoopedWAM succeeds in 71.3% of 80 trials across four tasks.

replace CryoGaussian: Heterogeneous Cryo-EM Reconstruction Made Explicit with Gaussian Splatting

Authors: Ziwen Wang, Jiajun Fan, Chaoran Cheng, Ruihan Guo, Yanru Qu, Juexiao Zhou, Ge Liu

Abstract: Single-particle cryo-electron microscopy (cryo-EM) determines macromolecular structures from noisy particle images. However, rare, transient compositional states and subtle, continuous conformational variation are difficult to resolve. We present CryoGaussian, a Gaussian splatting method for heterogeneous cryo-EM reconstruction that fits an explicit Gaussian density representation directly to the particle images through a differentiable rasterizer rendering orthographic line integrals modulated by the contrast transfer function. The Gaussians are initialized randomly, with their number derived from the estimated spatial support of the molecular density and their minimum size bounded by the Nyquist frequency of the particle images. To capture the compositional and conformational variation across particle images, we learn particle-image and per-Gaussian latents that jointly modulate the base position, amplitude, and scale of each Gaussian. Each structural state is then reconstructed by fitting the Gaussians conditioned on its inferred latent. On CryoBench, CryoGaussian achieves state-of-the-art reconstruction accuracy for both compositional and conformational heterogeneity. On experimental data, it resolves a rare compositional state of the 50S ribosomal subunit (class C4, 1.3% of particles) with rRNA helix 68 docked in the absence of the central protuberance, and continuous conformational motion of the SF3b and helicase subcomplexes of the pre-catalytic spliceosome. We further optimize the rasterizer for additive cryo-EM projection, reducing training time by 12.8-28.0$\times$. Reconstructed density maps are available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/ZiwenWang28/CryoGaussian.

replace MixReact: Physics-Based Reactive Humanoid Control from Multimodal Cues via a Discrete-Continuous Mixture of Transformers

Authors: Bin Li, Xing Zhu, Jingya Wang

Abstract: Humanoid agents in everyday environments are expected to react to diverse cues as humans do, with semantically appropriate and physically executable behaviors. Existing cue-driven reaction generation methods support only a narrow range of cue modalities and are limited to purely kinematic generation, leaving the resulting reactions susceptible to physical artifacts. To this end, we present MixReact, the first unified framework for physics-based reactive humanoid control from text, audio, and video cues, integrating a Discrete--Continuous Mixture of Transformers (DisCo-MoT) for state prediction with a History-aware Action Transformer (HAT) for physical execution. DisCo-MoT couples a discrete masked-modeling branch with a continuous autoregressive diffusion branch via one-way mixture attention within a MoT architecture. Guidance from discrete context mitigates collapse into averaged behaviors in one-to-many reaction generation, while direct prediction in continuous state space bypasses the decoding bottleneck of discrete representations. HAT then maps the predicted next state and observed execution history to executable actions, enabling robust closed-loop control in physics-based simulation. Experiments across all three modalities demonstrate consistent improvements over the baselines in fidelity, diversity, and physical consistency, while ablations validate the complementary contributions of the components. Our code will be released to facilitate future research. Project page: https://binlee26.github.io/MixReact_Page/
